             2021 Edition


           Produced by the Department in charge of Agricultural Surveys and Statistics
                                     PREFACE
In order to better understand the use of agricultural inputs, the Minister of Agriculture and Rural
Development created a working group to set up an agricultural inputs observatory in Cameroon.
Coordinated by the Department in charge of Agricultural Surveys and Statistics (DESA), the working
group aims at contributing to the development of agriculture through the gradual establishment of an
information desk for agricultural inputs in Cameroon. Specific objectives include (i) producing reliable
data on the availability, accessibility and use of agricultural inputs in order to periodically monitor
market conditions and identify potential impediments and necessary policy changes; and (ii) allowing
users to access statistical, regulatory and documentary information on agricultural inputs on a single
platform.
The Observatory’s first task was to provide the stakeholders in the sub-sector, with the legislative
and regulatory instruments governing their activities, so that no one would be ignorant of the law.
This collection of legislative and regulatory instruments on agricultural inputs results from the
compilation and formatting of instruments in force as of 31 December 2021. It is divided into 4 parts,
namely:
    - Legal instruments on phytosanitary products, fertilisers, seeds and seedlings;
    - Legal instruments on seeds and seedlings;
    - Legal instruments on the fertiliser sub-sector;
    - Legal instruments on phytosanitary products.
In each part, the instruments are classified according to the hierarchy of legal norms: international
conventions, laws, decrees, orders, decisions and circulars. In each of these categories, the
instruments are classified in chronological order.
These instruments will enable actual and potential stakeholders in the field of agricultural inputs
(farmers, importers, researchers, distributors, users and controllers) to better master the domain.
They are guidance tools to enable an optimal use of inputs in agricultural production and ensure food
security.
This collection is the outcome of a collaboration between the Department in charge of Agricultural
Surveys and Statistics (DESA), the Department in charge of Regulation and Quality Control of
Agricultural Inputs and Products (DRCQ), as well as other partners.
We would like to take this opportunity to thank them.




Page | 1
                PRESENTATION OF THE
                    COLLECTION
This collection provides the various stakeholders involved in agricultural input, with the legal
instruments governing their activities, while protecting consumers, users, as well as the environment.
There are three types of agricultural inputs: seeds and seedlings, fertilisers and phytosanitary
products.
This collection comprises four parts as follows:
    1. Common legal framework for phytosanitary products, fertilisers, seeds and seedlings;
    2. Legal framework for seeds and seedlings;
    3. Legal framework for fertilisers;
    4. Legal framework for phytosanitary products.
In each part, the hierarchy of legal instruments is observed as follows: International Conventions,
Laws, Decrees, Orders, Decisions, Letters and Circulars.
Lengthy instruments such as the Stockholm Convention on Persistent Organic Pollutants (POPs)
and Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development have been inserted as hyperlinks.

1.2. LAW




           Page | 6
   1.2.1.      Law No. 2018/20 of 11 December 2018 Framework Law on Food Safety

The Parliament deliberated and adopted,
the President of the Republic hereby enacts the law set out below:
       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
1- Purpose and Scope of Application
Section 1: This law lays down the principles and bases for regulating food products, feed for animals
intended for human consumption and food additives and supplements, in order to ensure a high level
of consumers life and health protection and environmental soundness.
Section 2: The provisions of this law shall apply to the production, manufacturing, preparation,
handling, parceling, storage, transportation, packaging, preservation, import, export, distribution and
sale of food products or any other related activity.
Section 3: This law seeks
   -   ensure consumer food safety;
   -   prevent and control food-borne diseases;
   -   make the declaration of food poisoning mandatory;
   -   promote national and international food trade by establishing an effective safety system
       based on scientific principles;
   -   contribute to establishing specific standards for food products consumed and marketed in
       the c country, and ensure their application in control and monitoring plans;
   -   improve the quality of food produced domestically by implementing good production,
       manufacturing and hygiene practices, and a sanitary and phytosanitary risk control system;
   -   facilitate the integrated management of food safety at the various stages of the food chain;
   -   promote mechanisms for coordination between the various relevant food sector authorities
       and gradually prepare then to incorporate their activities into regional or international
       organizations, notably Codex Alimentarius (CODEX), the World Organization for Animal
       Health (OIE), the International Plant Protection Convention (IPPC), the International Food
       Safety Authorities Network (INFOSAN).
   -   develop an efficient official inspection and control system based on standards, technical
       regulation and, as appropriate, scientific data;
   -   support and stimulate the development of the food industry and encourage
       competitiveness on the domestic and international market;
   -   promote the participation of consumers and various stakeholders in the food chain with
       respect to the consumption, processing and marketing of food products, as well as in the
       application and conduct of the national food safety policy:
   -   establish a framework to facilitate the implementation of national and international food
       requirements;
   -   strengthen the responsibility of any producer, processor or distributor in the protection
   -   of consumers health by setting up self-checking systems and release for consumption
       authorizations;
   -   ensure communication on risks, in conjunction with epidemiological surveillance networks
       and early warning or health surveillance systems;
   -   set up a national system of authorizations for the consumption of foodstuff, animal feed,
       food additives and supplements.


Page | 7
        II - Definitions
Section 4: For the purposes of this law, the following definitions shall apply:
intended for human consumption, and includes drink, chewing gum and any substance which has
been used in the manufacture, preparation or treatment of "food" but does not include cosmetics or
tobacco or substances used only as drugs;
Adulterated food: any food to which any substance is added, removed or substituted
to change its composition, weight or volume for fraudulent purposes or to conceal or correct any
defect in its quality or properties;
Food unfits for human consumption: any primary product or food which, without being spoiled or
intoxicated, does not have all the necessary hygienic guarantees, taking into account certain
undesirable elements that it contains, either by contamination or by degradation of its microbiological
and/or chemical quality;
Animal feed: any substance or product, including food additives, whether processed
or unprocessed, intended to be used for oral feeding of animals;
Processed food: any food colored, dyed, pulverized, polished, coated, mixed, canned, flavored,
diluted or thickened with any substance; intended for human consumption, and includes drink,
chewing gum and any substance which has been used in the manufacture, preparation or treatment
of "food" but does not include cosmetics or tobacco or substances used only as drugs;
Adulterated food: any food to which any substance is added, removed or substituted
to change its composition, weight or volume for fraudulent purposes or to conceal or correct any
defect in its quality or properties;
Food unfits for human consumption: any primary product or food which, without being spoiled or
intoxicated, does not have all the necessary hygienic guarantees, taking into account certain
undesirable elements that it contains, either by contamination or by degradation of its microbiological
and for chemical quality;
Animal feed: any substance or product, including food additives, whether processed
or unprocessed, intended to be used for oral feeding of animals; from risk assessment, of weighing
policy alternatives, in consultation with all interested parties considering risk assessment and other
factors relevant for the health protection of consumers and tor the promotion fair trade practices,
and, if needed, selecting appropriate prevention and control options
Risk communication: the interactive exchange of information and opinions throughout the risk
analysis process concerning hazards and risks, risk-related factors and risk perceptions, among risk
assessors, risk managers, consumers, industry, the academic community and other interested
parties, including the explanation of risk assessment findings and the basis of risk management
decisions;
Pest risk analysis: the process of evaluating biological or other scientific or economic
evidence to determine whether an organism is a pest, whether it should be regulated, and the
strength of any phytosanitary measures to be taken against it;
Self-control: all measures taken by farmers, whether carried out by themselves or
by a third party, to ensure that the products they manage at all stages of production, processing and distribution
meet the legal requirements for food safety, product quality and traceability, as well as the monitoring actual
compliance with these requirements;
Processing aid: any substance or material not including apparatus or utensils, and not consumed as a food
ingredient by itself intentionally used in the processing of raw materials, foods or their ingredients, to fulfill a
certain technological purpose during treatment or processing and which may result in the non-intentional but
unavoidable presence of residues or derivatives in the final product;


                                                                                                         Page | 8
Food chain: all stages from primary production of food to consumption through handling, transport,
processing, packaging, distribution and storage;
Food supplement: foodstuff that is a concentrated source of nutrients, substances with a nutritional
or physiological effect, plants or plant extracts that are intended to overcome the deficiencies of a
person's regular diet. These supplements can be in various forms including: capsules, lozenges,
tablets, pills, powder sachets, fluid filled ampoules, flasks with a dropper, solution;
Contaminant: any substance not intentionally added to food, which is present in such
food as a result of the production (including operations carried out in crop husbandry, animal
husbandry and veterinary medicine), manufacture, processing, preparation, treatment, packing,
packaging, transport or storage of such food or as a result of environmental contamination. The term
does not include insect fragments, rodent hairs and other extraneous matter;
Official control: any form of control that the competent authority conducts to check compliance with
regulations,
Parcelling: an object which, regardless of the nature of materials that constitutes it, 1s
intended to contain and protect the foodstuff to allow their handling and their transportation, as well
as to ensure their presentation;
Label: any indication mark, sign or design or any other written description, printed painted marked,
etched or tamped on the food packaging or any other feature related to packaging;
HACCP (hazard analysis and critical control point): a system which identified evaluates and
control hazard which are significant for food safety;
Food hygiene: all conditions and measures necessary to ensure food security and safety at all
stages of the food chain;
Ingredients: any substance, including food additives, used in the manufacture or preparation of food
and present in the final product although in a modified form;
Food safety: assurance that food will not causes damage to the consumer when prepared and /or
eaten according to its intended use;
Inspection: official examination of institutions, food and their treatment, companies of the food
sector their production and the management system, including document, tests on finished products
and food practices, the origin and destination of incoming and outgoing products, to verity their
compliance with legal standards;
Official laboratory: Accredited laboratory per se in conformity with this law;
Phytosanitary measures: any legislation, regulation or official procedure having the purpose to
prevent the introduction of and/or the spread of quarantine pests, or to limit the economic impact of
regulated non-quarantine pests;
 Sanitary measures: any measure applied to protect human life or health within the territory of the country
from risks arising from additive, containing, toxins or diseases –causing organisms in food or feedstuffs or risks
arising from diseases carried by foods which are animals, plants or products thereof or from risks arising from
any other hazards in foods;
Food standards: documents approved by a recognized body which provides a common and the regular use
,of rule ,guidelines or characteristics for products or related processes and production methods of which the
observance is not compulsory .Prescriptions in terms of terminology ,symbol, packaging ,mark, marketing and
labelling relating to a product, the process and production methods may equally treat only these services;
Nutrients: fatty acids, amino acids, proteins, simple sugars, probiotics, vitamins and minerals;
Economic operator: any natural or legal person I the field of production, manufacturing, preparation,
manipulation, treatment, packaging, transportation, packing, conservation, import, export,
distribution, food sales or any other related activity;


Page | 9
Entry and exit point: airport, ports and inlands ports, parcel post centers or officially designed land
border points;
Pre-packaging: packing a product in a package without the knowledge of the buyer, such that the
quantity of the product in the package has a given value beforehand and which cannot be modified
without notice;
Production: farming, harvesting, capturing, treatment or food processing including the acquisition,
the treatment, the rearing of farm animals before slaughter until there are released for consumption;
Quality: All properties and characteristics of a product which makes it fit to satisfy the hidden or
expressed needs of the users. Food quality comprises the following four components: hygienic,
nutritional, organoleptic and use;
Risk: a function of the probability of an adverse health effect and the severity of that effect,
consequential to a hazard (s) in food;
Surveillance: scheduled observation or evaluation of a parameter, with respect to a particular point
or time, which is later compared to a reference;
Traceability: the ability to trace and follow a food, feed and food-producing animal or substance
intended to be or expected to be incorporated into a food or feed, through all stage of production,
processing and distribution;
Utensil: the whole or part of a tool, instrument, machines, appliances and other instruments used
for that could be used for the production, the manufacturing, the preparation, the manipulation, the
packaging, the transportation, the packing, the conservation, the import, the export, the distribution,
the sales, the serving or storage of any food.
        CHAPTER II: GENERAL PRINCIPLES OF FOOD SAFETY
Section 5: In accordance with the food safety principle, food products and objects that come in
contact with food products, including ingredients, packaging, and utensils used for their production,
manufacturing and preparation should not constitute a risk factor to human health.
Section 6: (1) The applicable legislation and the intervention of competent authorities and economic operators
with regards to food shall be based on the principles of risk analysis.
(2) Risk assessment shall comply with scientific criteria and be carried out independently and transparently.
(3) Risk management and communication on risk shall be based on risk assessment.
Section 7 :(1) Hygienic measures taken shall be based on available scientific evidence.
(2) They shall not apply in a manner as constitute arbitrary pr unjustified discrimination or a disguised restriction
to commerce, in accordance with the principles of free exchange.
Section 8: The food legislation shall seek to protect the interest of consumers and provide them with essential
conditions for making a well-informed choice of food products to consume
In this regard, it shall be responsible for preventing:
    1. fraudulent and misleading practices;
    2. the marketing of defective and for adulterated food products;
    3. any practice that may mislead the consumer.
Section 9: (1) The implementation of the principle of precaution shall enable decision makers to take action
where scientific data is insufficient, inconclusive or uncertain, but where, according to information gathered
from an objective and preliminary scientific assessment, they are reasonable grounds to be worried about
potentially dangerous health hazards resulting from a phenomenon, a product or a process.
(2) Its implementation may be required to maintain the level of heath protection chosen.




                                                                                                        Page | 10
Section 10: All food related activities shall be carried out in accordance with the provisions standards
,guidelines and other national and international recommendations contained in regularly ratified
international treaties governing food safety ,notably those of Codex Alimentarius ,the World Trade
Organization (WTO),the International Plant Protection Convention(IPPC),the World Organization for
Animal Health (OIE),the Worldwide Organization of Consumers (OIC) as well as those established
by the Cartagena Protocol on Biotechnological Risk Protection.
Section 11: (1) Economic operators of the food sector shall ensure that their activities comply with
the provisions of the food legislation.
(2) They shall be responsible for protecting consumer heath in relation to the product they release
for consumption.
Section 12: As part of the health inspection procedures, economic operators shall enjoy the
guarantees of transparency, impartiality, and proportionality granted to persons subject to an
inspection measure.
Section 13: On the proposal of health security entities, the competent authority shall:
   -   take appropriate measure to ensure that the actors concerned participate in food safety
       decision making process;
   -   depending on the nature, the seriousness and scope of food safety risks, take appropriate
       measures to inform the actor concerned of the nature of these risks and the measures
       taken to prevent, reduce or eliminate them. To this end, economic operators as well as
       relevant government services, each in his own sphere of competence as laid down for the
       implementation of this law, shall be bound to provide monthly data required for monitoring;
   -   provide access to information in its possession relating to food safety, including information
       on hazardous substances and activities.
       CHAPTER III: ORGANIZATION AND FUNCTIONING OF THE FOOD SAFETY SYSTEM
            1. Organization
Section 14: A food safety policy and strategy paper shall define the Government vision of food safety
and outlines each stakeholder’s responsibilities and activities.
Section 15: Special instruments shall lay down the conditions for organizing the food system.
            2. Functioning
Section 16: The food control procedure shall take the following elements into account:
   -   visual and documentary inspection;
   -   food chain inspection;
   -   inspection of the production environment;
   -   collection of samples;
   -   Physico-chemical and microbiological analysis;
   -   interpretation of the results of the analyses of samples collected.
Section 17: Control operations shall be carried out in compliance with the following principles:
   -   transparency between the economic operator and the government service(s);
   -   impartiality, honesty and confidentiality in the part of authorized officers;
   -   complementarity and effectiveness pf the action of each competent authority;
   -   use of nationally and internationally recognized standards.




Page | 11
Section 18 :( 1) Laboratories accredited and/or approved by the competent authority shall be
responsible for supporting food safety entities.
(2) A separate instrument shall be lay down the conditions for accrediting and/or approving
laboratories.
Section 19 :( 1) Any substance added to a food product must be subject to prior authorization by
the competent authority, or authorized by a national or international standard.
(2) Special instruments shall establish the list of authorized food substances and lay down conditions
for their use.
Section 20: In case of emergency or force majeure, the competent authority may, through a duly
reasoned act:
   -   impose a total ban on the production, manufacturing, preparation and sale of any food
       product;
   -   impose conditions for producing, manufacturing, preparing and selling food products;
   -   request the analysis and examination of any food product;
   -   require that any food should be kept or isolated in a location and restricted from use for a
       period deemed necessary;
   -   request the destruction or disposal of any foodstuff;
   -   order any necessary measure;
   -   request the treatment of any foodstuff, as appropriate.


Section 21:(1) Any economic operator in Cameroon involved in the production, preservation,
processing, transportation, packing, packaging, import, export, distribution and sale of foodstuff must
be registered with the competent authority.
(2) The competent authority shall proceed with registration, where the economic operator complies
with the national and international legislation in force in his area of activity.


Section 22: The self-control of a production or distribution establishment must be based on a guide
duly validated by the competent authority, to ensure product compliance before its supply for
consumption.
Section 23: The principle of traceability shall be applied to all activities relating to human food and
feed for animals intended for human consumption.
Section 24: (1) Economic operators, as well as food producing companies and their staff must
comply with all the rules of hygiene provided for the implementation of this law, in line with the
standards of Codex Alimentarius or the guidelines of any other international organization involved in
food safety.
(2) The competent authority and, its behalf, its official food control and monitoring services, shall
ensure compliance with hygiene rules and practices.
Section 25: Any economic operator, who manufactures, processes or distributes food products must
develop and implement a hazard analysis, critical control point (HACCP) system or another
recognized system in compliance with the terms defined by international standards.
Section 26: Any pre-packaged foodstuff must be labelled in accordance with the legislation in force.
Section 27: Public sensitization and food safety promotion activities shall be organized by the
entities responsible for food safety.



                                                                                            Page | 12
            3. Application for Authorization to Release Foodstuff, Animal Feed, Food
               Additives and Supplements for consumption
Section 28:(1) The authorization to release for consumption (ARC) shall be issued by the competent
authority.
(2) It shall comprise the following elements:
    - the report on the sanitary control of the production site, using the BPF/BPH, HACCP or
         other quality system;
    - the self-control system;
    - the results of analysis carried out by the accredited or approved laboratories;
    - expert opinion on the product concerned;
    - the status or knowledge and current control of the risks associated with these foodstuffs.
Section 29: The following shall be subject to ARC:
    - foodstuff produced at national level;
    - imported foodstuffs;
    - feed for animals intended for human consumption;
    - food additive and processing aids;
    - food supplements.
Section 30: The validity of authorization for release for consumption of foodstuffs and animal feed
shall be three (3) years renewable.

       CHAPTER IV: FOOD INSPECTION
Section 31: Inspections carried out pursuant to this law concern:
   -   imported, exported, and locally marketed foodstuffs;
   -   food processing companies and their environment, as well as transportation means the
       equipment and materials used for involved at the different stages of the food chain;
   - food processing company staff,
   - the various principles and methods used.
Section 32: The general organization of food inspection in Cameroon shall be lay down by a
separate instrument.
       CHAPTER V: FINANCING FOOD SAFETY ACTIVITIES
Section 33: Food safety activities may be financed through:
    - State subsides;
    - any support from development partners;
    - any other form of financing in compliance with the legislation in force.
Section 34: The condition for collecting, mobilizing and using funds intended to finance food safety
activities shall be lay down in a separate instrument.
       CHAPTER VI: ADMINISTRATIVE AND CRIMINAL PENALTIES
Section 35: Without prejudice to criminal proceedings, the competent authority may, in the event of
failure by companies to fulfil their obligations under this law, or where a serious or immediate danger
is established, impose one of the following penalties:
   -   suspension or cancellation of the registration of the company;
   -   seizure or destruction of any food, utensil, material, substance or other object in connecting
       with an offence committed or used in the commission of an offence;
   -   suspension of the approval of any laboratory, equipment or professional authorized by this
       law;
   -   temporary closure of the company, temporary or permanent banning of the use of company
       equipment or some company premises;
   -   temporary or permanent banning of the official of the defaulting food processing company
       from participating in any other food processing company.



Page | 13
Section 36: Without prejudice to the punishment of offences already provided for with respect to
public health offences, a prison term of from three (3) months to three (3) years and a fine of from
CFAF one hundred thousand (100 000) to CFAF two million (2000 000) shall be imposed on :
   -   whoever supplies adulterated food or food unfit for human and /or animal consumption;
   -   the sworn officer who seizes foodstuff for reasons other than those provided for under this
       law,
   -   the sworn officer who, without authorization, discloses information gathered in the
       discharge of duties conferred by this law.

   CHAPTER VII: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISION
Section 37: Implementing instruments shall, as appropriate, supplement the provision of this law.
Section 38: All previous provisions repugnant to this law are repealed.
Section 39: This law shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency, and
inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French.

                                                                     Yaoundé, 11 December 2018.
                                                                                         Paul Biya,
                                                                       President of the Republic.

Page | 17
   1.3.3.     Decree No. 2011/2584 /PM of 23 August 2011 lay down conditions for the
       protection of soils and the sub-soil

       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
1. This decree lays down the conditions for the protection of soils and the sub-soil.
2. For the purposes of implementation of this decree, the following definitions shall apply:
distribution: supply to the market, transportation, storage or sale of pesticides or fertilizers or any
chemical products.
harmful effect to the environment: any effect which is noxious or which renders the environment
hazardous to human, animal or plant life.
fertilizer: any substance or material containing one or more nutritive plant elements approved and
used as such for the purpose of enhancing plant growth and production.
bulk fertilizer: fertilizer distributed without packaging or in any other unpackaged form.
compound fertilizer: fertilizer containing at least two nutritive elements in its chemical
composition.
custom-made fertilizer: mixed fertilizer, pre-pared following the individual specifications supplied
by the consumer.
label: indication of everything in a written, printed or graphic form on the immediate packaging or
during a specific message on a fertilizer.
operator: a natural or legal person exercising commercial and/or industrial activities
which may lead to soil degradation.
manufacturing: preparing, composing, creating active ingredients, adding substances, mixing,
formulating, packaging or repackaging, labelling or treating in any form the active ingredient for the
purpose of sale.
noxious body: any species, strain or plant or animal biotype, as well as the pathogenic
agent, harmful to plants and plant products.
pesticide: any substance or compound substances intended for repelling, destroying or controlling
pests, vectors of diseases and undesirable plant or animal species causing damage and also harmful
during the production, processing, storage, transportation or marketing of food, agricultural products,
timber and non-Wood Forest products.
body of water: any part of the territory which is occupied or is likely to be occupied comprising the
sea, a river, stream, brook, lake, marshland or backwater.
chemical product: a product obtained by processes or chemical combination.
adulterated product: a product which Contains any poisonous or noxious substance
in such substantial quantity as to render it harmful to plant, animal, human, aquatic life, the soil or
water when used following the mode of usage indicated on the label.
advertising: offer of sale and use of pesticides by the print or electronic media, notice
boards, presentations, gifts, shows or by word of mouth.
mulching: agricultural technique which consists in covering young plants with straw or manure, to
avoid evapotranspiration and other parasites.
arable land: surface part of the soil suitable for agricultural activities.
erosion zone: part of the land where, due to the nature of soils or conditions of their occupancy,
lack of plant cover or hedge, their declivity, the agricultural activities and the other human activities
leading to soil degradation.




                                                                                             Page | 18
         CHAPTER II: SPECIAL CONDITIONS FOR THE PROTECTION OF SOILS AND THE
         SUB-SOIL
         I- Protection against Erosion and Desertification
3. Any activity relating to the exploitation of soils must be such as to avoid or reduce soil erosion and
desertification.
4. (1) Exploitation shall be prohibited in high-risk erosion zones.
(2) An order of the Senior Divisional Officer enacted on the proposal of technical services with
territorial jurisdiction, shall delimit the high-risk erosion zones and lay down safety Conditions.
         II. Protection against the Loss of Arable Lands.
5. Any activity which degrades or modifies the quality and/or structure of arable lands
or contributes to the loss of such lands shall be prohibited.
         III. Control of the Pollution of the Soil and its Resources by Chemical Products,
         Pesticides and Fertilizers

6. Any natural or legal person who uses fertilizers, pesticides or chemical products shall be bound
to comply with national and international standards.
7. The intensive use of fertilizers in agricultural exploitation shall be subject to prior assessment of
the physical and chemical conditions of the soil.
8. Besides the conditions laid down by the regulations in force in Cameroon, the distribution of
fertilizers, pesticides or chemical products shall be subject to the sticking of a label comprising the
following indications:
     - net weight of the packaging content;
     - mark or name of the product;
     - name and address of the manufacturer or the approved distributor;
     - specifications relating to the quality and guarantee;
     - indications concerning the product toxicity level;
     - risks to public health and the environment;
     - active principle of the product;
     - product usage notice.
9. Any natural or legal, private or public person who possesses an agricultural holding
and is involved in the intensive use of fertilizers and/or pesticides or soil conditioning apparatuses,
shall be bound to regularly conduct an assessment of their impact on the environment, in accordance
with the regulations in force.
10.Any natural or legal person who desires to manufacture or package fertilizers and /or pesticides
on the national territory, shall be bound to carry out an environmental impact assessment, in
accordance with the regulation in force.

         CHAPTER III: LIST OF THE FERTILIZERS, PESTICIDES AND OTHER CHEMICAL
         SUBSTANCES SUBJECT TO AUTHORIZATION
11. (1) The list of fertilizers, pesticides and other chemical substances subject to the provisions of
this decree shall be that whose use is authorized by regulation in force.
(2) Any other chemical product not featuring on the approval list, shall be prohibited save for research
purposes.
12. (1) The insecticides, rongicides, fungicides, herbicides and metal bromides of the Customs Code
N°2093 30;2903 3000;3808 10;3808 20;3808 30;3808 40;3808 90 which contain the substance
depleting the ozone layer, as regulated by the Montreal Protocol, shall be controlled jointly by the
Ministries in charge of environment and agriculture.
(2) The control referred to under paragraph (1) above concerns the fertilizers and/or pesticides not
in conformity with the provisions of the Carthage and Montreal Protocol the Bale, Bamako,
Rotterdam, Stockholm and Rio on Convention on biodiversity. It is aimed at:
Avoiding the importation, production and/or use on the national territory of fertilizers and/or pesticides
not conformity with the statutory provisions;
Ensuring the prevention of the importation, production, distribution and/or use on the territory of the
fertilizers containing noxious substances or harmful properties, even when used in accordance with
the prescribed doses and likely to effect the development of plants, human and animal health and
the environment.
Page | 19
     CHAPTER IV: QUANTITY AUTHORIZED AND CONDITION OF USE OF FERTILIZERS,
PESTICIDES AND OTHER CHEMICAL SUBSTANCES

13. the quantity authorized and the conditions of use of fertilizers, pesticides and other chemical
substances, shall be those laid down by the regulations in force.

       CHAPTER V : MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS

14. (1) any infringement of the provisions of this decree shall be liable to the sanctions laid down by
law N°96/12 of 5 August 1996 relating to environmental management, law N°2003/3 to regulate the
activities of the fertilizer subsector in Cameroon and law N° 2003/3 of 21 April 2003 relating to
phytosanitary protection.

(2) However, the compromise and arbitration provided for in the abovementioned framework law
shall be implemented as and when necessary.
15. All previous provisions repugnant to this decree are hereby repealed.
16. The Ministers in charge of the environment, agriculture, State property, mines and
technological development and forestry are responsible, each in his own sphere, implementation of
this decree, which shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency, and inserted
in the Official Gazette in English and French.

                                                                           Yaoundé, 23 August 2011.
                                                                                      Prime Minister
                                                                                    Philemon YANG

 2. PART TWO: LEGAL FRAMEWORK FOR SEEDS
              AND SEEDLINGS




Page | 21
2.1. LAWS




            Page | 22
   2.1.1.      Law N° 2001/14 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed production and marketing

The National Assembly deliberated and adopted
the President of the Republic hereby enacts the law set out below:
       PART I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Section 1- (1) This law shall lay down the conditions for carrying out seed production and marketing
activities in Cameroon.
(2) It is aimed at fostering agricultural development by:
   −   optimizing the use of agricultural research findings to improve seed varieties;
   −   protecting the seed sector against unfair competition;
   −   guaranteeing the quality of seeds intended for farmers;
   −   protecting breeders against imitation;
   −   preserving national photogenetic resources.
 (2) The Seed Fund shall be financed from certification fees as well as from any other appropriate
resources.
(3) The establishment as well as the organization and management of the Seed Fund shall be laid
down by decree of the President of the Republic
Section 2- For purposes of this law and its instruments of implementation, the following shall mean:
“Seed” all or part of plant organism enabling the multiplication or production thereof, namely grain,
cutting, plant, shoot, tuber, bulb, spore or vitro- plant;
“Seed authority” public service charged with formulating and implementing Sate policy on seeds,
notably, the Ministry in charge of Agriculture;
“Seed production and marketing activities”: any operation consisting in producing, processing,
importing, exporting, or marketing seed;
“breeder»: a person who has discovered and grown a new variety. The term shall not include a
person who has redeveloped or d rediscovered a variety whose existence is widely known or is a
topic of common knowledge;
“new variety” plant varieties created or discovered resulting from a special genetic process or from
a special composition of hereditary processes and different from any other plant group and which
constitute a distant entity in respect of their ability to multiply;
“nurseries” plantation and farms reserved for the production notably of seedling of plant and fruits
trees, cocoa trees, oil palms, coffee trees, ornamental plants, forest trees and vegetables.
Section 3-(1) Seed production and marketing activities governed by this law and defined in section
2 above shall be carried out under State control. To this effect the State shall stipulate recognized
technical standard relating to seeds, and ensue quality control and certification of seeds.
(2) The duty of defined technical standards quality control and certification of seed incumbent on the
State may be instructed to a specialized institution set up for this purpose by decree of the President
of the Republic.
(3) Seed related research shall remain subject in this law and regulations in force.




Page | 23
        PART II SEED PRODUCTION AND MARKETING ACTIVITIES


Section 4-Seed production and marketing activities may concern one, more or all of the activities
defined in Section 2 above.
        CHAPTER I: GENERAL CONDITIONS FOR CARRYING OUT SEED PRODUCTION AND
        MARKETING ACTIVITIES
Section 5-(1) Any individual or corporate body shall be free to carry out seed production or marketing
activities in any part of the country, subject to compliance with the law and regulations in force, with
the applicable technical standards as well as with international commitment subscribed to by
Cameroon notably the International Convention on Biodiversity.
(2) Seed production and marketing activities shall however be subject to prior declaration under
condition laid down by regulations.
Section 6-The importation, production and marketing of seeds shall be subject to the conditions
defined in specifications fixed by joint order of the ministers in charge of agriculture and of trade.
Section 7- Any individual or corporate body carrying out a seeds production or marketing activities
shall keep a register or transaction per species, variety and category under condition defined by
regulations.
Section 8-(1) A national Seed and New Plants Varieties Council shall give an advisory opinion on
all matters relating of production, marketing, quality control and certification of seeds and new plant
varieties.
(2) A decree of the President of the Republic shall define the setting up, organization and functioning
of the National Seeds and New Plant Varieties Council.
        CHAPTER II: CLASSIFICATION, QUALITY CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF
        SEEDS
Section 9-(1) Seeds of all plant species and varieties shall be classified in three categories:
    −   Basic seeds;
    −   Certified seeds;
    −   Standard seeds.
(2) The criteria for classification shall be laid down by regulation.
Section 10 – (1) An official Catalogue of Species and Varieties in which the plant varieties developed in or
introduce into Cameroon shall be registered under conditions laid down by regulation is hereby instituted.
(2) The featured of the official catalogue of species and varieties as well as the conditions for its keeping shall
be defined by regulations.
Section 11 – (1) basic seeds and certified seeds produced in Cameroon shall be certified by the seeds
authority.
(2) All seeds marketed in Cameroon shall be subject to quality control by the seeds authority.
(3) The seed certification and quality control mechanism and procedures provided for in subsections (1) and
(2) above shall be defined by regulation.
Section 12-(1) Officers in charge of seed certification and seed quality shall take the oath in accordance with
the law in force.
(2) A separate instrument shall fix the technical power and lay down the rules and regulation of the officers
referred to in subsection (1) above.
Section 13 – Seed certification activities, separation homogeneity- stability (SHS) and agricultural
and technological value (ATV) tests for the entry varieties into the catalogue referred to in section 12
above as well as the conformity shall be subject to payment of a fee whose amount and collection
conditions shall be lay down by joint order of the minister in charge of agriculture and the minster in
charge of finance.
                                                                                                      Page | 24
Section 14-(1) Only seeds or plant varieties registered in the Official Catalogue of Species and
Varieties in one of the categories referred to in section 9 of this law shall be sold in Cameroon.
(2) The seed marketed must meet general standard of chemical treatment, storage, packaging and
labelling fixed by joint order of the minster in charge of agriculture and the minster in charge of trade.
Section 15-(1) Seed quality control and certification analysis shall be conducted in the referral
national laboratory or in any other laboratory approved for this purpose by joint order of the minister
in charge of agriculture and the minister in charge of scientific and technical research.
(2) Where test results are challenged, second analysis may be conducted at the expense of the
plaintiff, in another approved laboratory.
       CHAPTER III: SEED PRODUCTION DEVELOPMENT
Section 16 - (1) Incentives must be provided notably in the areas of finance, customs duties and
taxes, real estate, land and logistics in order to promote private investment in the seed sector and
make national seed production more competitive.
(2) Indirect aid to seed development in the private sector may be granted by the Government to
facilitate personal training.
Section 17 - (1) A seed fund is hereby established in order to support seed production, seed
research as well as local seed development and preservation
(2) The Seed Fund shall be financed from certification fees as well as from any other appropriate
resources.
(3) The establishment as well as the organization and management of the Seed Fund shall be laid
down by decree of the President of the Republic.
       CHAPTER IV: PROTECTION OF NEW PLANT VARIETIES
Section 18 - The protection of new plant varieties as defined by this law shall be governed by the
provisions of Annex X to the 19 February 1999 Agreement on t the Revision of the Bangui Accord of
2 March 1977 to institute the African Intellectual Property Organization (AIPO).
       PART III: OFFENCES AND PENALTIES
Section 19 - (1) Without prejudice to action for third party liability that may be instituted and
notwithstanding certain administrative penalties provided for by Law No. 90-31 of 10 August 1990
governing commercial activity in Cameroon that may be inflicted by the minister in charge of
agrıculture on persons undertaking activities governed by this law whoever
   −   fails to keep a register of transactions by species and by variety.
   −   objects to seed quality control markets substandard seed
   −   markets species and varieties not registered in the catalogue of species and varieties:
   −   falsifies seeds,
   −   in general, undertake seed-related activities in violation of the provisions of this law, shall be
       punished with imprisonment for from 1(one) to 3 (three) months or with fine of from 50,000
       (fifty thousand) CFA francs to 2,000,000 (two million CFA francs, or with both such
       imprisonment and fine.
(2) In the event of a repetition of the offence, the penalties provided in subsection (1) above shall be
doubled.
Section 20 -The penalty provided for by this law shall apply without prejudice to the provisions of
the Penal Code.




Page | 25
Section 21 - (1) Without prejudice to the recognized prerogatives of the legal department and
general criminal investigation officers, sworn officials of the seed authority shall be responsible for
detecting, establishing and prosecuting violations of the provisions of this law.
(2) While performing their duties, sworn officials must carry their professional cards.
Section 22 - (1) Any violation established shall be recorded in a normal report. The detection and
establishment of violations shall be carried out by 2 (two) officers who shall draw up reports. Such
report shall have the force of prima facie evidence.
(2) The report thus drawn up shall be countersigned by the accused. Where the latter refuses to do
so, this shall be mentioned in the margin of the report.
Section 23 - (1) Any report establishing an offence shall be notified to the offender by any written
means. The latter shall have 20 (twenty) days from the date of such notification, within which to
challenge it. Beyond such period no complaint shall be admissible.
(2) The complaint shall be lodged with the seed authority which shall decide within 15 (fifteen) days
following reception of the petition. Beyond such period, the petition shall be deemed to have received
a favorable response and report establishing the offence shall become null and void.
(3) Where after considering the petition, the seed authority deems it justified, it shall accede to the
plaintiff’s request and the establishment of the offence disregarded. Otherwise, the seed authority
shall institute legal proceeding in accordance with the laws in force.
Section 24 - (1) Violations of this law and its implementation instruments may give rise to a
settlement between the seed authority and the offender, where the latter so requests.
(2) The settlement referred to above shall be registered at the expense of the offender and shall put
an end to public prosecution where it is reached prior to pronouncement of the judgment on the
merits by a competent court.
(3) The fines resulting from the settlement provided for above shall be subject to the same conditions
of collection as the fines imposed by ordinary law courts.
       Part IV: Miscellaneous and Final Provisions
Section 25 - The Minister in charge of Agriculture shall enter in the Official Catalogue of Species
and Varieties, the species and varieties already known and grown in Cameroon, after updating their
descriptive card indexes provided by persons operating in the sector.
Section 26 - The various stakeholders of the seed sector shall have a period of (1) one year with
effect from the date of enactment of this law within which to comply with its provisions.
Section 27 - All previous provisions repugnant hereto are hereby repealed
Section 28 - This law shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency and
inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French.
                                                                               Yaoundé, 23 July 2001
                                                                          President of the Republic
                                                                                           Paul BIYA




                                                                                            Page | 26
   2.2. DECREES




Page | 27
   2.2.1.     Decree N° 2005/153 of 4 may 2005 bearing the creation, organization and
       functioning of a national seeds and new plant variety council.

                               THE PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law N°2001/014 of July 2001 on Seed Activity;
Mindful of Decree N°2004/320 of 8 December 2004 organization the Government;
Mindful of Decree N°2005/118 of 15 April 2005 bearing the organization of the Ministry of Agriculture
and Rural Development;
                                             DECREES
Article 1 – A National Seeds and New Plant Variety Council, under the Minister in charge of
Agriculture, abbreviated as: NSNPVC is hereby created.
Article 2- NSNPVC shall be a consultative organ charged with giving an opinion on all matters
relating to the production, the commercialization, quality control, certification of seeds and plants,
and on plant varieties susceptible to developing and orienting the implementation of nation seed
policy.
In this regard, it shall:
    -   advise the government on all subjects concerning the sustainable development of the seed
        sector;
    -   give an opinion on the legal and regulatory draft texts relating to seed activity;
    -   propose the insertion or the radiation of species and varieties in the official catalogue of
        species and varieties;
    -   give an opinion on applications for financial support addressed to the Seeds Fund;
    -   Give an opinion on the introduction into the national territory of new technologies, including
        genetically modified organisms relating to plant material.
Article 3 – (1) NSNPVC shall comprise the following:
    −   President: The Minister in charge of Agriculture or his representative;
    −   Members
    −   Two (02) representatives of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Finance;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of public health;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of development programme;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of research;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Environment;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Forest;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry of Livestock;
    −   One (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Commerce;
    −   The Director General of the Institute for Agricultural Research;
    −   Two (02) representatives of State University Institutions in Cameroon;
    −   three (03) representatives of seeds establishments including one (01) for cereals, grain
        vegetables, fodder and market garden seeds, one (01) for producers of multiplication plant
        seeds and one (01) for nurseries;
    −   One (01) representative of agricultural multipliers of seed and plants;
    −   Three (03) representatives of farmers;
                                                                                           Page | 28
    −  One (01) representative of professional importers and exporters of seeds;
    −  One (01) representative of industries of agricultural products transformation;
    −  Two (02) representatives of seeds and plants distributors;
    −  One (01) representative of financial institutions of the seed sector;
    −  One (01) representative of the chamber of agriculture, livestock and Forests;
    −  One (01) representative of the chamber of commerce, Industry, Mines and handicraft.
    −  (2) The President of NSNPVC may appeal to all other persons by reason of their
       competence on the questions inscribed on the agenda, who shall participle in the work of
       NSPVS with a consultative voice.
Article 4- (1) the members of NSNPVC from the public and private sectors shall be designated by
the Administrations and the socio-professional Organization where they belong.
(2) The composition of NSNPVC shall be fixed by an order of the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
(3) The duration of the mandate of the President and members of the NSNPVC shall be three (03)
years renewable.
Article 5- (1) the secretariat of the NSNPVC shall be ensured by the Department in charge of regulation,
quality control and certification of seeds and plants.
(2) The secretariat shall:
    -   prepare meetings;
    -   draft opinions, proposals and recommendations of the NSNPVC;
    -   ensure the monitoring and implementation of conventions in the field of competence of NSNPVC;
    -   draft reports and minutes of meetings;
    -   conserve the documentation of NSNPVC;

Article 6- (1) a commission for the approval of species and varieties is hereby created under the
NSNPVC. The commission, on the basis of the results of the Distinction, homogeneity and
Stability (DHS) and the Agronomic and Technology Values (ATV) tests, shall give its opinion on
the species and the applicant varieties to be inscribed on the Official Catalogue of Species and
Plants.
(2) However, the NSNPVC may establish, where necessary, Special Committees by topic, species
or group of species to study the specific technical aspects relevant to their area of competence.
(3) The presidents of release commission and the Special Committees cited in paragraphs (1) and
(2) above shall be chosen by the NSNPVC from among its members.
Article 7 – The functions of the President and members of NSNPVC shall be gratuitous. However,
the President and the members as well as the persons invited in consultative capacity, shall benefit
from siting allowances the amount of which shall be fixed by an Order of the Minister in charge of
Agriculture.
Article 8- (1) NSNPVC shall meet on the convocation of its President at least two (02) times per
year or at the request of the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
(2) The NSNPVC may validly deliberate only in the presence of at least two thirds (2/3) of its
members.
Article 9 - (1) The NSNPVC shall give its opinion by a simple majority of members present. In case
of a tie, the vote of the President shall be decisive.
(2) The Reports or minutes of meetings shall be transmitted to the Minister in charge of Agriculture
within fifteen (15) days from the date of the meeting.

Page | 29
Article 10 -The NSNPVC shall address a report to the Prime Minister, Head of Government, on
month after the closure of the budgetary year.
Article 11-The running expenses of the NSNPVC shall be supported by the budget of the Ministry
in charge of Agriculture.
Article 12-All other previous provisions contrary to this Decree, especially those of Decree
N°90/10009 of 13 June 1990, bearing the creation of the Seed Council, are hereby repealed.
Article 13-The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development is charged with the implementation of
this decree which shall be registered band published following the urgency procedure, then inserted
in the Official Gazette in French and English.
                                                                         Yaoundé, the 4 may 2005
                                                                       President of the Republic
                                                                                        Paul BIYA




                                                                                        Page | 30
   2.2.2.    Decree N° 2005/169 of 26th May 2005 on the creation, the organisation and
       management of the seed fund.

                                    THE PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC
Mindful of the Constitution,
Mindful of Ordinance N°62/OF/4 of the 7th February 1962 to regulate the presentation, the conditions of
executing the budget of the Republic of Cameroon, her incomes, her expenditures and all other forms of related
operations, modified by law N° 2002/001 of 19th April 2002;
Mindful of Law N°2001/014 of 23rd July 2001 on seed activity;
Mindful of Decree N°2004/320 of 8th December 2004 organizing the Government;
                                                   DECREES
        PART I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Section 1: The present decree creates and fixes the conditions for the organization and management of the
seed fund, hereinafter referred to as “the Fund”.
Section 2: (1) The seed fund is a special account in the treasury meant to support the development of seed
activity, carry out research on seed-related issues, as well as the development and preservation of local seeds.
(2) This fund is placed under the authority of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
        PART II: RESOURCES AND EXPENDITURES OF THE FUND
Section 3: (1) the annual resources of the Fund, according to the Finance law are made up of:
    -   revenue from operations of seeds certification and conformity tests;
    -   registration charges of seed varieties in the official catalogue;
    -   rents from seed multiplication farms and the dissemination of plant material;
    -   charges for issuing seed activity certificates;
    -   State subventions;
    -   All other resources authorized by the finance law.
The resources of the Fund are public property hence, as such, they must be checked annually and controlled
by competent state authorities.
Section 4: (1) expenditures to be handled by the Fund include:
    -   Support to the development of see activity;
    -   Seed-related research operations;
    -   The development and preservation of local seeds.
(2) All expenditures involved in the Fund must first of all be checked and approved by the controller of finance
appointed under the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
        PART III: THE MANAGEMENT OF THE FUND
Section 5: the Fund is managed by:
    − a manager;
    − an accountant.
Section 6: The Ministry in charge of Finance delegates by order, his power of budget prescription to the
Minister in charge of Agriculture with the aim of proper of the Fund created by this decree. To this effect, the
Minister in charge of Agriculture:
    -   ensures the engagement, the liquidation and the prescription of all expenditures;
    -   authorizes the creation of proper bank accounts for the fund;
    -   takes all necessary measures pertaining to the management of the Fund.

Page | 31
Section 7: (1) The Minister in charge of Agriculture sets up an administrative account for every
period that makes precision of all income and expenditure effected.
(2) The administrative account is transmitted to the Minister in charge of Finance and also to
competent State control agency of such accounts.
Section 8: The accountant is in charge of:
   -   ensuring financial recovery and the liquidation of the expenses of the Fund;
   -   handling all the transactions involving cash and other assets of the Fund;
   -   setting up a management account for each period;
   -   preparing the annual administrative account to be submitted for signature by the Minister in
       charge of Agriculture;
   -   Ensuring and certifying the sincerity and accuracy of all documents pertaining to the Fund.
Section 9: The accountant is appointed among other accountants of the treasury by an Order of the
Minister in charge of Finance, he is a public accountant, hence he is subject to all disciplinary rules,
expected to manage and keep the account as per the accounting system of the Treasury
Department.
Section 10: The accounting system of the Fund must follow the rules of the public account system.
The system of all public financial operations is applicable to the Fund.
Section 11: (1) All projects and programs submitted for funding from the resources of the FUND
must present the following:
   - priorities with respect to science and seed technology;
   - studies and research works to be carried out;
   - The cost of operations to be carried out.
(2) Disbursing the funds for the execution of projects provided for in sub-section (1) above is done
in conformity with the regulations in force.
Section 12: the following can benefit financially from the resources of the Fund:
   - charges related to the control and certification of seeds;
   - seed companies operating in Cameroon who are in conformity with the seed law;
   - local farmers involved in the multiplication of seed and plants;
   - Private or public research in the domain of science and seed technology.
   PART IV: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Section 13: (1) the Minister in charge of Agriculture is responsible for the technical follow up and
control of all public or private programmes and projects financed by the Fund.
(2) The programmes and projects of the public sector are prioritized in the use of the resources of
the Fund.
Section 14: the conditions for returning the resources of the Fund are provided for in a joint text from
the Minister in charge of Agriculture and the Minister in charge of Finance.
Section 15: this decree shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency and
inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French.
                                                                                Yaoundé 26 May 2005
                                                                           President of the Republic
                                                                                            Paul BIYA


                                                                                            Page | 32
   2.2.3.    Decree N°2005/3090/PM of 29th august 2005 to fix the quality and the duties of
       sworn officials in charge of control and seed certification

                        THE PRIME MINISTER HEAD OF GOVERNMENT
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law N°2001/014 of 23rd July 2001 related to seed activity;
Mindful of Decree N° 92/089 of 4th may 1992 prescribing the attributes of the Prime Minister modified
and completed by decree n° 95/145 of 4" august 1995;
Mindful of Decree N° 2004/320 of 8 December 2004 to organize the Government;
Mindful of Decree N° 2004/321 of 8 December 2004 appointing the Prime Minister;
                                             DECREES
       PART I: THE QUALITY OF THE AGENTS IN CHARGE OF QUALITY CONTROL AND
       SEED CERTIFICATION
Section 1: The present decree states the quality and the missions of the sworn staff in charge of the
control and certification of seeds.
Section 2. (1) Under the reserve of their competence and their experience on seed science and
seed technology could be the following staff of the seed administration as staff in charge of control
and seed certification:
   -   Seed inspector: civil servants of category A or contract officers of administration of categories
       10 to 12:
   -   Seed Analyst: civil servants of category A or contract officers of the administration of
       categories 10 to 12:
   -   Seed Controller or seed laboratory technician: civil servant of at least Category C or contract
       officer of at last category 7.
(2) The officers in charge of quality control and seed certification are appointed by the minister in
charge of Agriculture.
Section 3. Before getting in to activity, and at the request of the Minister in charge of Agriculture,
the agents referred to in Section 2 above shall take oath before the court of First instance of their
various places of work according to the regulations in force.
       PART II - THE MISSION OF AGENTS IN CHARGE OF CONTROL AND SEED
       CERTIFICATION
Section 4. (1) Seed inspectors have the duty to carry out seed certification on the field and the
control of the quality of seeds on sale.
(2) Seed Analysts have the duty to carry out seed sample analyses in the laboratory. They are
assisted by seed laboratory technicians.




Page | 33
Section 5. (1) Seed inspectors and controllers have the authority to:
   -   Visit nurseries, multiplication plots and seed packaging centres;
   -   take seed samples for laboratory analyses to ensure that seeds that arc placed for
       certification or on sale respect the norms and conditions laid down in the seed law and the
       regulations in force;
   -   Visit seed storage and seed sales structures:
   -   proceed to the examination of the documents of the seed establishments and seeds
       merchants in view of checking that the seeds distributed in the country respect the standards
       defined by the law and regulations in force:
   -   take note of all disorder, proceed to conservatory confiscation, order for quarantine control
       and request the degrading or the destruction of seeds which do not meet the standard:
   -   follow-up the transaction procedures.
(2) The destruction of seeds referred to in (1) above is executed by a commission designated by the
Minister in charge of agriculture at the expense of the defaulter.
Section 6. (1) During their mission of control and seed certification, seed inspectors and controllers
are fortified with a warrant issued by the Minister in charge of agriculture.
(2) During their mission of control, and where need arises, the seed inspectors and controllers can
appeal to the judiciary police officers according to regulations in force.
       PART III - GENERALS PROVISIONS.
Section 7. Advantages attached to the functions of seed inspectors, analysts, and controllers and
laboratory technicians are fixed by a joint Order of the Minister of Finance and the Minister of
Agriculture with respect to recommendations from the National Seeds Council.
Section 8. Any sworn agent transferred or dismissed from his functions loses his status.
Section 9. The Ministers in charge of agriculture and Finance are charged with the implementation
this decree which shall be registered, published according to the emergency procedure and inserted
in the Official Gazette in English and French.


                                                                        Yaounde the 29 august 2005
                                                             Prime Minister, Head of Government
                                                                                     INONI Ephraim




                                                                                           Page | 34
   2.3. ORDERS




Page | 35
   2.3.1.     Joint Order No. 381/MINADER/MINCOMERCE of 7 August 2006 to lay down the
       general standards for chemical treatment, storage, packaging and labelling of seeds.

  THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT AND THE MINISTER OF
                                  TRADE
Mindful of the Constitution,
Mindful of Law No. 90/031 of 10 August 1990 on commercial activities;
Mindful of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2004/320 of 8 December 2004 to organise the Government;

Mindful of Decree No. 2004/322 of 8 December 2004 to form the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/089 of 29 March 2005 to organise the Ministry of Trade;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development,
                               HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: This Order lays down the general standards for chemical treatment, storage, packaging
and labelling of seeds.
Article 2: Marketed seeds shall be subject to the provisions of Law No. 90/031 of 10 August 1990
on commercial activities, Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities, as well as
their implementing instruments and the general standards laid down by this joint order.
Article 3: Seeds shall be stored under good conditions to maintain adequate moisture, temperature
as well as good health conditions. The storage facilities shall be isolated from any warehouse that
may contain seeds for consumption or products that may alter the quality of the seeds.
Article 4: Raw seed lots shall be identifiable from the point of transportation to the packaging stage. In this
regard, the transportation equipment or storage container shall be labelled with the producer's reference, the
name of the specie, the name of the variety, the category and the lot number and, if necessary, a certificate of
origin for the parent seeds.
Article 5: Seed treatments shall comply with the seed and phytosanitary regulations in force in Cameroon.

Article 6: (1) Each package containing any category of seed and, where necessary, starting genetic material
shall bear an official label issued by the seed certification authority.
           (2) A duplicate label shall be placed inside the package, in case the name of the species, variety,
lot number, or production date are not permanently printed on the package or on the outer label.

Article 7: (1) The official labels shall have the following colours:

    -    white with purple stripes for pre-basic seeds;
    -    white, for basic seeds;
    -    blue, for first generation certified seeds (R1);
    -    red, for certified seeds of subsequent generations, specifying the serial number of the
         generation starting with R2 certified seeds;
    -    brown for standard seeds.
            (2) The colours of the labels or stickers of any category of seed authorised to be
marketed shall be approved by decision of the Minister of Agriculture upon opinion of the consulting
the National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties.


                                                                                                    Page | 36
Article 8: (1) Unless there is a special authorisation for some species, the mixture of seeds of
different species, varieties or categories shall not be marketed.
             (2) However, the marketing of mixed seeds of species intended for sowing lawns and
meadows, shall be authorised provided that the composition is mentioned on the package.
Article 9: The information on the package and the label for each species or group of species shall
comply with the relevant regulations in force.
Article 10: In cases where officially sealed seed packages containing "certified seeds" or "basic
seeds" have been opened by the company for compelling reasons, the seed certification authority
shall permit re-sealing at the request of the company, which shall carry out all relabelling and
resealing operations under its supervision. During the new sealing, a new official quality control of
the seed shall be carried out and a new number shall be assigned.
Article 11: The Secretaries General of the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development and of the
Ministry in charge of Trade are responsible, each in their respective capacities, for the
implementation of this joint order, which shall be registered, published according to the procedure of
urgency, and then inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French.
                                         Yaounde the 7 August 2006

 The Minister of Agriculture and Rural                                    The Minister of Trade

   2.3.2.      Order No. 0938/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 12 September 2012 relating to
       the organisation and functioning of the Species and Varieties Approval Commission
       within the National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties

                 THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No.2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No.2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/410 of 9 December 2011 to form the Government;
Mindful of the recommendations of the National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties during its
meeting held on 22 September 2009,
Considering service needs,
                              HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
        CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: This Order lays down the organisation of Species and Varieties cultivated or marketed in Cameroon
(CHEV), established within the National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties (CONSOV).
Article 2: (1) The Species and Varieties Approval Commission shall, on the basis of the DUS and VAT test
results, provide technical advice on species and varieties to be registered in the Official Catalogue of Species
and Varieties. In this capacity, it shall be responsible for:
    -   Evaluating the appropriate measures to take for the improvement of national agricultural production
        through the use of certified seeds and seedlings.
    -   Developing protocols (guidelines) for technical tests of distinctness-uniformity-stability (DUS) and
        agronomic and technological value (VAT) of species or groups of species cultivated and/or marketed
        in Cameroon and updating them as necessary.
    -   Giving advice on the introduction of new technologies, including genetically modified organisms, into
        the national territory, based on scientific, technical and economic results.
    -   Directing and coordinating probationary testing activities (technical tests of Distinctness, Uniformity,
        Stability (DUS) and Agronomic and Technical Value (VAT) on the field for new varieties, bearing in
        mind that Article 25 of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities is applicable for
        the already known species and varieties, cultivated and marketed in Cameroon.
    -   Examining applications to be registered in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties, based on
        the information forms submitted by the economic operator and/or the result of the DUS and VAT tests.
(2) The deliberations of the Species and Variety Approval Commission shall be sent to the President of
CONSOV.

        CHAPTER II: ORGANISATION AND FUNCTIONING
Article 3: (1) The Species and Variety Approval Commission shall comprise the following:
    - President: The Director of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural
        Inputs and Products of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development;
    - Vice-President: the Director General of Agricultural Research for Development;
    - Secretary: the Sub-Director of Seed Regulation and Plant Quarantine of the Ministry in
        charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.




                                                                                                    Page | 38
    -    Members:
          • the Sub-Director of Seeds and Seedlings of the Department in charge of Agricultural
               Development;
          • the Sub-Director of Agricultural Extension of the Department in charge of Professional
               Agricultural Organisations and Support to Agricultural Holdings;
          • the Representative of the Institute of Agricultural Research for Development (IRAD);
          • the Representative of private sector breeders for the species, or group of species
               concerned;
          • the applicant to be registered in the official catalogue or their representative;
          • the President of the Seed Trade Association (ACOSEC);
          • the Representative of producers of the species, or group concerned.
             (2) The President of the Species and Variety Approval Commission shall request any other person
with expertise in the relevant species to take part in the work of the CHEV as a consultant.
             (3) The members of the CHEV shall be appointed by the administrations and socio-professional
bodies to which they belong.
             (4) Representatives of public or private breeders and producers shall only be invited to the CHEV
session if the species studied or the subject on the agenda falls within their field of competence.

Article 4: The members of the CHEV are appointed by decision of the Minister of Agriculture and
Rural Development.
Article 5: The secretariat of the CHEV shall be the responsibility of the Secretary of CONSOV.
Article 6: (1) The CHEV shall meet as convened by its President or at the request of the President
of CONSOV.
           (2) The invitations to which the required working documents specifying the date, place
and agenda of the meeting are attached, shall be sent to the members of the Commission at least
seven (7) days before the date of the meeting.
           (3) Opinions and recommendations issued by the committee shall be adopted by a simple
majority of the members present. In the event of a tie vote, the vote of the President shall have the
casting vote.
           (4) At the end of each session of the Commission, a report shall be sent to the President
of CONSOV.
       CHAPTER II: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 7: The services of the President and the members of the CHEV are free of charge. However,
travel and subsistence expenses incurred for the sessions shall be covered by the CONSOV.
Article 8: The term of office of the President and the members of the CHEV shall be three years,
renewable.
Article 9: Operating expenses of the CHEV are borne by the budget of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture and Rural Development.
Article 10: The President of CONSOV is responsible for the execution of this Order, which shall be
registered and published according to the procedure of urgency, and then inserted in the Official
Gazette in English and French.
                                                              Yaounde the 12 September 2012
                                                The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                  Lazare ESSIMI MENYE
