   2.3.3.    Order No. 0072/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 22 April 2019 to lay down
       procedures for the approval of species and varieties.

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/153 of 4 May 2005 on the creation, organisation and functioning of the
National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/001 of 4 January 2019 to appoint a Prime Minister, Head of Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/002 of 4 January 2019 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Joint Order No. 380/MINADER/MINCOMMERCE of 7 August 2006 to lay down the
specifications for seed production, import and marketing;
Mindful of Joint Order No. 381/MINADER/MINCOMMERCE of 7 August 2006 to lay down the general
standards for chemical treatment, storage, packaging and labelling of seeds, and considering the
service needs,
                                 HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
        CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: This Order sets out the procedures for the approval of plant species and varieties.
Article 2: For the purpose of this Order, the following definitions shall be accepted:
    - Seeds: all or part of a plant that allows for its multiplication or reproduction, including: seed,
        cuttings, seedlings, shoot, tuber, bulb, spore, vitro-plant.
    - Approval: process whereby the competent authority approves the import, distribution and
        use of a new variety based on a comprehensive scientific data showing that the variety is
        effective for its intended uses and does not present a risk on human, animal, or
        environmental health.
    - DUS: Distinctness-Uniformity-Stability
    - VAT: Agronomic and Technological Value.
    - Request for DUS and VAT testing: procedure carried out to include a new plant variety in
        the list of candidate species and varieties for DUS and VAT testing.
    - Administrative costs: costs relating to the introduction of any request to examine plant
        species and varieties candidates for DUS and VAT testing.
    - DUS and VAT tests fees: fees incurred for the payment and performance of DUS and VAT
        tests conducted in accordance with the regulations in force. For hybrid varieties, DUS fees
        are applied to the hybrid and to each of its components.
    - Experiments: Date and place from which the trials necessary for the DUS and VAT tests
        are set up.




                                                                                            Page | 40
     CHAPTER II: APPROVAL PROCEDURE
Article 3: Any new variety shall be subject to the approval procedure prior to its registration in the
official catalogue of plant species and varieties.
Article 4: Varieties eligible for approval must meet the following requirements:
     - Bear a name that cannot be confused with those of already existing varieties;
     - Be recognised as distinct, uniform and stable (based on the DUS test results);
     - Have a relevant agronomic and technological value (based on the VAT test results).
Article 14: These charges shall be set in line with Joint Order MINADER/MINFI.
Article 15:
     - Pro forma invoices corresponding to the administrative costs and the test costs shall be
          sent to the applicant, specifying the deadline for payment from the issue date of the
          invoice.
     - Proof of payment of the fees shall be required to initiate any further procedure.
    CHAPTER IV: FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 16: The provisions of this Order shall repeal all other prior contrary provisions.
Article 17: The Secretary General of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture shall be responsible for
the implementation of this Order, which shall be registered and published according to the procedure
of urgency, and then inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French.
                                                                               Yaounde, 22 April 2019
                                              The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                  Gabriel MBAIROBE

Page | 43
APPENDIX

OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, QUALITY CONTROL AND
CERTIFICATION OF SEEDS OF SOME LEGUME S (GROUNDNUT, SOYBEAN, BEAN AND
COWPEA)
SCOPE
The production of certified cowpea seeds (Vigna unguiculata), groundnut (Arachis hypogea),
soybean (Glycine max.) and beans (Phaseolus vulgaris) based on the of the conservation
(genealogical) selection of the variety (of each species), registered in the Official Catalogue of
Species and Varieties, over successive generations, and on maintaining the seed in good physical,
physiological and health conditions.
ORGANISATION OF THE PRODUCTION
Production system: The production system is based on the principle of the breeding line, from a
starting genetic material corresponding to that approved and registered in the Official Catalogue of
Species and Varieties. The multiplication of the pre-basic seeds is under the responsibility of the
breeder or the maintainer and coordinated by the Department in charge of regulations, quality control
and certification of the seeds and seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Starting genetic material (line): these are plants selected by the breeder or maintainer, which
enable the variety’s continuous conservation (genealogical) breeding each year. These plants are
called G0 (generation 0).
Pre-basic seed: the pre-basic seeds or G1, G2 and G3 refer to successive generations resulting
from the multiplication of the starting genetic material:
    - the seeds harvested from the starting genetic material, form the first generation known as
        G1 (generation 1);
    - the product obtained from the sowing of the first generation forms the second generation;
        that is G2 (generation 2) ;
    - the product obtained from the sowing of the second generation forms the third generation;
        known as G3 (generation 3).
The production of G1, G2, and G3 pre-basic seeds is placed under the responsibility of the breeder or
maintainer or the duly authorized party and under the official supervision of the Department in charge of
regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture .

Basic seeds: the product obtained from the sowing of the third generation, forms the fourth
generation G4 (generation 4), which are the basic seeds. The basic seeds always refer to only one
generation. At the breeder’s request and after the approval of the Department in charge of
regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and seedlings at the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture and Rural Development, the G3 generation can be certified as basic seed.
Certified seeds: Certified or R1 seed is the first generation of basic seed propagation. Certified
seeds can have two generations, that is R1 certified seeds, R2 certified seeds.
Production requirements
Genus
Sowing: a genus is a set of all the plants sown from one or more pods of a single plant.
Previous cropping: the propagation farm must not have grown legumes or oilseeds in the previous
season.

                                                                                                    Page | 44
Sowing: the product of each seedling is sown in rows according to the plant genus.
Isolation: the G0 and G1 production field can be surrounded by a production of G1, G2, G3 or G4
with 1m between them or be separated from a crop of the same species by a minimum distance of
5m, for the same variety and 30m, for a different variety, or planted in a field of different species.
Purification:
   - varietal: any aberrant or doubtful genus will be eliminated upon identification. If the plant has
        already blossomed, the doubtful genus must be removed, as well as at least one row on
        each side.
   - Health: any plant affected by disease shall be uprooted. The doubtful genus must be carefully
        removed in order to avoid contaminating the remaining healthy plants.
Harvesting: the selected genera are harvested for a dual purpose:
   - the most representative plants of the variety are harvested separately to serve as G0 for the
      following year, to make up the security stock and to continue the pedigree selection;
   - all other genera (plants) can be harvested either separately or together to constitute the G1;
   - security stock: security stocks are of two kinds: in plants (or pods from the same plant); a
      number of the most representative self-pollinated plants are kept (plant/pods G0) and in
      seeds; half of the seeds obtained from the separate harvesting of rows are kept (seeds G0),
      the other half is used to continue the pedigree selection.
Basic seed
Sowing: the production of G2 is carried out in a single field. The production of G3 and basic seed
may be carried out in several fields provided each field has a minimum area of 0.5 ha. In a case
where specialised farmer- seed growers are contracted to produce G2 and G3 (pre-basic seed) and
basic seed (G4), the fields must be sufficiently close to the specialised seedling company to enable
normal monitoring of production.
Isolation: the plots sown with G1 and earmarked for the production of G2, and those sown with G2
to produce G3, can be surrounded respectively, by a production of G1, G2, G3 or G4 with a
separation distance of 1 m, or be separated from the crop by a minimum distance of 10 m with the
same variety and from 10 to 20 m with a different variety
Certified seeds
Conditions for multiplication: certified seed producing companies can multiply only one variety of
the same species for the same farmer- seed grower. Each propagation farm cannot be less than one
hectare. A company cannot submit less than 5 ha per variety to control.
Isolation: the certified seed production plot is separated from any crop of the same species by a
minimum distance of 1 m for the same variety and 5 m for a different variety.
CULTIVATION RULES
During plant controls and harvest, the cultivation of soy, groundnut and cowpea, must follow these rules:

    -   Plot identification: Propagation farms shall be marked from the onset by a signpost or some other
        notice to identify the plot, crop, variety, and possibly the category. The crops must be sown in rows at
        least 40 cm apart. Each plot must be sown with a single lot of parent seed.

    -   Preceding crop: Leguminous crops or oleaginous plants should not have been cultivated in the plot
        intended for seed cultivation, during the previous season.



Page | 45
    -   Minimum area: The area of a seed farm must not be less than 0.5 ha for basic seed and 1
        ha for certified seed.
    -   Isolation: The isolation of the plot from any other crop of the same species must comply with
        the requirements for the category of the seed to be produced. Non-conformity is grounds for
        rejection.
    -   Crop Condition: The production farms must be well maintained: that is, density, absence of
        weeds in the production plots and around the plots, enabling the scoring and control of
        varietal identity and purity. The poor state of a field can be grounds for rejection
    -   Species purity: The presence of other species in the propagation plot, whose grains cannot
        be eliminated under normal sorting conditions, is grounds for rejection.
    -   Varietal purity: An impurity is considered to be any plant significantly different from the
        varietal type or an aberrant plant. In this context, the varietal impurities considered may be
        plants of a different variety, plants showing non-conformities with one or more characteristics
        considered important in the definition of the variety or aberrant plants. Soya and groundnut
        fields must not contain a rate of impurities higher than 1% for the production of pre-basic and
        basic seed, and 2% for the production of certified seed. Beans and cowpeas must not contain
        a rate of impurities higher than 3% for the production of pre-basic and basic seed and 5%
        for the production of certified seed.
    -   Diseases: The inspection should focus on harmful cryptogamic viral and bacterial diseases.
        The importance of the presence of these diseases which reduce the utility value of seeds
        can be grounds for rejection of the crops. The proportion of plants infected by these diseases
        must be less than 0.1% and 0.5% for basic and certified seed farms respectively.
    - Hazardous Weeds: Propagation farms must be free of weeds. The tolerance threshold for
      the presence of weed is 0.05% for pre-basic/basic seed farms and 0.1% for certified seed
      farms.
INSPECTION OF SEED FARMS
Prerequisites
The inspection must be carried out in the presence of the seed grower or a representative. The seed
inspector/controller must be provided with satisfactory evidence of the grade and origin of the parent
seed. The seed inspector / controller shall also be informed of the crop grown during the previous
season.
Number of Inspections and Crop Grading
Number of inspections: The propagation farms must be inspected at least twice for certified seed
and three times for basic seed:
    -   the first inspection shall be carried out before blossoming occurs, to check for isolation,
        absence of new sprouts;
    - the second, during blossoming, to check for impurities and the condition of the crop; and
    - the third shall be conducted at full maturity, to re-check the purity and health of the crop.
Further inspections shall be required during harvest and, if possible, upon delivery of raw seed.




                                                                                            Page | 46
Crop Grading: The compliance of the crops with the tolerance threshold set by this regulation shall
be assessed by counting, following the modalities specified on Inspection forms by the Department
in charge of regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and seedling of the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.

The approval or the partial or total downgrading of the crop shall be based on the plot sampling
method in homogeneous counting units for the characteristic under consideration.
After each inspection, the Seed Inspector/Controller shall fill the Inspection Form, that will support
the proposal to reject or accept the propagation field, or recommend some corrective measures
before a final decision is taken.

The Inspection Form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the seed grower or a
representative. In case the latter refuses to sign, this shall be mentioned on the refusal Inspection
form and notified to the seed grower concerned within 15 days following the inspection.
SEED LOTS
A seed lot is a specified quantity of seed, physically identifiable, for which a test report may be issued.
Sampling:
Samples taken upon delivery of raw seeds: An official sample, earmarked for prior control is taken
by the seed inspector/controller from a single harvest during the delivery of raw seeds and kept at
the disposal of the Department in charge of regulations, control and the certification of seeds and
seedlings at the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.
Sampling of lots for certification: In order to verify that the seed lots presented for certification
meet the pre-established standards, official seed samples for analysis shall be collected by the seed
inspector/controller. Two samples shall be taken from each lot and kept at the disposal of the
Department in charge of regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and seedlings of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development: one shall be destined for the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed analysis laboratory; the other for post control.
The minimum weight of a sample to be taken from a seed lot is 1000 g for laboratory tests and 1000
g for post control.
Lot size
Basic seed: A lot of basic seeds is a amount of seeds that is homogeneous in terms of identity,
specific purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size and whose weight does not exceed
ten (10) tonnes, unless an exemption is granted by the Department in charge of regulations, quality
control and certification of seeds and seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural
Development
Certified seeds: Certified seed lots shall not exceed twenty-five (25) tonnes; they shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, specific purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Mixing of varieties: The product of several basic and certified seed producing plots may be mixed,
provided that the different propagation plots have been sown with seeds of the same origin, and
graded by the same inspector/controller. In this case, the company or the seed producer must
declare the fields from which the product has been mixed, indicating the numbers transferred from
the crop declaration of each of these fields to the Department in charge of regulations, quality control
and certification of seeds and seedlings at the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural
Development. A new number shall definitely be assigned to the new lot.

Page | 47
CERTIFICATION RULES AND STANDARDS

Lots presented for certification must meet the requirements of these regulations and the following
acceptable standards:
                 Characteristics                          Basic seeds         Certified seeds
Minimum varietal purity:
Soybean, groundnut                                                    99 %                  98 %
Beans and cowpeas                                                     97 %                  95 %
Minimum species purity:
Soybean, Groundnut,                                                   99 %                  98 %
Beans and cowpeas                                                      98%                  97 %
Minimum germination capacity                                          70 %                  70 %
Maximum humidity                                                      12 %                  12 %
Minimum number of other plant species seeds in a 1,000
g sample                                                                 4                      10

Maximum weed seed content                                           0.0 5%                  0.1 %
Maximum content of inert materials (stones, earth,
straw...)                                                              2%                     3%

LEGUME SEED MARKETING CONTROL
Lots of locally produced or imported seeds put on the market for sale shall be inspected and if
necessary, samples shall be taken by seed inspectors/controllers for analysis at the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed analysis laboratory, to ensure that they meet the
standards and rules set by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.




                                                                                       Page | 48
   2.4.2.      Decision No. 00654/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 7 September 2006 to
       approve and make enforceable the official technical regulations for the production,
       quality control and certification of seeds and grafted citrus fruit seedlings (Pessea
       americana Mill)

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/153 of 4 May 2005 on the creation, organisation and functioning of the
National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree. No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/002 of 4 January 2019 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing;

session of 25 September 2020 (9th session)

Article 1: The Official Technical Regulations for the production, quality control and certification of
citrus fruits seeds and seedlings (Citrus spp) attached in Appendices I, II and III are as from the date

Article 2: (1) At the beginning of each farming season, declarations of citrus seed and seedling

       (2) These declarations shall be prepared by seed producing companies, as well as
nurserymen producing seeds and citrus seedlings to be controlled and certified prior to their sale on
the national territory.
       (3) The department in charge of regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and
seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture shall be informed about any changes made to the
declaration of crop production, no later than one month after production.
Article 3: The Department of Regulation and Quality Control of Agricultural Inputs and Products
shall be responsible for the execution of this decision, which shall be published in English and
French, wherever necessary.
                                                                        Yaounde, 7 September 2006
                                              The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                   Clobert TCHATAT

                                                                                           Page | 50
APPENDIX: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, CONTROL AND
MARKETING OF BANANA SEEDLINGS (MUSA SPP).
Scope
The production of plant materials for banana is achieved through vegetative propagation of clones
developed by the breeder after several selections. This vegetative propagation method ensures the
maintenance and conservation of the genetic and morphological features of the variety. During all
these propagations, focus is mostly on the health conditions of the plant material.
Organization of production
Production system: The production of plantain seedlings should be based on pedigree selection
according to the following:
     - The starting genetic material for plant propagation are healthy seedlings and the resulting
         seedlings. Together they form the B0 family (parent seed or starting genetic material);
     - The successive descendants of each B0 constitute 1st generation (B1), 2nd generation (B2),
         3rd generation (B3) up to B6 families, respectively.
Production requirements
Starting genetic material: the absence of parasites on each B0 plant (nematode and weevils) and
any other particular disease, notably (fusariosis, virosis) shall be verified at the National Laboratory
for Seed Analysis or in any other approved laboratory using an appropriate method, following a
protocol proposed by the breeder or the maintainer and approved by the Department in charge of
regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and seedlings at the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture.
Breeding material: B0 or B1 material shall be grown in a research station or by a private individual
with the required experience, all of them having the necessary equipment to carry out the disease
detection tests. It shall be placed under the responsibility of the breeder or maintainer and subject to
verification by the Department in charge of regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and
seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
B2 to B6 material shall be grown by the seedling company in its geographical area. It shall be subject
to various tests conducted at the National Laboratory for Seed Analysis or in any other approved
laboratory, on samples taken by the seed inspectors/controllers.
Families are individualised up to B1. They may remain so thereafter or be mixed in B2 when they
are produced in the same plot. From B2 onwards, families of the same age, if they remain individual
species, will successively constitute the selection material (pre-basic) of the 2nd generation B2, the
3rd generation B3, etc. The B0 and B1 families lose their individual state when they are mixed. The
B2 to B6 material must comply with the disease standards for the 'Elite' class following three growing
inspections.
The pre-basic seedling is harvested from B0 to B2 material. The health of B0 starting seed, as well
as B1 and B2 breeding material, is controlled by seed inspectors/controllers of the Department in
charge of regulations, quality control and certification of seeds and seedlings of the Ministry in charge
of Agriculture.
Basic seedlings:
     - Class “SE'' (Super Elite): harvested from B3 to B6 material;
     - Class ''E'' (Elite): harvested from a single generation of Super Elite seedlings.

Certified seedlings: Class ''A'': harvested directly from basic seedlings (Class SE or E).
Cultivation rules
During the inspections of seed farms and propagation nurseries, banana seedlings must comply with
the following rules:


Page | 51
Plot identification
Seed farms or propagation nurseries shall be marked at the outset with a signpost or some other
indication to identify the plot, crop, variety and category.
Site Preparation: The plot intended for seedling production must not have grown a banana crop for
at least 3 years, or must be treated to avoid specific diseases.
Health condition: The inspection should focus on diseases and pests of bulbs, roots and leaves
such as nematodes and weevils and fusarium that spread with the use of infected plant materials. In
any case, any farm or nursery with very high severity and incidence of these diseases and pests
during growth should be rejected. To this end, the proportion of infected seedlings, cannot be greater
than zero (0) for the starting seedlings and 0.5% for the pre-basic seedlings.
Minimum area: The minimum area of a seed farm presented for inspection cannot be less than 0.5
hectares. The area must be larger for the production of plant material ready to be distributed to
farmers.
Isolation: Isolation is not a problem for plantain, with the plant propagation method.
However:
     - seed farms of pre-basic (B0 to B2) and basic (B3 to B6) categories with various varieties
         must be separated from each other by a minimum distance of 5 m. The same distance is to
         be applied between the purified variety intended for the production of seedlings and the same
         variety intended for the production of bunches;
     - seed farms of the basic and certified seed categories bearing various varieties must be
         separated from each other by a minimum distance of 3 m to avoid mixing seedlings at
         harvest. The same distance must be applied between the purified variety intended for the
         production of seedlings and the same variety intended for the production of bunches.
In the nursery, beds bearing the same variety or various varieties must be separated from each other
by a minimum distance of 1m.
The homogeneity of the varieties and the sizes of the seedlings.
The seed farms or nursery beds must have seedlings of the same variety and the same size at the
time of the planting.
Purification
Purification is obligatory, in seed farms and nurseries, from the beginning of planting until the
seedlings are harvested. It consists in uprooting the roots affected by nematodes, weevils,
cercosporiose and fusariose, virosis as soon as the symptoms appear. They must be completely
eliminated from the field.
Seed farms must be well maintained, especially during the first 4 months of seedling growth.
In the propagation nursery of mini-seedlings, it is necessary to weed sick seedlings, as well as those
which do not develop well.
Crop Condition: It must allow for proper inspection. The bad state of a woodlot or a nursery,
especially the presence of weeds and diseases or pests, can result in refusal or downgrading.
Conservation of seedlings
Methods of conservation
After treatments with approved pesticides, the seedlings are packaged in 17 cm x 24 cm x 40 µm
polyethylene bags or other local materials. They must guarantee the viability of the seedlings and
limit the losses resulting from dehydration (caused by the direct exposure of plant materials to
sunlight) or excess humidity causing the buds to break.
Seedlings put in the bags or pots must be placed in a trench in vertical position under a well-
ventilated shelter.
Seedlings from wood lots intended directly for planting can be transported in bulk after treatment and
sun exposure.

                                                                                           Page | 52
Storage life
Under storage conditions as described above, naked seedlings may be stored for up to 2 weeks.
Nurseries
Inspections must be carried out in the presence of growers or their representatives. Seed
inspectors/controllers shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the grade and origin of the
seeds used. They shall also be informed about the crops grown during the previous three seasons.
Number of Inspections and Crop Grading
Crops in wood lots shall be inspected at least three times for basic (Super Elite class) and at least
twice for basic (Elite class) and certified (Class A) seeds:
     - the first inspection shall be carried out after planting, to to check compliance with the isolation
         rules, the condition of the plot (cleanliness of the farms) and the seedlings (density, health
         status);
     - the second inspection of the wood lots shall be carried out within 2 to 3 months after planting
         in order to identify off-types and infected seedlings to be purified and/or destroyed;
     - the third shall be conducted just before or during the harvesting of seedlings to help
         determine the right time of harvesting and to ensure the quality of harvested seedlings. In
         this case, samples shall be taken for laboratory testing.
In all cases, the purpose of the final inspection is to ensure appropriate decisions at harvest time
and to ensure the good quality of harvested seedlings. Seedlings put in the bags or pots must not
contain those belonging to a different variety or infected seedlings.
Crops grown in a nursery for the production of mini-seedlings shall be subjected to at least one
control. This control shall be carried out before the digging of the seedlings, that is, after 2 to 3 weeks
in the nursery, to check the farming and health condition of the seedlings, as well as their varietla
purity.
During the last inspection, the seed controller shall note the number of seedlings likely to be put on
sale.
Parasites, diseases and foreign or non- compliance to variety shall be assessed by counting.
Crop Grading
The compliance of crops with the tolerance threshold set by this regulation shall be assessed by
counting, following conditions specified in Inspection forms by the Department in charge of the
regulation, control, quality and certification of seeds and seedlings of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture and Rural Development.
The approval or the partial or total downgrading of a wood lot or nursery, for a given characteristic,
is based on the plot sampling method in countable homogeneous units,
After each inspection, the Seed Inspector/controller shall complete the Inspection Form, based on
which he/she will propose the rejection or acceptance of the wood lot or propagation nursery, or
recommend some corrective measures before the final decision..
The Inspection Form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the grower or a
representative. Any refusal by the grower to sign shall be mentioned thereon. The total or partial
rejection of a wood lot or propagation nursery shall be noted on the Inspection Form and notified to
the grower within 15 days following the inspection.




Page | 53
Certification standards
The plant material presented for certification shall meet the requirements of these technical
regulations and the following standards during the harvest period.
 Description (Maximum content)                             Basic        seedlings Certified seedlings
                                                           (Class SE and E)             (Class A)
 Off-types                                                                    1%                    2%
 Injured seedlings                                                           10%                  15%
 Drying of the seedlings                                                    0.5%                    1%
 Deformed germinated injured mini-seedlings                                   1%                    5%
 Diseases and parasites:
 - Nematodes                                                                  1%                    2%
 - Weevils                                                                    1%                    2%
 - Cercosporiosis                                                             2%                    5%
 - Fusarium                                                                   1%                    2%
 Homogeneity:
 - Variety                                                                  0.5%                    1%
 - Calibre                                                                    5%                  10%
Marketing control
Locally produced or imported seedlings put on sale shall be inspected and, if necessary, samples
shall be taken by seed inspectors/controllers for analysis at the National Seed Testing Laboratory or
any other approved seed testing laboratory, to ensure their compliance with the standards and rules
defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.
