Page | 55
APPENDIX I: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, CONTROL AND
CERTIFICATION OF COMPOSITE MAIZE SEED (ZEA MAYS)
Scope of Application
The production of certified seeds of composite maize varieties is based on the maintenance of the
result of the combinations made by the breeder between lines, cultivars (varieties), in order to obtain
a composite, as described during its registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties,
and to maintain good physical, physiological and health conditions.
Production Organisation
Production System: This involves the multiplication of approved composite that are registered in
the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties. The propagation scheme for pre-basic seeds from
starting material G0 shall be under the responsibility of the plant breeder or maintainer and subject
to official verification by the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds
and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Basic seeds shall correspond to the generation of pre-basic seed proposed in the conservation
breeding protocol by the plant breeder or maintainer from the starting material G0 and set by the
Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Certified seeds shall be derived directly from basic seeds.
Production Requirements
Origin of Pre-basic and Basic Seeds: the origin of such seeds shall be proven on presentation of
invoices or certificates of origin.
Previous cropping: composite varieties of maize for the production of basic seeds shall only be
propagated on fallow plots or those cultivated during the previous season with species belonging to
other families (pulses, solanaceous plants, tuber crops, etc.)
Maize should not have been sown on the certified seed propagation farm in the previous year, unless
it is the same variety, the same generation or a previous generation that has successfully met the
standards for seed propagation.
Isolation: an isolation distance of at least 300 metres for basic seeds and 200 metres for certified
seeds shall be observed from farms of other varieties or farms of the same variety that do not comply
with varietal purity standards.
To reduce the risk of sexual pollution in the farming environment, it is recommended to propagate
only one variety in a growing area.
Purification
In-growth purification: all aberrant and off-type plants shall be removed before pollination.
Post-harvest cob sorting: cob sorting shall be carried out before drying. Aberrant or doubtful cobs
of maize and those in poor health shall be removed.
Among the cobs inspected, there shall be no more than 0.5% of off-types or more than 1% of cobs
with seeds of different colours for basic seeds; more than 1% and 2% of the same impurities for
certified seeds.
Cultivation Rules
During in-growth inspections, composite maize seed crops shall comply with the following rules:
    -   Plot Identification: propagation farms shall be marked from the outset with a sign or other indications
        to identify the plot.
    -   Minimum Area: the area of a propagation plot shall be no less than 0.5 ha, or at least 1 ha for basic
        and certified seeds, respectively.
    -   Crop Condition: propagation farms shall be well maintained from a planting point of view: appropriate
        density, absence of weeds in the propagation plots and in their immediate vicinity, thus contributing
        to the grading and control of purification. Poor crop condition may be a cause for rejection.


                                                                                                   Page | 56
    -   Varietal Purity: impurities shall be removed as soon as possible. An impurity is any plant
        differing in at least one characteristic from the inspected variety.
A farm which, during any inspection, shows more than 2% of off-types or doubtful plants having
released pollen when more than 5% of the plant in the farm have receptive silks, shall be rejected.
    - Health Condition: the inspection shall focus on seed-borne cryptogamic and viral diseases.
A farm with more than 0.1% of plants infected with these fungi and viruses shall be rejected.
    - Hazardous Weeds: seed propagation farms shall be free of all hazardous weeds.
        Inspection of Seed farms
Prior Arrangements: inspection shall be carried out in the presence of the seed grower or a
representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the
category and origin of the seeds used. He/she shall also take note of the crop grown in the previous
season.
Number of Inspections: propagation farms shall be inspected at least twice for certified seeds and
three times for basic seeds:
    - The first inspection before flowering shall be carried out to check the isolation, the previous
        cropping and the absence of regrowth;
    - The second one, during flowering, shall be carried out to check the crop condition, purification
        and other unfavourable factors;
    - The third one shall precede the harvest to identify diseased and off-type cobs.
Further inspections shall be required at harvest time and if possible, on delivery of raw seeds.
Crop grading: the compliance of crops with the standards laid down by these Regulations shall be
graded by counting, in line with the procedures specified in the inspection forms prepared by the
Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.
The approval or the partial or total downgrading of the crop, for a given characteristic, shall be based
on the plot sampling method in countable homogeneous units,
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form, based on
which he/she will propose the rejection or approval of the propagation farm, or recommend some
remedial measures before the final decision.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the seed grower or a
representative. Any refusal by the grower to sign shall be mentioned thereon. The rejection of a propagation
farm shall be recorded in the inspection form and notified to the seed grower concerned within 15 days of the
inspection.
Seed Lots: Sample Collection
Sample collection on delivery of raw seeds: a sample for prior control, shall be taken from the
cobs and placed at the disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification
of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.
Sample collection for certification: in order to check compliance of the seed lots submitted for certification
with defined standards, official seed samples for analysis shall be taken by seed inspectors/controllers. These
samples shall be taken in duplicate from each lot and placed at the disposal of the Department in
charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture and Rural Development: one sample for the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any
other approved seed testing laboratory, and the other for post-control.
The minimum weight of a sample to be collected from a seed lot shall be 1,000 g for laboratory tests
and 1,000 g for post-tests.




Page | 57
Lot Size
Basic Seed: a basic seed lot is a maximum quantity of ten (10) tonnes of homogeneous seeds in
terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Basic seed lots may be produced by mixing the products of several plots in some proportions,
provided that the plots have been sown with basic seeds of the same origin. The seed grower shall
declare to the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings
of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development, the plots whose product are intended
to be mixed, specifying the numbers on the crop declaration for each plot. A new lot number shall
then be assigned to the mixed product.
Certified Seeds: certified seed lots shall not weight more twenty (20) tonnes. They shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Certification Rules and Standards
Lots submitted for certification shall meet the requirements of these Regulations, and the following
standards or acceptance rules:
Description                                                       Basic Seed       Certified Seeds
Varietal purity (minimum)                                                   98%                 98%
Minimum species purity                                                      98%                 98%
Minimum germination capacity                                                90%                 80%
Moisture in woven bags (maximum)                                            12%                 12%
Moisture in hermetic vacuum packaging (maximum)                               8%                 8%
Maximum seed content of other crop species                                  0%                 0.1%
Maximum weed seed content                                                   0%                   0%
Maximum content of inert materials (stones, earth, straw,
                                                                            2%                   2%
etc.)


Seed Marketing Control
Lots of locally produced or imported seeds for sale shall be inspected and, if necessary, samples
shall be taken by seed inspectors/controllers for analysis at the National Seed Testing Laboratory or
any other approved seed testing laboratory, in order to ensure that they comply with the standards
and rules defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.
APPENDIX II: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, CONTROL AND
CERTIFICATION OF HYBRID MAIZE SEED (Zea mays)
Scope of Application
The production of hybrid maize seeds shall be based on the maintenance of lines, the production of
hybrid combinations declared at the time of registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and
Varieties, and good physical, physiological and health conditions.
Production Organisation
Production System
Basic Seeds: this shall be pure lines or single cross hybrids resulting from the cross breeding of
pure lines.
Certified Seeds: certified seeds shall be derived directly from basic seeds. These may be single,
three-way hybrids, double cross hybrids or, where appropriate, any other hybrid registered in the
Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties. There shall be only one generation of certified seeds.
                                                                                          Page | 58
Production Conditions
Basic Seeds
Line
Isolation: a line propagation shall be carried out in an isolated plot. The distance between the plot
and any other maize farm of a different variety shall be at least 300 m.
Purification: all aberrant plants shall be removed as soon as they are detected, before pollination.
Purification shall continue after harvest. Aberrant or doubtful cobs and grains, as well as those with
poor health conditions, shall be eliminated.
Single Hybrid
Sowing: it shall be carried out in line with the instructions given by the plant breeder, during the
registration of the variety in the Official Catalogue. The rows of the male parent shall be marked. The
plot shall be bordered as from 2 m by at least two rows from the male parent.
Isolation: the plot meant for the production of single hybrid basic seeds shall be isolated from any
maize crop by at least 300 m, unless that crop is the same hybrid or a hybrid using the same male
parent.
In-growth purification: in both the male and female parents, “aberrant” plants and regrowth shall
be eliminated as soon as they are detected in any case, before pollination. Plants contaminated with
a pest that may reduce the seed value shall also be eliminated.
Castration: all panicles identified as female parents shall be eliminated before pollination. This
applies to both main stem and tiller panicles.
Harvesting: male and female parents shall be harvested separately following instructions given by
the basic seed-producing company. The male parent shall either be eliminated after flowering or
harvested separately from the female parent, shall be delivered for consumption.
Sorting of Cobs: it must be conducted before drying. If necessary, this is done before ginning.
Aberrant or doubtful cobs, are eliminated, as well as those in poor health conditions.
Certified Seeds
Sowing: it shall be carried out in line with instructions given by the plant breeder, at the time the variety is
registered in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties. The rows for the male parent shall be marked
and the plot shall be bordered as from 2 m by at least two rows of the male parent.
Isolation: the plot for the production of certified seeds shall be isolated by at least 200 m from any maize crop
of a different variety or from any undesirable source of pollen.
In-growth purification: in both the male and female parents, “aberrant” plants and regrowth shall be
eliminated as soon as they are be detected in any case, before pollination. Plants contaminated with a pest
that may reduce the value of the seed shall also be eliminated.
Castration: all panicles of the line or single hybrid selected as female parents shall be eliminated before
pollination. This applies to both main stem and tiller panicles.
Harvesting: male or female parents must be harvested separately. The male parents harvested first and
delivered for consumption. The seed shall be harvested from the female parent. In case the producer provides
storage, he/she shall take all necessary steps to ensure good preservation of cobs and to avoid mixing.

Sorting of Cobs: it must be carried out before drying, and if necessary, completed before ginning.
Aberrant or doubtful cobs, as well as those in poor health conditions, shall be eliminated.


Page | 59
Cultivation Rules
Maize seed crops shall comply with the following rules, during crop and harvest inspections:
Previous cropping: maize should not have been sown in the previous year, unless it is the same
variety, of the same generation or a previous generation that has itself met the standards for seed
propagation.
For basic and certified seeds, the areas of the farms shall not be less than 0.5 ha and 1 ha
respectively.
Isolation: the isolation of the plot from any other maize crop shall comply with the conditions laid
down for the category of seeds to be produced.
Pollination: farms for hybrid maize seed multiplication shall be sown so as to enable both the male
and female parents to grow in separate rows and avoid mixing. A proportion of male rows in the
entire farm shall also be maintained in accordance with the instructions given by the seed breeder,
at the time the variety is registered in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties.
Genetic Purity (Varietal): impurities and regrowth shall be eliminated as soon as they are detected.
Any plant that is obviously different from the variety in one or more traits in the description of the
parent plant or variety is considered an impurity.
In-growth
Basic Seed: line propagation: a maximum of 0.1% of impurities shall be tolerated;
Hybrids: A maximum of 0.1% of impurities shall be tolerated, in the male and female parents
respectively.
Certified Seeds: a maximum of 0.2% of impurities shall be tolerated, in the male and female parents
respectively.
Castration: male panicles of the line or hybrid selected as female parent shall be eliminated before
pollination.
A plant is considered to have produced pollen when it includes, before its silks wither, more than 10
flowers whose anthers are outside the glumes, counted on both stem and tiller panicles.
Basic Seeds: a maximum of 0.3% of pollinating plants in the female parent shall be tolerated during
any inspection, and a maximum of 1% at the end of all inspections.
Certified Seeds: a maximum of 0.5% of pollinating plants in the female parent shall be tolerated
during any inspection, and a maximum of 1% at the end of all inspections.
During Harvest: a maximum of 0.1% of aberrant cobs shall be tolerated after sorting.
Health Condition: the presence of a cryptogamic disease reducing the value of seeds may be a
reason for refusal when the acceptable infestation threshold is exceeded. A maximum of 0.1% of
infected plants shall be tolerated.
Inspection of Seed farms
Prior Arrangements: inspection shall be carried out in the presence of the seed grower or a
representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the
category and origin of the seed used. He/she will also take note of the crop grown in the previous
season.



                                                                                           Page | 60
Number of Crop Inspections and Grading
Basic Seeds: the basic seed propagation farm shall be inspected at least three times:
    -   The first inspection shall be carried out before flowering to check the isolation distance, the
        absence of regrowth, the crop condition, purification and respect for the proportion of male
        and female rows (for single hybrids);
    -   The second shall be carried out during flowering to check purification, and to ensure that
        castrations are carried out in time, particularly in the case of single hybrids;
    -   The third inspection shall be carried out before harvest to identify diseased and off-type cobs;
        this is the most appropriate time to observe varietal features.
Further inspections may be carried out during harvest and if possible, on delivery.
Certified Seeds: the certified seed propagation farm shall be inspected at least three times:
    -   The first inspection shall be carried out before flowering to check the isolation distance, the
        absence of regrowth, crop condition, purification and respect for the proportion of male and
        female rows;
    -   The second shall be carried out during flowering to check purification, and to ensure that
        castrations are carried out in time;
    -   The third shall be carried out before harvest to identify diseased and off-type cobs; this is the
        most appropriate time to observe varietal features.
Further inspections may be carried out during harvest or on delivery.
Crop Grading
The compliance of crops with the standards laid down by these Regulations shall be graded by
counting, in line with the procedures specified in the inspection forms prepared by the Department
in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in of
Agriculture and Rural Development.
The method of sampling the plot into countable homogeneous units, for the feature under
consideration, shall allow for the acceptance or partial or complete downgrading of the crop.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form based on
which he/she shall propose the rejection or acceptance of the propagation farm, or recommend some
remedial measures before the final decision.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the seed grower or a
representative. Any refusal by the grower to sign shall be mentioned thereon. The rejection of a
propagation farm shall be mentioned in the inspection form and notified to the seed grower
concerned within 15 days of the inspection.
Seed Lots
Sample Collection
Sample collection on Delivery: a sample, shall be collected from the cobs for prior control and
placed at the disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds
and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.




Page | 61
Sample collection for certification: seed samples for analysis shall be collected by the
inspector/controller from each lot submitted for certification, in order to check the conformity of the
seeds with certification standards. These samples shall be taken in duplicate and placed at the
disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development: one for the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed testing laboratory, and the other for post-control.
Lot Size
Basic Seeds: basic seed lots shall not exceed ten (10) tonnes in weight. They shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Basic seed lots may be produced by mixing the products of several plots in specific proportions,
provided that these plots have been sown with basic seeds of the same origin. The seed company
shall declare to the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development, the plots whose products
are mixed, specifying the numbers on the crop declaration for each plot. A new number shall be
assigned to the mixture.
Certified Seeds: certified seed lots shall not exceed twenty (20) tonnes in weight. They shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Certification Standards
Lots submitted for certification shall meet the requirements of these Regulations, and the following
standards or acceptance rules:
Description                                   Basic Seeds                       Certified Seeds
Minimum germination capacity                       90%                                90%
(Maximum) moisture in woven bags                    8%                                 8%
(Maximum) moisture in woven bags                   12%                                14%
                                                  0.1%                                0.2%
Varietal purity (% of maximum impurity
                                         (line propagation and                 (male and female
tolerated)
                                              single hybrid)                        parents)
Minimum species purity (% of the weight)           98%                                98%
Maximum seed content of other plant species                 0%                        0.1%


Seed Marketing Control
Lots of locally produced or imported seeds for sale shall be inspected and, if necessary, samples
shall be collected by seed inspectors/controllers for testing at the National Seed Testing Laboratory
or any other approved seed testing laboratory, in order to ensure their compliance with the standards
and rules defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.




                                                                                             Page | 62
APPENDIX III: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, CONTROL
AND CERTIFICATION OF RICE SEEDS (Oryza sativa)
Scope of Application
The production of certified rice seeds shall be based on cultivars (varieties), as described at the time
of its registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties, and on good physical,
physiological and health conditions.
Production Organisation
Production System: basic seed production shall be carried out in three (3) seasons, using pedigree,
based on the following scheme:
     - The panicles from the initial plants shall be called G0 and shall be sown according to lines;
     - The product obtained by threshing lines (after re-selecting the lines to form G0) constitutes
         the first generation known as G1;
     - The product obtained by sowing the first generation constitutes the second generation (G2);
     - The product obtained by sowing the second generation forms the third generation (G3) which
         constitutes the basic seed.
The classification as basic seeds of G3 shall be subject to the results recorded on the sample(s)
taken from G2 and the quantities of certified seeds required to be produced.
The production of pre-basic seeds G1 and G2 shall be placed under the responsibility of the plant
breeder or maintainer or the duly accredited delegate and under the official supervision of the
Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
In order to maintain the breeding of a variety, delegates shall obtain all the necessary propagating
material in the form of panicles from the plant breeder or a representative. For each new line, the
delegate shall request the plant breeder or a representative at least every three (3) years to supply
at least ten (10) reference panicles.
Production Requirements
Lines
Sowing: A line consists of all the plants harvested from the sowing of one or more panicles of a
single plant.
Previous cropping: rice should not have been sown on the propagation farm in the previous
season.
Sowing: the rows shall be sown in pedigree lines, with one panicle per row (panicle-row). The
number of lines sown by a basic seed-producing company depends on the tonnage it intends to
produce per variety. The minimum number of lines to be sown shall be 300 per variety.
Isolation: G0 and G1 production plots shall be isolated from any rice farm (regardless of variety) by
a minimum distance of five (5) m.
Purification:
     - Varietal: any aberrant or doubtful line shall be eliminated as soon as they are detected;
     - Health: any disease-infected plant shall be eliminated. Wild rice, weeds and any disease-
         infected plants shall be eliminated as soon as they are detected and ultimately before
         heading and harvesting. The diseased plant shall be eliminated so as to avoid contaminating
         the remaining healthy plants.
Harvesting: the selected lines shall be harvested for two purposes:
The most representative self-pollinated plants of the variety shall be harvested separately to
establish the following year's G0, build up the security stock, as well as continue the pedigree
selection.
All other lines (plants) may be harvested either separately or together to form G1.


Page | 63
Plant family grouping: the plants harvested from the line shall be sown in panicle-rows, in as many
rows as necessary.
This nursery shall be carried out by each family (set of plants from the same panicle-row), with the
possibility of always returning to the initial panicle.
The family may be sown irrespective of the plant origin, or subdivided into subfamilies (subfamily:
set of panicle-rows resulting from the sowing of one or more panicles of a single plant). This sub-
division makes it easier to eliminate hybrid plants.
The minimum number of rows for all families shall always be 300 per variety.
The minimum number of families shall be 10, with no more than 100 panicle-rows in each family.
Security stock: security stocks shall be of two kinds. In panicles, a number of panicles (the most
representative of the initial panicle) identical to the one used to sow the families shall be kept in good
condition (panicles G0); and in seeds, half of the seeds obtained by separate harvesting of the lines
shall be maintained (seed G0), the other half shall be used for further pedigree selection.
Basic Seeds
Sowing: G1 shall be produced in a single farm. Production of G2 and basic seeds may be carried
out in several farms, provided that each farm has a minimum area of 0.5 ha. In case the production
of G1 and G2 (pre-basic seeds) and basic seeds are sub-contracted to specialised agri- growers.
farms shall be sufficiently close to the institution (research station, specialised pre-basic and basic
seed farm) to contribute to the smooth monitoring of production. For G1, G2 and basic seed
categories, farmers may normally multiply only one variety per farm.
Isolation: plots sown with G1 for the production of G2, as well as those sown with G2 to produce
G3, may be surrounded by a production of G1, G2, or G3 respectively, separated by 1 m, or 10 m
minimum from the crop, for the same variety, and 20 to 30 m for a different variety.
In-growth purification: all aberrant or doubtful plants, wild rice, weeds, and disease-infected plants
shall be eliminated as soon as they are detected and ultimately before heading and harvesting. The
diseased plant shall be eliminated so as to avoid contaminating the remaining healthy plants.
Certified Seeds
Multiplication Requirement: seed companies may only multiply one variety of the same species
with one seed grower.
Each propagation farm may not be less than 1 ha. Companies shall not submit less than 5 ha per
variety for control.
The seed grower shall only multiply the contracted variety and shall not multiply for several
companies.
Isolation: the certified seed production plot shall be separated from any crop of the same variety by
a minimum distance of 1 m and 5 m for a different variety.
In-growth purification: all aberrant or doubtful plants, wild rice, weeds, and disease-infected plants
shall be eliminated as soon as they are detected and ultimately before heading and harvesting. The
infected plant shall be eliminated to avoid contaminating the remaining healthy plants.
Cultivation Rules
During in-growth inspections and harvest, rice seed crops shall comply with the following rules:
Plot Identification: propagation farms shall be marked from the outset with a sign or other
indications to identify the plot, variety and generation.
Minimum Area: the area of a seed farm shall not be less than 0.5 ha for basic seeds, and 1 ha for
certified seeds.




                                                                                              Page | 64
Crop Condition: Production farms shall be well maintained as per farming conditions: appropriate
density, absence of weeds in production plots and around the vicinity of the plots, thus contributing
to the grading and control of purification. Poor crop conditions of a farm may be a reason for refusal.
Varietal Purity: impurities shall be eliminated as soon as possible. Any aberrant plant, or one that
is significantly different from the varietal type, shall be considered an impurity. The rice seed
production farm shall not have an impurity level higher than 1% for basic seed production, and 2%
for certified seed production.
Diseases: the inspection shall focus on harmful cryptogamic diseases (especially rice blast). The
extent to which the presence of this disease reduces the usefulness of the seed, may cause crops
to be rejected. The proportion of plants infected by this disease shall be less than 0.1% and 0.5%
for basic and certified seed farms respectively.
Hazardous weeds: propagation farms shall be free of wild rice and other weeds. The maximum
tolerance for the presence of wild or red rice plants is zero (0) for basic seed farms and 1/50 m² for
certified seed farms.
Inspection of Seed Farms
Prior Arrangements: the inspection shall be carried out in the presence of the seed grower or a
representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the
category and origin of the seed used. He/she shall also note the crop grown in the previous season.
Number of Crop Inspections and Grading
Number of Inspections: the propagation farm shall be inspected at least twice for certified seeds and thrice
for basic seeds:
    -   The first inspection shall be carried out before heading to check isolation, absence of regrowth and
        the origin of basic seeds;
    -   The second shall be carried out immediately after heading to check impurities and crop condition;
    -   The third shall be carried out at full maturity to check purity and health condition of crops.
Further inspections shall be carried out during harvest and if possible, on delivery of raw seeds.
Crop Grading
The compliance of crops with the standards laid down by these Regulations shall be graded by counting, in
accordance with the procedures specified in the inspection forms prepared by the Department in charge of
Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural
Development.
The method of sampling the plot into countable homogeneous units, for a given feature, shall determine the
acceptance, or partial or complete downgrading of the crop.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form based on which he/she
shall propose the rejection or acceptance of the propagation farm, or recommend some remedial measures
before the final decision.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the seed grower or a
representative. Any refusal by the grower to sign shall be mentioned thereon. The rejection of a propagation
farm shall be mentioned in the inspection form and notified to the seed grower concerned within 15 days of
the inspection.
Seed Lots
A seed lot shall be a specific, physically identifiable quantity of seeds for which a test report may be
issued.
Sample Collection
Sample collection on delivery of raw seeds: the seed inspector/controller shall take an official
sample, for prior control, from specific harvest on delivery of raw seeds. It shall be placed at the
disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.
Sample collection for certification: in order to check that seed lots submitted for certification meet
the defined standards, official seed samples for testing shall be taken by the seed
Page | 65
inspector/controller. These samples shall be taken in duplicate from each lot and placed at the
disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development: one for the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed testing laboratory, and the other for post-control.
The minimum weight of a sample to be collected from a seed lot shall be 1,000 g for laboratory tests
and 1,000 g for post-tests.
Lot Size
Basic Seeds: basic seed lots shall not exceed ten (10) tonnes in weight. They shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Certified seeds: certified seed lots shall not exceed twenty-five (25) tonnes in weight. They shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Mixing of varieties: mixing the products of several plots producing pre-basic or basic seeds shall
be prohibited.
The products of several plots producing certified seeds may be mixed, provided that the various
multiplication plots have been sown with seeds of the same origin and recorded by the same seed
inspector/controller. In this case, the company or producer shall indicate to the Department in charge
of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture and Rural Development, the farms with mixed products, specifying the numbers stated
on the crop declaration for each of these farms. A new number shall be permanently assigned to the
new lot.
Certification Rules and Standards
Lots submitted for certification shall meet the requirements of these technical regulations, and the
following rules and standards:
Description                                                  Basic Seeds           Certified Seeds
Minimum varietal purity (% of grains)                              99%                   98%
Minimum specific purity                                            98%                   98%
Minimum germination capacity                                       75%                   75%
Maximum moisture                                                   12%                   12%
Maximum content in number of seeds of other plant
                                                                     5                    10
Species in a 500 g sample
Maximum Weed Seed Content                                            0                 1/50 m²
Maximum content of inert materials (stones, earth,
                                                                    2%                    2%
straw, etc.)

Rice Seed marketing control
Lots of locally produced or imported seeds for sale shall be inspected and, if necessary, samples
taken by seed inspectors/controllers for testing at the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other
approved seed testing laboratory, in order to ensure their compliance with the standards and rules
defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.




                                                                                           Page | 66
APPENDIX IV: TECHNICAL AND OFFICIAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION,
CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF OPEN POLLINATED SORGHUM SEEDS (SORGUM
VULGARE)
Scope of Application
The production of certified open pollinated sorghum seeds shall be based on cultivars, as described
at the time of their registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties, and on good
physical, physiological and health conditions.
Production Organisation
Production System: the production system shall be based on pedigree from a starting material
complying with the one approved and registered in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties.
The propagation scheme for pre-basic seeds shall be placed under the responsibility of the plant
breeder or maintainer and subject to official verification by the Department in charge of Regulations,
Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Basic seeds shall correspond to the generation of pre-basic seeds proposed in the conservative
breeding protocol by the plant breeder or maintainer from the starting genetic material (G0) and
approved by the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Certified seeds shall be derived directly from basic seeds.
Production Requirement
Basic Seeds
Previous cropping: the multiplication of an open-pollinated variety of sorghum shall only be allowed
on fallow plots or those cultivated during the previous season, with species belonging to other
families: legumes, solanaceous plants, tuber crops, etc.
Isolation: an isolation distance of at least 200 metres shall be respected for farms of other varieties
or of the same variety not conforming to the varietal purity standards.
To reduce the risk of sexual pollution on the farm, it is recommended to multiply only one variety on
a farm.
In-growth purification: it consists in eliminating all weeds and tillers. All aberrant and off-type plants
shall be eliminated before flowering. Stunted, late maturing or pest-infected plants shall also be
eliminated.
Certified Seeds
Previous cropping: sorghum should not have been sown on certified seed propagation farms in
the previous year, unless it is the same variety, the same generation or a previous generation
complying with the standards for seed propagation.
Isolation: an isolation distance of at least 200 metres shall be respected for farms of other varieties
or for farms of the same variety not conforming to the varietal purity standards.
To reduce the risk of sexual pollution on the farm, it is recommended to multiply only one variety on
a farm.
In-growth purification: all aberrant and off-type plants shall be eliminated before flowering. Stunted,
late maturing or pest-infected plants shall also be eliminated.
Cultivation Rules
During in-growth inspections, sorghum seed crops shall comply with the following rules:
Plot Identification: propagation farms shall be marked from the outset with a sign or any other
indications to identify the plot, the crop and possibly, the variety.
Minimum Area: the area of a propagation plot may not be less than 0.5 ha and 1 ha for basic and
certified seeds, respectively.




Page | 67
Crop Condition: propagation farms shall be well maintained to suit farming conditions: appropriate
density, absence of weeds in the propagation plots and in the immediate surrounding of the plots,
thus contributing to the grading and control of purification. Poor crop conditions may be a cause for
rejection.
Varietal Purity: impurities shall be eliminated as soon as possible. Any plant differing in at least one
feature from the inspected variety shall be considered an impurity. For basic seeds, only one off-
type plant per 30 m² shall be tolerated. For certified seeds, only one off-type plant per 10 m² shall be
tolerated.
Inspection of Seed farms
Prior Arrangements: the inspection shall be carried out in the presence of the seed grower or a
representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the
category and origin of the seed used. He/she shall also note the crop grown in the previous season.
Number of Crop Inspections and Grading
Number of Inspections: the propagation farm shall be inspected at least twice for certified seeds
and thrice for basic seeds:
     - The first inspection shall be carried out before flowering to check isolation and the absence
        of regrowth;
     - The second shall be carried out during flowering to check crop conditions and purification.
     - The third shall be carried out before harvest to identify diseased and off-type panicles.
Further inspections shall be carried out during harvest and if possible, on delivery of raw seeds.
Crop Grading
The compliance of crops with the standards laid down by these Regulations shall be graded by
counting, in line with the procedures specified in the inspection forms prepared by the Department
in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge
of Agriculture and Rural Development.
The method of sampling the plot into countable homogeneous units, for a given feature, shall
determine the acceptance, or partial or complete downgrading of the crop.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form based on
which he/she shall propose the rejection or acceptance of the propagation farm, or recommend some
remedial measures before the final decision.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the seed grower or a
representative. Any refusal by the grower to sign shall be mentioned thereon. The rejection of a
propagation farm shall be recorded in the inspection form and notified to the seed grower concerned
within 15 days of the inspection.
Seed Lots
A seed lot shall be a specific, physically identifiable quantity of seeds for which test report may be
issued.
Sample Collection
Sample collection on delivery of raw seeds: the seed inspector/controller shall take an official
sample, for prior control, from individual harvests on delivery of raw seeds. It shall be placed at the
disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.




                                                                                            Page | 68
Sample collection for certification: in order to check that the seed lots submitted for certification
meet the defined standards, official seed samples for testing shall be taken by the seed
inspector/controller. These samples shall be taken in duplicate from each lot and placed at the
disposal of the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture: one for the National Seed Testing Laboratory or
any other approved seed testing laboratory, and the other for post-control.
The minimum weight of a sample to be collected from a seed lot shall be 1,000 g for laboratory tests
and 1,000 g for post-tests.
Lot size
Basic seeds: a basic seed lot is a quantity of homogeneous seeds in terms of identity, species
purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size, and which shall not exceed five (5) tonnes
in weight.
Basic seed lots may be produced by mixing the products of several plots in specific proportions,
provided that these plots have been sown with basic seeds of the same origin. The seed grower
shall declare to the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and
Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture, the plots whose product shall be mixed, specifying
the numbers on the crop declaration for each plot. A new lot number shall be assigned to the mixed
product.
Certified Seeds: certified seed lots shall not exceed twenty (10) tonnes in weight. They shall be
homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination capacity, moisture content and size.
Certification Rules and Standards
Lots submitted for certification shall meet the requirements of these Regulations, and the following
standards or acceptance rules:
Description                                                 Basic Seeds       Certified Seeds
Varietal purity (minimum)                                       98%                  98%
(Minimum) species purity                                        98%                  98%
(Minimum) germination capacity                                  80%                  80%
(Maximum) moisture in woven bags                                12%                  12%
(Maximum) water content in airtight packaging                  8%                    8%

Maximum seed content of other crop species                     5/kg                 0.1%

Maximum weed seed content                                      5/kg                 0.1%
Maximum content of inert materials (stones, soil,
                                                               2%                    2%
straw, etc.)

Marketing Control of Sorghum Seeds
Lots of locally produced or imported seeds for sale shall be inspected and, if necessary, samples
taken by seed inspectors/controllers for testing at the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other
approved seed testing laboratory, in order to ensure their compliance with the standards and rules
defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.

  2.4.5.    Decision No. 00541/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 7 September 2006 to
      approve the official technical regulations for the quality control of some vegetable
      seeds

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 on seed activities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2004/320 of 8 December 2004 to organise the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2004/322 of 8 December 2004 to form the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/153 of 4 May 2005 on the creation, organisation and functioning of the
National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties;
Mindful of Decree No 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing of seeds, especially under article 22,
                                   HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: The production, import, quality control and marketing of vegetable seeds under optional
certification, shall be organised in accordance with the provisions of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July
2001 on seed activities, its implementing instruments, and the technical regulations approved by this
Decision.
Article 2: (1) Optional certification shall be granted for some vegetable seeds.
            (2) However, official samples shall be taken by seed inspectors and controllers on import,
in packaging, splitting or repackaging units, in storage and sales warehouses for varietal and health
control, as well as for technological testing (species purity, germination capacity, etc.) in the
laboratory.
Article 3: The complete wording of the attached technical regulations on the quality control of some
vegetable seeds shall hereby be approved.
Article 4: The Director in charge of Regulations and Quality Control of Agricultural Inputs and
Products, in the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development, shall be responsible for
the execution of this Decision, which shall be registered and published in English and French
wherever necessary.

                                                                        Yaounde, 7 September 2006
                                             The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development,
                                                                                  Clobert TCHATAT




                                                                                           Page | 70
APPENDIXES: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE QUALITY CONTROL OF SOME
VEGETABLE SEEDS
Scope of Application
The production of vegetable seeds shall be based on cultivars, the use of hybrid combinations declared at the
time of registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties, and on good physical, physiological and
health conditions.
Definitions
Species under Optional Certification:
For the purpose of these official technical regulations, vegetables under optional certification scheme shall
refer to plants belonging to the species listed below:
Garlic: Allium sativum                                            Spinach basella: basela rubra
Amaranth: Amaranthus spp                                         Okra: Hibiscus esculentus
Aubergine: Solanum melongena                                     Lettuce: Lactuca sativa
Basil: Basilicum americanum                                      Melon: Cucumis melo
Beetroot: Beta vulgaris                                           Nightshade: solanum esculentum

Carrot: Dancus carota                                            Black nightshade: Solanum nigrum
Celery: Apium gravieolens                                        Turnip: Brassica rapa
Chicory:                                                         Onion: Allium cepa
Cauliflower: Brassica oleracea var.Botritis                      Guinea sorrel: hibiscus sabdarifa
Chinese cabbage                                                  Watermelon: Ctrilus lanatus
Head cabbage: Brassica oleracea                                  Parsley: Petroselinum crispum
Var capitata                                                     Pea:
Red cabbage: Brassica oleracea var.rubra DC                      Pepper: Capsicum annum
Chives: Allium fistolosum                                        Chilli pepper: capsicum frutescens
Cucumber/Gherkin: Cucumis sativus                                 Leek: Beta vulgaris
Courgette: Cucurbita pepo
Spinach: Spinacia oleracea
Indian spinach: Basela alba

Lots
Maximum lot weight: a seed lot shall be defined as a quantity of seeds with a maximum weight
depending on the species:
Seeds of standard size or larger than maize grain: 20 tonnes;
Seeds smaller than maize grain: 10 tonnes.
Homogeneity of lots: a lot shall be homogeneous in terms of identity, species purity, germination
capacity and moisture.
The seed company shall assign a number on each lot during packaging, splitting or repackaging, in
order to identify each lot.
Small Packages: for the purpose of these Official Technical Regulations, small packages shall refer
to packages containing seeds with a minimum net weight of:
    - 5 kg for legumes;
    - 500 g for onion, beetroot, turnip, watermelon, squash, courgette, carrot, radish, spinach;
    - 100 g for all other species of vegetables listed in Paragraph 2.1 above.


Page | 71
Eligibility Requirements
Only seeds of varieties registered in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties may be imported,
produced, packaged, split, repackaged and marketed.
For self-pollinated species, the characteristics of lots of a packaged, split, repackaged and marketed
variety shall comply with those of the samples submitted for registration in the Official Catalogue of
Species and Varieties. Reference standards shall be placed under the responsibility of the
Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
For cross-pollinated species, the characteristics of lots of a packaged, split, repackaged and
marketed variety shall be identical, homogenous and remain stable from year to year. These
characteristics shall be those identified on the samples submitted for registration in the Official
Catalogue of Species and Varieties. Reference standards shall be placed under the responsibility of
the Department in charge of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
For hybrid species, the traits of the parents and lots of bonded, split, repackaged and marketed F1s
shall be identical, homogeneous and remain stable from year to year. These characteristics shall be
those identified on the samples submitted for registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and
Varieties. Reference standards shall be placed under the responsibility of the Department in charge
of Regulations, Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture.
Standards
Vegetable seed lots shall comply with the following standards:

 Guinea Sorrel                   250              97               0.5                  65
 Watermelon                      250              98               0.1                  75
 Parsley                          10              97               1.0                  65
 Chilli Pepper/Pepper             40              98               0.5                  65
 Leek                             20              97               0.5                  70

 Tomato                           20              97               0.5                  60



                                                                                           Page | 72
Quality Control
Obligation of vegetable seed companies: vegetable companies shall provide the following to the
Department in charge of regulations, control and certification of seeds and seedlings of the Ministry
in charge of Agriculture:
     - for a minimum of three (3) years, any document indicating the origin of the imported,
          conditioned, split, repackaged seed lots;
     - for a minimum of two (2) years, a sample taken from each imported, packaged, split or
          repackaged lot.
Control of vegetable seeds: The inspectors/controllers of the Department in charge of regulations,
control and certification of seeds and seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture may take
official samples at any stage of processing, transportation and marketing. Official controls are carried
out subsequently. They may include identity and true purity, specific purity, maximum seed content
and other species, germination capacity and health status.
Weight reduction of the official sample: For F1 hybrid varieties of the above-mentioned species,
the minimum weight of the official sample may be reduced to half of the set weight. However, the
official sample shall a weight of at least 5g and/or contain at least 400 seeds.
For lots packed in small packages, the official sample may be reduced to two bags per lot; the
number of seeds per sample shall be at least 200.
Labelling requirements
Information required on the supplier's label or on the package:
     - Name and address of the supplier responsible for affixing the Labels or the identification
          mark;
     - Name and address of the distributor for imported seeds only;
     - Date (month and year) of closure and last germination test.
     - Species, variety, grade (standard seeds)
     - Lot reference number given by the supplier responsible for tagging;
     - Declared net or gross weight at packaging, or declared number of seeds or clusters.
Label size: The minimum size for the label shall be 110 mm x 67 mm, except for small packages

    - Seeds testing on request: 1,500 CFA francs per sample

         • Imported seeds: 50,000 CFA francs
    - Re-registration of seed varieties in the official catalogue for a period of 5 years
         • Locally produced seeds: 12,500 CFA francs
         • Imported seeds: 25,000 CFA francs
    - Inspection and control of seed farms for certification: 10,000 CFA francs per hectare per
        crop cycle.
    - Issuance of activity certificates to seed companies for 5 years
         • First category seed company: 300,000 CFA francs
         • Second category seed company: 150,000 CFA francs
         • Third category seed company: 50,000 CFA francs
        • Analysis of Distinction, Homogeneity Stability (DHS) and Agronomic Technological
            Value (VAT): 2,000,000 F CFA

from royalties, but subject to plant quarantine regulations and seed regulations for the introduction

Article 6: This decision shall be registered and published wherever necessary.

                                                                                            Page | 78
APPENDIX 1: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, QUALITY
CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF ARABICA COFFEE SEEDS (COFFEA ARABICA)
Application scope
Due to the auto-compatibility of arabica coffee, seed production is based on the maintenance of the
variety developed by the seed breeder/grower stated in the Official Catalogue of Species and
Varieties. This method of propagation ensures the maintenance and preservation of the genetic and
morphological traits of the variety.
Production Organisation
Arabica coffee seed production is based on the selection proposed by the breeder or maintainer.
Starting genetic material: The starting genetic material of the generative propagation is the healthy
material (base) clearly identified resulting from the natural self-fertilisation.
Seed farms are inspected/controlled for the absence of diseases and pests. Samples collected may
be sent, if necessary, to the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other approved laboratory.
The variety is grown in a company or by an individual with a qualified and experienced staff, having
appropriate equipment to carry out this programme.
Arabica coffee seeds are produced under the responsibility of the seed company and subject to quality
controls by seed inspectors and controllers from the Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and
Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
The seed producing company must have:
    -   a coffee arabica seed farm;
    -   the appropriate facilities and equipment for production, maintenance and health protection;
    -   adequate facilities for the processing, packaging and conservation of Arabica coffee seeds .
Cultivation rules
During inspections and controls, the seed farm must comply with the following rules:
Identification of the seed farm: The seed farm is indicated at the entrance by a signpost or other
indications to identify the plot, species, variety and category.
Site Preparation: The soil must be thoroughly weeded over an area that extends well beyond the
area provided for the seed farm.
Health condition: The plot must be in good health condition. In all cases, a seed farm with a very
high severity and incidence for diseases, such as coffee anthracnose and growing pests shall be
rejected.
Minimum area: The minimum area of a seed farm submitted for certification must be at least one
(1) hectare. It must support the implementation of the programme declared by the producing
company.
Isolation: The distance between an arabica coffee seed farm and coffee farms must be at least 100 m
Health purification: Purification is mandatory in the seed farm, from the beginning of planting to the
harvest of seeds. It involves either the removal of off-types or the elimination of all fruits from the
tree as soon as symptoms appear on it.
Crop condition: It must ensure proper inspections. The poor condition of seed farms, including the
presence of weeds, diseases or pests, may result in its rejection or downgrading.
Harvest: The harvest is made from healthy ripe fruits, with bright red colours.
Depulping and Fermentation: Seeds are depulped manually. After pulping, they are put in jute
bags for fermentation. After fermentation, the seeds are washed and drained on a fine mesh rack in
the shade. They are stirred daily for one to two weeks.
Drying is done in the shade until a moisture content of 13% is attained. Afterwards, they will be
sorted to eliminate round seeds, off-types and malformed seeds and then packaged in jute or cotton
bags for conservation.



Page | 79
Seed conservation
Preservation methods: After treatment with registered pesticides, the seeds are packaged in jute
or cotton bags of one kilogram each. These bags must ensure the viability of the seeds and limit
losses caused by dehydration due to direct exposure of the plant material to sunlight or excess
moisture. The seeds can also be coated in charcoal powder or treated with a fungicide and stored
in a closed container under 100% room temperature.
The seeds must be stored in a specially equipped room under appropriate temperature and humidity
conditions.
Storage period: The storage conditions of seeds mentioned above must be 4 months for the first
method and 12 months for the second method.
Seed Farm Inspection
Prerequisite: The inspection must be carried out in the presence of the person in charge of the seed
company or a representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be given satisfactory evidence of
the variety used. The seed farm must be easily accessible.
Number of inspections: Crops intended for the production of arabica coffee seeds, are subject to
four categories of inspections per year.
     - An inspection to verify the origin of the parent seed, upon installation of the plot;
     - An inspection to check the state of maintenance of the operation, especially before the
          berries get ripe;
     - An inspection to evaluate the harvest;
     - An inspection to assess the conditions for harvesting, drying, treating and storing seed.
Crop grading: The compliance of crops with the tolerance threshold set by this regulation is graded
by counting, in line with the procedures specified on the inspection forms prepared by the
Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of
the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Any tree affected by anthracnose must be properly harvested;
If more than 10% of the trees in a plot are infested by anthracnose, the production of the said plot
for that season shall be denied certification.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller will complete the inspection form on the basis of
which he/she will propose the rejection or acceptance of the plot or seed farm or seed production
facility concerned, or recommend some corrective measures before a final decision is made.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the head of the seed
company or a representative. In case of refusal by the latter, this is mentioned.
The total or partial rejection of a seed farm or its production shall be registered in the inspection form
and notified to the person in charge of the seed company within 15 days following the inspection.
Certification standards
Seeds submitted for certification shall meet the requirements of this technical regulation and
standards:
Description of seed lots                                            Certified seed (Grade)
Varietal purity (minimum)                                                                      98 %
Germination rate (minimum)                                                                     85 %
Insect-attacked seeds (maximum)                                                                 2%
Seeds with lesions (maximum)                                                                    1%
Moldy seeds (maximum)                                                                           2%
Weed seeds (maximum)                                                                          0.5 %
Inert matter (maximum)                                                                        0.5 %




                                                                                              Page | 80
Marketing control
Locally produced or imported seeds, as well as those marketed are inspected/controlled and, where
necessary, samples are taken by seed inspectors/controllers for analysis at the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed testing laboratory, to ensure that they meet the
standards and rules set by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.
Companies producing arabica coffee seeds must maintain a seed transaction register in line with
Article 17 of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing.




Page | 81
APPENDIX 2: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, QUALITY
CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF ARABICA COFFEE SEEDLINGS (COFFEA ARABICA)
Application scope
The production of arabica coffee seedlings is based on the maintenance of the variety developed by
the breeder during registration in the Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties.
Production Organisation
The production of arabica coffee seedlings shall be based on the use of certified seeds.
Starting genetic material: The starting genetic material for the production of arabica coffee
seedlings shall be certified seeds.
Seed farms are inspected/controlled for the absence of diseases and pests. If necessary, samples
may be collected and sent to the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other approved laboratory.
Certified Arabica Coffee seedlings are seedlings of certified seeds of varieties listed in the Official Catalogue
of Species and Varieties.
Cultivation rules
Nurseries, arabica coffee seedlings must meet the following rules:
Identification of the nursery: Nurseries are indicated at the entrance by a sign or any other indications to
identify their species, variety, category, etc.
Preparation of the nursery site: The selected site shall be perfectly weeded on an area that largely extends
the area planned for the nursery.
Boards or beds are separated from each other by aisles. Several boards or beds form a series. Two
consecutive rows are separated by paths. A track of equal width must surround the seeding or
nursery.
Boards are no longer than 20 metres long and 1.20 to 1.50 metres wide. The dimensions of the
aisles and tracks must allow for the movement of people and equipment.
Empty clear or coloured polyethylene bags with gussets must have the following characteristics:
     - Thickness: 40 microns,
     - Width: 18 to 25 cm,
     - Height: 30 to 35 cm,
     - Perforated base with 15 holes of 5 mm diameter.
Protection of seed boxes and nurseries: The nursery is harnessed under shade to protect the
seedlings from high insolation.
The seedlings are placed in a nursery under a balanced light shade intercepting 50% of light.
Choice and preparation of the substrate: The substrate must enable the proper development of
the germs and seedlings. The substrate used shall be of good quality and free of plant debris.
Health condition: In all cases, a nursery whose severity and incidence for diseases (rust and
cercosporiose) and pests, are very high during growth, shall be denied certification.
Minimum area: The nursery area must be capable of producing a minimum of 15,000 seedlings
annually ready for the market.
Isolation: Isolation is not a problem for Arabica coffee seedlings. However, in germinators or
nurseries, beds of the same variety shall be at least 50 cm apart.
Varieties homogeneity and seedling sizes: The beds of the germinators must have plantlets of
the same size during transplanting.
Health purification: Purification is mandatory in germinators and nurseries, from the beginning of
the programme to the harvest of the seedlings. It consists in the elimination of sprouts affected by
anthracnose or rust, as soon as symptoms appear. The infested plant is removed from the
germinator or nursery. The nursery must be well maintained throughout the plant growth period.
Nursery operation: This must ensure proper inspections. The poor cropping conditions of a nursery,
involving the presence of weeds, attacks of diseases or pests can lead to the refusal of certification.


                                                                                                     Page | 82
Nursery Inspection
Prerequisites: The inspection must be done in the presence of the nursery farmer or a
representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the
variety and origin of the seed used. The nursery must be easily accessible.
Number of inspections: Nurseries for the production of certified Arabica coffee seedlings are
subject to at least three inspections per year.
    - The first inspection of the germinator shall be carried out at the beginning of the
        transplanting of the seedlings to observe whether the rules concerning the condition
        beyond parcel (cleanliness of the germiner) and the seedlings (health condition) have been
        complied with and to identify the infected types and seedlings to be cleaned;
    - The second inspection of the nursery is carried out 4 months after transplanting the
        seedlings in order to identify the off-types to be treated and the infected seedlings that will
        also have to be removed and destroyed;
    - The third inspection must be carried out 7 to 8 months after transplanting, just before or
        during the elimination of the seedlings; making it possible to choose and ensure the good
        quality of the seedlings to be marketed.
During the last inspection, the seed controller shall register the number of seedlings likely to be
placed on the market.
Only good quality seedlings are removed from the nursery with the following characteristics (to be
reformulated):
    - Height at least 50 cm;
    - Diameter: at least 0.5 cm (Bic diameter);
    - Seedlings with abnormalities such as stunted and weak plants are not put on the market.
Crop grading
The compliance of the seedlings with the tolerance threshold set by this Regulation shall be
assessed by counting in line with the procedures specified on the inspection forms prepared by the
Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedlings of
the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
The method of sampling the nursery in countable homogeneous units, for the characteristic to be
considered, allows partial acceptance or downgrading of the nursery.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form based on
which he/she will propose the rejection or acceptance of the nursery, or recommend some corrective
measures before a final decision is made.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the nursery grower or a
representative. In case of refusal by the latter, this is mentioned.
The total or partial rejection of a nursery is noted on the inspection form and notified to the nursery
farmer, within 15 days following the inspection.




Page | 83
Certification standards
Plant material submitted for certification must meet the requirements of the following Technical
Regulations and standards:
On leaving the nursery and on delivery of the seedlings
 Characteristics of seedlings                                        Certified seedlings
 Varietal purity (minimum)                                                                98 %
 Undeveloped seedlings (maximum)                                                           1%
 Narrow-leaved seedlings (maximum)                                                         1%
 Stunted seedlings (maximum)                                                               1%
  Seedlings with curled leaves (maximum)                                                   1%
 Twisted seedlings (maximum)                                                               1%
 Threaded seedlings                                                                        1%
 Seedlings too buried (maximum)                                                            1%
 Yellow seedlings (maximum)                                                                1%
 Seedlings with genetic abnormalities (maximum)                                            1%
 Diseases and parasites:
 - Anthracnose (maximum)                                                                 0.5 %
 - Rust (orange and floury) (maximum)                                                      1%
 - Cercosporiose                                                                            1%

Marketing control
Locally produced or imported seedlings placed on the market are inspected/controlled and if
necessary, samples are taken by seed inspectors/controllers for analysis at the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed testing laboratory, to ensure that they meet the
standards and rules defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.
Companies producing arabica coffee seedlings shall keep a seed transaction register in line with
Article 17 of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing.
