Page | 85
APPENDIX 1: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, QUALITY
CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF OIL PALM SEED (Elaeis guineensis var. Tenera)

Application scope
The production of oil palm seed is based on the maintenance of the male and female parents
developed by the breeder and the hybrid combinations declared at the time of registration in the
Official Catalogue of Species and Varieties. This method of propagation ensures the maintenance
and preservation of the genetic and morphological characteristics of the variety.
Production Organisation
The production of oil palm seed is based on material resulting from reciprocal recurrent selection
and/or pedigree selection proposed by the breeder or maintainer.
Starting genetic material: The starting genetic material of seed propagation consists of:
      - healthy female spawners (var. Dura) and plants derived from self-pollination. The set
         constitutes the family A0 (female parent of the starting genetic material);
      - healthy male spawners (var. Pisifera), selected by the breeder, constitute the B0 family
         (male spawners of the starting genetic material).
The F1 offspring obtained by artificial pollination of the A0 female parent family, by pollen of the B0
male parent family, constitutes the basic seed (var. Tenera) which is a hybrid of Dura x Pisifera.
The health status of each parent plant A0 and B0 shall be checked at the National Seed Testing
Laboratory or at any other approved laboratory using an appropriate method according to a protocol
proposed by the breeder or maintainer and approved by the Department in charge of regulations,
quality control and certification of seeds and plants of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Both female and male breeding equipment is grown at a research station or by an individual with
the required experience, all with the necessary equipment and qualified personnel. It shall be under
the responsibility of the breeder or maintainer and is checked by specialists appointed by the
Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Oil palm seeds are produced under the responsibility of the breeder or maintainer and are verified
by the Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling
of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
The seed producing company must have:
      - an oil palm seed farm (female spawners);
      - an oil palm seed farm (male spawners) and/or a supply contract for the supply of basic
         material (pollen);
      - facilities and equipment necessary for seed production, maintenance and health protection;
      - adequate storage facilities for oil palm pollen and seed.
Cultivation rules
During inspections of seed farms, oil palm parent must comply with the following rules:
Identification of the seed farm: The seed farms are marked at the entrance by a sign or any other
indication to identify the plot, crop and variety. The different parents are clearly identified.
Site preparation: The land must be perfectly weeded over an area that goes far beyond the area
planned for the seed farm.
Health condition: The parent shall be in good health conditions. In any case, a seed farm with a
very high severity and incidence of diseases and pests during growth shall be rejected. To this end,
all infected parent is discarded from basic seed production.
Minimum area: The minimum area of a seed farm presented for inspection must allow for the
program declared by the producing company.
Isolation: Isolation of male and/or female inflorescences involved in seed production shall be in
special systematic isolation bags designed for this purpose.
The number of isolation bags must be compatible with the level of production envisaged. A triple-
use bag may only be used for a maximum of three fertilizations; a single-use bag may only be used
once.



                                                                                            Page | 86
Pollination: For the production of basic oil palm seeds, pollination is artificial. It is done between
the genitors previously indicated by the breeder or the maintainer. The inflorescences are protected
by isolation bags. At the end of the pollination, each fertilized regime receives a label including at
least the identity of the female genitor, the identity of the male genitor and the date of pollination.
Health purification: Purification is compulsory in seed farm, from the beginning of planting to the
harvesting of the seeds. It consists in the elimination of the sick parent as soon as the symptoms
appear.
Seed conservation
Preservation methods: After treatment with registered pesticides, the seeds are stored in crates
and then packaged in polyethylene bags. These bags must ensure the viability of the seeds and limit
losses due to dehydration caused by direct exposure of the plant material to sunlight or excess
humidity.
The seeds must be stored in a specially equipped room under temperature and humidity conditions
defined by the protocol proposed by the breeder or maintainer and validated by the Department in
charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture.
Storage period: The storage conditions mentioned above must allow a storage period of the seeds
from 3 months to 2 years.
Seed Farm and Sprout Inspection
Prerequisite: The inspection must be carried out in the presence of the breeder, the maintainer or
his representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory evidence of the
variety, grade and/or origin of the pollen used. The seed farm must be easily accessible.
Number of inspections: Crops intended for the production of oil palm seeds are subject to three
categories of inspections per year.
     - An inspection is carried out to verify the existence of the spawning trees, the pollination
         equipment and the methods used;
     - An inspection is carried out every month to check the quality of the hybridization carried out
         (blank fertilization);
     - An inspection allows the verification of the stock of the bags of female fertilization, the bags
         of male fertilization and the contracts or invoices of purchase of pollen;
During the last inspection, the seed inspector/controller must identify the number of seeds that may
be offered for sale.
The storage, heating and seed germination rooms are subject to inspection.
Crop rating: Crop compliance with the tolerance threshold set out in this Regulation shall be
assessed by count, in accordance with the procedures specified on the inspection forms prepared
by the Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling
of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Any seed production from disease-infected parents shall will be rejected;
Any poor isolation of the female and male inflorescences shall result in the rejection of the seeds
from the regimes considered;
Any absence of labels on fertilized diets shall result in the rejection of the production in question;
Any excessive deviation of “blank fertilization” controls exceeding 10% error is intolerable.
After each inspection, the Seed Inspector/Controller shall complete the Inspection form based on
which he/his will propose the rejection or acceptance of the seed farm or seed production concerned,
or to recommend some corrective measures before a final decision is made.
The inspection form must be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the maintainer or a
representative. In the case of refusal by the latter, mention shall be made of this. Total or partial
rejection of a seed farm shall be recorded on the inspection record and notified to the maintainer
within 15 days of the inspection.




Page | 87
Certification standards
Plant material submitted for certification must meet the requirements of this Technical Regulation
and the following standards:
At the end of germination of preheated seeds
Seeds delivered as “sprouted seeds” must meet the following conditions:

 Characteristics (Maximum content)                                          Basic seed
 Seeds at the “white dot” stage, i.e. (undifferentiated germ)                              100%
 Differentiated seed                                                                        5%
 Seeds not germinated                                                                       1%
 Brown sprout seeds                                                                         5%
 Seeds with lesions                                                                          1%
 Presence of mould development on sprouted seeds                                            1%
 Moldy seeds                                                                                1%
 Weed seed                                                                                  0%
 Inert materials                                                                            1%

In any case, the total percentage of seed with defects must not exceed 5 %.
For lots of 500 or more sprouted seeds, it is recommended to place these seeds in packages
containing expanded polystyrene beads.
Marketing control
Locally produced or imported seeds for sale are inspected and, if necessary, samples are taken by
seed inspectors/controllers for analysis at the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other
approved seed testing laboratory, to ensure that they meet the standards and rules defined by the
laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.
Establishments producing oil palm seeds shall keep a record of seed transactions in accordance
with Article 17 of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing.




                                                                                       Page | 88
   2.4.10.    Decision No. 0800/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 21 July 2016 to approve the
       technical regulations for the production, quality control and certification of hybrid
       seeds and cocoa seedlings (Theobroma cacao).

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution,

Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/153 of 4 May 2005 on the creation, organisation and functioning of the
National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and

organized in accordance with the provisions of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed
activity, the implementing instruments and the official technical regulations laid down in this Decision.
Article 2: (1) At the beginning of each farming season, crop declarations of hybrid seeds of cocoa
seedlings, detailed on relevant forms, shall be sent to the Department in charge of Regulations,
Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.

growers of cocoa plants to be subject to quality control and certification for marketing on national
territory.

in charge of Agriculture is responsible for the implementation of this decision, which will be registered
and published in English and French.

Page | 89
APPENDIX 1: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, QUALITY
CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF HYBRID COCOA SEED (Theobroma cacao)
Application scope
The production of hybrid seeds is based on the maintenance of the male and female parents
developed by the breeder/breeder and hybrid combinations described in the Official Catalogue of
Species and Varieties. This method of propagation ensures the maintenance and preservation of
the genetic and morphological characteristics of the variety.
Production Organisation
The production of hybrid cocoa seed is based on the selection of the two parents (male and female)
recommended by the breeder or maintainer.
Starting genetic material: The starting genetic material of seed propagation consists of:
    - healthy and clearly identified female parents (selected varieties);
    - healthy and clearly identified male parents (selected varieties).
Seed farms are inspected/controlled for the absence of diseases and pests. If necessary, samples
taken may be sent to the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other approved laboratory.
These two parents are derived from vegetative propagation (by cuttings or grafting);
The F1 offspring obtained by artificial or manual pollination of the female spawner, by the pollen of
the male spawner, constitutes the certified hybrid seed.
Male and female clones are grown by an establishment or individual who has acquired the required
experience and qualification and by any person with a certificate of seed activity.
Hybrid cocoa seeds are produced under the responsibility of the seed establishment or an individual
and are subject to quality controls by the seed inspectors/controllers of the Department in charge of
Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture.
The seed producer must:
    - have the facilities and equipment necessary for production, maintenance and health
        protection;
    - have the material and equipment necessary for manual pollination.
Cultivation rules
Seed farms shall meet the following requirements:
Seed farm identification: Seed farms shall be marked at the entrance with a sign or other indications to
identify the plot, crop and variety. The different spawners are clearly identified.
The seed producer must have a bi-clonal cocoa seed farm.
Site Preparation: The field must be thoroughly weeded over an area that extends well beyond the area
provided for the seed farm.
Health condition: The spawners must be in good health condition. In all cases, a seed farm with
very high severity and incidence for diseases and pests during growth shall be refused for
certification.
Minimum area: The seed farm must have a minimum area of 0.5 hectares. Submitted for verification
for certification, the seed farm must enable the programme declared by the producing company to
be carried out.
Pollination: For hybrid seed production, pollination is controlled (manual). It is done between the
spawners previously indicated by the seed establishment. Manual pollination takes place from 6 to
10 hours. After pollination, each fertilized flower receives a pin. A tag is affixed to the tree to indicate
the identity of the female spawner, the identity of the male spawner, the date of pollination and the
number of flowers pollinated per foot.
Health and varietal purification: Purification is mandatory in the seed farm, from the beginning of
planting, until the harvest of the cabosses. It consists in the elimination of diseased spawners and
pods from the onset of symptoms as well as off-types in the plot.
                                                                                                Page | 90
Cultural state: It must allow to ensure proper inspections. Poor crop condition of seed farms,
including the presence of weeds, disease attacks or pests, may result in the refusal of certification.
Harvest of Pods: Only hybrid pods from controlled pollination shall be harvested as seed and
submitted for certification.
The estimation of certified beans per harvested pod shall depend on the variety listed in the
catalogue.
Seed conservation
Preservation methods: The pods shall be stored in a specially equipped rooms under appropriate
temperature and humidity conditions.
Storage period: Pods can be stored for 72 hours under ambient temperature conditions.
Prerequisite: The inspection shall be done in the presence of the person in charge of the
establishment or a representative. The seed inspector/controller shall be provided with satisfactory
evidence of the variety and/or origin of the parent clones. The seed farm must be easily accessible.
Number of inspections: Crops for the production of hybrid cocoa seed are inspected at least three
categories per year:
     - An inspection is carried out to verify the existence of the spawning trees, the pollination
         equipment and the methods used;
     - During the flowering phase, an inspection is carried out at least once a month to check the
         quality of the hybridization carried out.
During the last inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall estimate the number of hybrid pods
and therefore the number of beans that may be certified.
Crop grading: Crop compliance with the tolerance threshold set out in this Regulation shall be
assessed by count, in accordance with the procedures specified in the inspection forms prepared by
the Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of
the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Any production of hybrid seeds from disease-affected parent shall be refused certification;
Any absence of pins and labels on the cocoa pods shall lead to the refusal for the certification of the
beans contained in the said cocoa pods;
Excessive pollination deviation exceeding 10% error is intolerable.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form based on
which he/she shall propose the rejection or acceptance of the seed farm or hybrid seed production
concerned, or recommend some corrective measures before a final decision is made.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the seed establishment
manager or a representative. In the case of refusal by the latter, mention shall be made of this. The
total or partial rejection of a seed farm shall be recorded on the inspection record and notified to the
person responsible for the seed farm within 15 days of the inspection.
Certification standards
Hybrid seeds delivered in the form of cocoa pods must meet the following conditions:
characteristics Certified seed                                               Certified seeds
Varietal purity with sleeve (minimum)                                                            98 %
Varietal purity without sleeve (minimum)                                                          85%
Germination rate (minimum)                                                                       85 %
Bean weight (minimum)                                                                             30 g
Mirid attacks on pods (maximum)                                                                  30 %
Brown rot                                                                                          0%




Page | 91
Marketing control
Locally produced or imported hybrid cocoa seeds placed on the market are inspected/controlled by
seed inspectors/controllers and as required, samples are taken for analysis at the National Seed
Testing Laboratory or any other approved seed analysis laboratory to ensure that they meet the
standards and rules defined by the laws and regulations in force in Cameroon.
Establishments or producers of hybrid cocoa seeds must keep a record of seed transactions in
accordance with Article 17 of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the
modalities for seed production, quality control and marketing.
APPENDIX 2: OFFICIAL TECHNICAL REGULATIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, QUALITY
CONTROL AND CERTIFICATION OF COCOA SEEDLINGS (Theobroma cacao)
Application scope
The production of the cocoa plants is based on the maintenance of the spawners developed by the
company, and hybrid combinations described at the time of registration in the Official Catalogue of
Species and Varieties.
Production Organisation
The production of cocoa plants must be based on the use of certified hybrid seeds.
Starting genetic material: Certified hybrid seed are the starting genetic material for the production
of cocoa seedlings. The cocoa plants are produced under the responsibility of the nursery owner
and are subject to checks by the seed inspectors/controllers of the Department in charge of
Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture.
These male and female parents are inspected/controlled for the absence of diseases and pests. If
necessary, samples taken may be sent to the National Seed Testing Laboratory or any other
approved laboratory.
Certified cocoa seedlings are produced by certified hybrid seeds of the varieties listed in the Official
Catalogue of Species and Varieties.
Cultivation rules
Tree nurseries must meet the following rules:
Site Selection: The nursery site must be chosen in an area that is easily accessible, close to a
permanent water point and on slightly sloping ground.
Identification of the nursery: Nurseries are indicated at the entrance by a sign or any other
indications to identify the plant species, variety, category, etc.
Preparation of the nursery site: The nursery site must be thoroughly cleaned (weeding, clearing
of stumps, etc.) on an area that largely exceeds the area planned for the establishment of the
nursery.
Boards or beds are separated from each other by aisles. Several boards or beds form a block. Two
consecutive blocks are separated by corridors. A trail must surround the nursery.
Boards are no longer than 20 metres long and 0.70 to 1 metre wide. The dimensions of the aisles (0.6-0.7 m)
and corridors (1-1.5 m) must allow for the movement of people and equipment.
Empty transparent or coloured polyethylene gusseted bags must have the following minimum characteristics:
    -   Thickness: 40-50µ,
    -   Width 20-27 cm,
    -   Height 30-35 cm,
    -   Perforated base of holes in the lower third.
Nursery protection: The nursery must be built under a 2 meter high shade to protect the plants
from high insolation.
Choice and preparation of the substrate: The substrate must allow for the proper growth of the
seedlings.

                                                                                               Page | 92
Health condition: In all cases, a nursery whose proportion of infested plant attacks must be refused
for certification. For this purpose, the proportion of infested seedlings may not exceed 10 % in
nurseries.
Minimum production: The nursery area must allow for the minimum production of 25,000 seedlings
annually ready to be delivered to farmers;
Isolation: Isolation is not an issue for cocoa seedlings. However, seedlings of different varieties
must be classified in separate blocks.
Variety homogeneity and seedlings sizing: Nursery beds shall have the same variety and size.
Health purification: Purification is mandatory in the nursery, from the beginning of planting to the
harvest of seedlings. It consists in the elimination of feet with diseases, as soon as symptoms
appear. The weeding must be complete and the plant removed from the nursery. The nursery must
be well maintained throughout the growing period.
Crop condition: It must allow to ensure proper inspections. Poor crop condition of a nursery,
including the presence of weeds, attacks of diseases or pests, can lead to its rejection or
downgrading.

Maintenance and conservation of seedlings in the nursery
Conservation methods: Once treated with registered pesticides, seedlings are packaged in
polyethylene bags or similar local materials with sizes that allow the seedlings to develop properly.
The seedlings are usually maintained and stored in the nursery before being transplanted to the
farm.
They must ensure viability and limit losses due to dehydration through direct sun exposure of the
plant material or through excess humidity.
Shelf life in the nursery: The shelf life of seedlings in the nursery shall not exceed 7 months.
Inspection of nurseries
Prior arrangements: The inspection shall be carried out under supervision of the head of the seed
establishment (nurseryman) or a representative. The seed inspector/controller shall receive
satisfactory evidence of the category, variety and origin of the hybrid seed used. The nursery shall
be accessible.
Number of inspections: Nurseries for the production of certified cocoa seedlings shall be inspected
at least three times a year.
    -   The first inspection shall be done 2 months after the beans have been sown to observe if the quality
        of the site (cleanliness of the nursery) and the seedlings (number, health condition, etc.) comply with
        the rules. This inspection also aims to identify poorly-developed and/or infested seedlings;
    -   The second inspection is held within 4 months after sowing the beans to check the poor growth of the
        seedlings and the maintenance and phytosanitary operations of the nursery.
    -   The third inspection must be done before or while removing the seedlings. It enables proper timing of
        the removal and ensure the quality of seedlings. The seed inspector /controller shall also record the
        number of potentially marketable seedlings.




Page | 93
Crop grading: The compliance of crops with the standards under this regulation is assessed by
counting, as specified in the inspection forms prepared by the Department in charge of Regulations,
Quality Control and Certification of Seeds and Seedling of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
The sampling method of the nursery in homogeneous counting units, for the characteristic to be
considered, authorizes the grading or the partial or total downgrading of the nursery.
After each inspection, the seed inspector/controller shall complete the inspection form based on
which him/her shall reject or approve the nursery, or recommend some corrective measures before
making a final decision is made.
The inspection form shall be co-signed by the seed inspector/controller and the nurseryman or a
representative. If the latter does not agree, it shall be noted. The total or partial refusal of a nursery
is recorded on the inspection form and notified to the nurseryman within 15 days of the inspection.

Certification standards
The plant material for certification shall meet the requirements of these technical regulations and the
following standards:
Description                                                                 Certified seedlings
Rolled leaf seedlings (maximum)                                                                    5%
Twisted seedlings (maximum)                                                                        1%
Short stunted seedlings (maximum)                                                                   5%
Filamented seedlings (maximum)                                                                     5%
Yellowish plants seedlings (maximum)                                                               5%
Diseases and pests :
     - Capsid attacks                                                                              0%
     - Spotted leaves                                                                              1%
     - Dead leaves                                                                                 1%

Marketing control
Locally produced or imported cocoa seedlings for sale shall be inspected and if necessary, samples
shall be analysed at the National Laboratory for Analysis of Agricultural Inputs and Products or any
other authorised seed laboratory by see inspectors/controllers to ensure that they comply with the
standards and rules provided for by laws and regulations in Cameroon.
Cocoa seedling producing companies shall keep a seed transaction register in accordance with
Article 17 of Decree No 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the modalities for seed
production, quality control and marketing.

   2.4.12.     Decision No. 00655/MNADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 1 December 2020 to lay down
       rules for the production and certification of fresh fruit and vegetables for export.

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0771/PM of 6 April 2005 to lay down the conditions for plant quarantine
operations;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2018/190 of March 2, 2018 supplementing certain provisions of Decree No.
2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2018/191 of 2 March 2018 to reorganise the government;
Mindful of Decision No. 01465/MINADER/CAB of 4 October 2017 on the organisation of regional
phytosanitary controls;
Considering the recommendations of Report No. ARES/3061360 of 19 June 2017 of the European
Union Commission on the audit of the national control of Cameroon plants and plant products exported
to the European Union;
Considering service needs,
                                   HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: This Decision lays down the conditions for the production and certification of fresh fruit and
vegetables for export.
Article 2: (1) The following crops are subject to this Decision:
      - Mango (Mangifera indica) ;                     - Passion fruit (Passiflora edulis)
      - Soursop (Annona muricata);                     - Capsicum pepper species (Capsicum
      - Citrus fruits (Citrus spp) ;                       annuum);
      - Chilli (Capsicum frustescens);                 - Aubergine (Solanum melongena) ;
      - Pineapple (Ananas comosus) ;                   - Tomato (Solanum lycopersicum);
      - European Black nightshade                      - Amaranth (Amaranthus spp);
          (Solanum scabrum) ;                          - Cassava leaves (Manihot esculenta);
      - Okok (Gnetum africanum);                       - Cassimanga (Spondias dulcis);
      - Safou (Dacryodes edulis)                       - Avocado (Persea americana).
           (2) The export of fresh fruit and vegetables, referred to in paragraph 1 above, is prohibited
without a phytosanitary certificate, including those transported in hand bags.
           (3) The export of fresh potatoes (Solanum tubersum) to the European Union (EU) is
prohibited.
           (4) The import of fresh potatoes (Solanum tubersum), except seeds, from the European
Union (EU) is prohibited.
Article 3: (1) Any individual or legal entity willing to export the products referred to in Article 2 above,
must be registered in the database of fresh fruits and vegetables exporters. Registration shall be done
through the submission of a file to the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.
           (2) The file, referred to in paragraph 1 above, shall comprise a stamped application for
registration in the database of fresh fruits and vegetables exporters, stating the exact address of the
exporter, crops (species) to be exported, as well as substitutes and facilities for the operations. The
application shall comprise:
     - a copy of the National Identity Card of the head (s) of the company;
     - a certified copy of the certificate of registration in the database of exporters of the Ministry in
        charge of Trade;
     - a list of production sites and their surface areas;
     - a list of export markets targeted for the products;
     - a location plan of packaging sites; and
     - copies of contracts with producers, where necessary.
(3) Upon receipt of the application, a system audit shall be conducted within 20 working days.
Page | 99
            (4) The audit shall provide evidence that the applicant has a good understanding of the target
market requirements.
These include written policies and procedures on the system, notably:
     - technical routes;
     - product safety and hygiene;
     - product handling hygiene;
     - pest management system;
     - traceability;
     - technician skills (training, diploma, contract, etc.);
     - Operations schedules (sowing, weeding, treatments, fertilisation, phytosanitary treatments,
         cleaning and disinfection of facilities and reusable equipment, etc.);
     - storage of inputs and products and records of use;
     - any other conditions deemed necessary to guarantee the quality of agricultural products.
Article 4: (1) All production and packaging sites for fruit and vegetables for export shall be registered.
            (2) Production sites shall be registered at the beginning of each crop cycle to facilitate
planning and certification.
Article 5: (1) A joint visit shall be undertaken by the Regional Department in charge of Quality Control
and the Plant Quarantine Station to approve the production and packaging sites and assign them
codes.
            (2) The lists of codes of the production sites and packaging warehouses are kept
respectively by the Regional Department in charge of Quality Control Department in charge of the
relevant Region and the Plant Quarantine Station.
Article 6: The technical itineraries for the production and packaging of fresh fruits and vegetables for
export are recorded in the technical production guides validated by the department in charge of
regulations.
Article 7: The procedures for inspecting farms, packaging warehouses and exit points are outlined in
the official technical regulations for phytosanitary control of various fruits and vegetables approved by
the Phytosanitary Authority.
Article 8: In the case of infection or infestation, the phytosanitary officer in charge of inspecting farms,
packaging warehouses or those working at the Phytosanitary Police Station, shall terminate the
certification process of the relevant plot and shall draw up an inspection report stating the abnormalities
observed and the measures to be taken.
Article 9: (1) Fruits and vegetables shall be sampled at least twice during the harvest period from the
production and packaging sites and analysed in the laboratory to check their health condition.
            (2) In case pesticides are used, samples of products shall be submitted for analysis of
pesticide residues.
Article 10: (1) Agricultural Posts, Phytosanitary Brigades or competent Regional Departments of
Quality Control shall carry out phytosanitary inspections in farms.
              (2) Phytosanitary inspections in packaging warehouses shall be carried out by the
Phytosanitary Post responsible for the shipment or by the Regional Department in charge of Quality
Control, where necessary.
              (3) Phytosanitary inspections at exit points shall be carried out by the Phytosanitary Post
responsible for the shipment.
Article 11: The following approved forms shall be issued after successful inspections:
     - the attestation of farm inspection, when the harvested product is of good quality;
     - the pre-certification attestation, when the product inspected in the warehouse and the
         packaging meet the standards.
     - the phytosanitary certificate, when traceability, phytosanitary status and additional
         declarations are respected.




                                                                                               Page | 100
Article 12: Inspections, site visits, sampling and laboratory analysis shall be borne by the user.
Article 13: In case of non-compliance with the phytosanitary certification procedure, the Director of
Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural Inputs and Products (DRCQ) may
suspend exports from the production site for a defined period.
Article 14: The Director of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural Inputs and
Products (DRCQ) and the Regional Delegates of MINADER shall be responsible, respectively, for the
implementation of this decision.
Article 15: This decision shall enter into force from the date of signature, and shall be registered and
published wherever necessary.

                                                                          Yaoundé, 1 December 2020
                                               The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                  Gabriel MBAIROBE

        and make enforceable the protocols of Distinctness, Uniformity and Stability (DUS) and
        Agronomic and Technological Value (VAT) tests for onion (Allium cepa)

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2001/014 of 23 July 2001 relating to seed activities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/153 of 4 May 2005 on the creation, organisation and functioning of the
National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/002 of 4 January 2019 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/3091/PM of 29 August 2005 to lay down the conditions for seed production,
quality control and marketing;
Mindful of Order No. 390/MINADER/MINCOMMERCE of 7 August 2006 laying down specifications for
the production, import and marketing of seeds and seedlings;
Mindful of Order No. 00073/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRSQV of 22 April 2019 laying down the
procedures for the registration of species and varieties;
Considering the recommendations of the National Council for Seed and Plant Varieties during the
session of 23 September 2020 (8th session),

                                   HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: The procedures for the Distinctness, Uniformity and Stability (DUS) and Agronomic and
Technological Value (ATV) tests for onion (Allium cepa), as well as their technical questionnaire
attached in Appendices I, II and III shall be approved and made enforceable from the date of signature
of this Decision.
Article 2: (1) Any new onion variety eligible for registration in the National Catalogue shall be subject
to Distinctness, Stability and Uniformity (DUS) and Agronomic and Technological Value (VAT) tests.
            (2) Applications for registration of onion varieties shall be sent to the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture as well as the questionnaire in 1Appendix III attached.
            (3) Breeders and importers of seeds shall register onion varieties for inspection and
certification, prior to marketing on the national territory.
            (4) Any modification on the registration application shall be addressed to the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture, no later than one month after setting up the test protocol.
Article 3: The Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural
Inputs and Products shall responsible for the implementation of this decision.
Article 4: This decision shall be registered and published in English and French, wherever necessary.
                                                                              Yaounde, 1 December 2020
                                                  The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                      Gabriel MBAIROBE

                                                                                              Page | 108
      3. PART THREE: LEGAL FRAMEWORK FOR
                  FERTILISERS




Page | 109
3.1. LAW




           Page | 110
   3.1.1.    Law n° 2003 / 007 of 10 july 2003 to regulate activities of the fertilizer sub sector
       in Cameroon

The National Assembly deliberated and adopted,
The President of the Republic enacts the law set out below:
          PART I GENERAL PROVISIONS
Section l: (1) This law shall govern the fertilizer sub sector in Cameroon. In that regard, it shall aim at:
     - increased farm productivity and agricultural production;
     - sustainable management of natural resources.
(2) Its scope of application shall cover the following activities: fertilizer production, importation,
packaging, distribution and use.
(3) This law shall be applicable to all forms of fertilizers, particularly mineral fertilizers, organic fertilizers
and biological fertilizers.
(4) The activities of the fertilizer sub sector defined in section (2) above may be carried out singly or
jointly with other activities.
Section 2: For the purpose of this law and subsequent enactments, the following definitions shall apply:
Competent Authority: The Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Fertilizer: any substance or matter containing one or more nutritive elements for plants which are
recognized and used as such with a view to enhancing plant growth and yield.
Label: refers to any written, printed or graphic inscription on the immediate packaging or in a fertilizer
specific message.
Registration: enrolment of an individual or corporate entity exercising an activity in the fertilizer sub
sector in Cameroon.
Production: the manufacturing of fertilizers.
Packaging: mixing and bagging of fertilizers.
Distributor: any individual or corporate entity dealing in the wholesale or retail supply or marketing of
fertilizers in Cameroon.
Fertilizer sub sector: a domain in the agricultural sector in which activities such as research, training
and regulation of soil fertility and fertilization, production, importation, packaging, training and
regulation of soil fertility and fertilization, production, importation, packaging, distribution and use of
fertilizing substance are carried out and in which stakeholders contribute to increase soil productivity
within the context of sustainable management.
Section 3: (1) The activities of the fertilizer sub sector governed by this law and stipulated in section 1
above shall be exercised under State supervision. In that regard, the Competent Administration shall
define the accepted technical standard in the field and ensure fertilizer quality control.
(2) Fertilizer research activities shall remain subject to the laws and regulation in force.


        PART II: EXERCISING ACTIVITIES IN THE FERTILIZER SUB SECTOR
        CHAPTER I: CONDITION FOR FERTILIZER PRODUCTION, IMPORTATION,
        EXPORTATION, DISTRIBUTION AND USE
Section 4: Any individual or corporate entity shall be free to produce, import and distribute fertilizers
throughout the national territory subject to the law and regulations in force.
Section 5: The conditions for producing. importing, exporting, packaging, storing and distributing
fertilizers or any other related activity shall be fixed by joint order of the minister in charge of agriculture,
the minister in charge of trade, the minister in charge of environment and forest and the minister in
charge public health.



Page | 111
       CHAPTER II: CONDITIONS FOR THE USE OF FERTILIZERS
Section 6: (1) The intensive use of fertilizers in a form shall be subject to prior assessment of the
physical and chemical state of the soil.
(2) The modalities and content of such assessment shall be laid down by regulation.
Section 7: (1) Any individual or corporate entity, whether public or private, which owns a farm and
intensively uses fertilizers shall be bound to regularly conduct an impact assessment of such fertilizers
on the environment.
(2) The modalities of such impact assessment shall be laid down by regulations.
Section 8: Any individual or corporate entity, whether public or private, which uses fertilizer shall be
bound to ensure that such products conform, in term of quantity and quality, to the standards stipulated
by the Administration.
       CHAPTER III: FERTILIZERS INSPECTION AND QUALITY CONTROL
Section 9: (1) Fertilizer quality control is hereby instituted. Such control shall entail the inspection of
production plants, packaging sites and distribution warehouses for fertilizers as well as taking samples
for laboratory analyses.
(2) Fertilizer quality control shall be ensured by sworn officials working in the government services of
the competent Authority. Such employees shall have free access to fertilizer production, storage,
packaging and distribution facilities.
(3) The procedures for controlling and taking and analysing samples shall be laid down by regulations.
Section 10: The conditions for fertilizers must meet the general standards defined by joint order of the
Minister in charge of agriculture, the Minister in charge of trade, the Minister in charge of environment
and forest and the Minister in charge of public health.
Section 11: The modalities for analysing samples of fertilizers taken for control purposes shall be laid
down by regulations.
       CHAPTER IV: INSPECTION AND CONTROL OF USE OF FERTILIZERS
Section 12: (1) A control of fertilizer-intensive farm is hereby instituted. The modalities for such control
shall be laid down by regulations.
Section 13: The control of fertilizer use shall be conducted by sworn officials under conditions laid
down in section 9 (2) above.
       PART III: DEVELOPING THE FERTILIZER SUB SECTOR
Section 14: (1) Incentives may be granted as and when necessary, particularly with regard to
financing, taxation, customs duties and the procurement of equipment, with a view to promoting private
investment in the fertilizer sub sector.
(2) Indirect support to the development of the fertilizer sub sector may be provided by state with a view
facilitating the training of staff in the field of fertilizers.
Section 15: For the purpose of promoting the development of fertilizer sub sector throughout the
national territory, a special account is hereby created in accordance with the provisions of Article 39
and 41 of Ordinance No. 62/0F/4 of 7 February 1962 relating to the finance system of the state.




                                                                                               Page | 112
Section 16: (1) The Special Account provided for in section 15 above shall also be sourced from:
interest accruing from the trust fund of the fertilizer Sub sector Reform Programme (FSSRP);
contributions from stakeholders in the sub sector as determined by joint order of the minister in charge
of agriculture and the minister in charge of finance; contributions from international donors; any other
contributions provided for by law; donations and legacies.
(2) A decree by the president of the republic shall define the terms and conditions for the Organization
and functioning of the special trust fund provided for in Section 15 above.
        PART IV: OFFENCES AND PENALTIES
Section 17: The following shall constitute violations of the provisions of this law and its instruments of
implementation:
    -   the production, distribution and/or use of fertilizers that do not comply with regulations;
    -   the production, distribution and/or use of fertilizer containing noxious substances or harmful
        Properties, even when used in prescribed quantity, and are likely to affect plant growth, harm
        human and animal health. and damage the environment:
    -   the flouting of registration formalities and refusal to submit fertilizers or documents relating
thereto for quality control;
    -   refusal to submit to the control procedures governing fertilizer use.
Section 18: (1) Without prejudice to civil liability and notwithstanding certain administrative penalties
provide for by law n° 90/3 of 10 august 1990 governing commercial activities in Cameroon what can
be meted out by the ministry in charge of agriculture to persons carrying out the activities governed by
this law, any person found guilty of committing the offences set forth in section l17 above shall be
punished with imprisonment for from 1 (one) to 5(five) years or with fine of from 50 000 (fifty thousand)
to 100 000 000 (one hundred million) CFA francs or with both such imprisonment and fine.
(2) In case of repeated offence, the penalties set out in subsection 1 above shall be doubled.
Section 19: The penalties provided under this law shall be applicable without prejudice to the
provisions of the penal Code.
Section 20: In addition to the penalties set out in section 18 above, the fertilizers in question may be:
    -   retuned to the country of origin, the expense of the importer or distributor in case of imported
        fertilizers;
    -   seized and destroyed where they are considered hazardous, using environment-friendly
        techniques, at the expense of the offender;
    -   declassified.
Section 21: (1) Without prejudice to the prerogatives of the Legal Department and Judicial Police Officers with
general jurisdiction, sworn officials of the competent government services shall be responsible for carrying out
investigation, establishing facts and instituting criminal proceedings against violators of the provisions of this law.
(2) In the performance of their duties, sworn officials shall be bound to hold professional identity cards.
Section 22: Any other offences not provided for by this law and its instruments of implementation shall fall within
the purview of ordinary law.
Section 23: (1) Any established offence shall require a duly drawn up report. The investigation and establishment
of offences shall be done by 2(two) officials who co-sign the report. The said report shall be authentic until
challenged.
(2) The report thus establishing shall be counter-signed by the accused. In case of refusal by the latter, the report
shall make mention thereof.

Page | 113
Section 24: (1) The report establishing the offence shall be notified to the offender by any written
means. The offender shall have 20 (twenty) days, with effect from the date of notification, to dispute it.
Thereafter, any dispute shall be inadmissible.
(2) The dispute shall be referred to the government services in charge of fertilizers which shall decide
thereon within 15 (fifteen) days of reception of such claim. Failing this, the claim shall be deemed to
have received a favourable reply and the report establishing the offence shall become statute barred.
(3) Where the government services in charge of fertilizers find the claim founded, such claim shall be
upheld and the report establishing the offence shelved. Conversely, the government services in charge
of fertilizers shall initiate legal proceeding in accordance with the laws in force.
Section 25: (1) Any violations of this law and its instruments of implementation may give rise to a plea-
bargaining between the services of fertilizers and the offender at the behest of the latter.
(2) The said plea-bargaining shall be registered at the expense of the offender and shall put an end to
the public prosecution where a plea-bargaining is reached prior to the substantive ruling passed by the
competent court.
(3) The fines arising from the plea-bargaining provided for above shall be consistent with the same
recovery regulations as are applicable to fines inflicted by ordinary law courts.
       PART V: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Section 26: The various stakeholders in the fertilizer sub sector shall have a period of 1 (one) year to
comply with the provisions of this law with effect from its date of enactment.
Section 27: All previous provisions repugnant hereto are hereby repealed.
Section 28: This law shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency and inserted
in the Official Gazette in English and French.
                                                                                  Yaounde, 10 july 2003
                                                                            President of the Republic.
                                                                                              Paul BIYA

   3.2. ORDERS




Page | 115
   3.2.1.    Joint Order No. 00115/MINADER/MINCOMMERCE/MINEPDED/MINSANTE of 29
       August 2019 to lay down conditions for fertiliser marketing in Cameroon

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
THE MINISTER OF TRADE,
THE MINISTER OF ENVIRONMENT, PROTECTION OF NATURE AND SUSTAINABLE
DEVELOPMENT, AND
THE MINISTER OF PUBLIC HEALTH,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 64-LF-23 of 13 November 1964 on the protection of public health;
Mindful of Law No. 96/12 of 5 August 1996 to lay down the legal framework relating to environmental
management;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No. 2004/002 of 21 April 2004 governing legal metrology in Cameroon;
Mindful of the framework law No. 2011/012 of 6 May 2011 on consumer protection in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No. 2015/018 of 21 December 2015 governing commercial activities in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No. 2016/004 of 18 April 2016 governing foreign trade in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No.2018/020 of 11 December 2018 - Framework Law on Food Safety;
Mindful of Decree No. 92/089 of 4 May 1992 to specify the duties of the Prime Minister as amended
and supplemented by Decree 95/145- bis of 4 August 1995;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2012/431 of 1 October 2012 to organise the Ministry of Environment, Protection
of Nature and Sustainable Development
Mindful of Decree No. 2012/513 of 12 November 2012 to organise the Ministry of Trade;
Mindful of Decree No. 2013/093 of 3 April 2013 to organise the Ministry of Public Health;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/02 of 4 January 2019 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/2584/PM of 23 August 2011 to lay down conditions for the protection of
soils and the sub-soil;
                                    HEREBY ORDER AS FOLLOWS:
       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: (1) This joint order lays down the conditions for the marketing of fertilisers in Cameroon.
            (2) It is issued pursuant to Article 10 of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the
fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon.
Article 2: For the purpose of this joint order, the following definitions shall apply:
Soil amendment: any substance used to enhance the physico-chemical and/or biological properties
of the soil in order to improve its performance.
Biostimulants: any substance or microorganism applied to plants to enhance nutrition efficiency,
abiotic stress tolerance, crop quality traits, and/or the availability of nutrients in the soil or rhizosphere,
regardless of its nutrients content.

                                                                                                 Page | 116
Marketing: The act of placing a product on the market or fixing the conditions and channels of
distribution for its sale.
Retail distributor: any natural person or legal entity, according to professional standards, who
purchases fertilisers from wholesale distributors and resells to farmers.
Wholesaler: any natural person or legal entity, according to professional standards, who purchases
fertilisers from a producer or through importation, and resells to retailers in bulk.
Packaging: mixing and packing fertilisers.
Deficiency: the amount of nutrients revealed by analysis to be less than the amount declared, which
may result from nutrient deficiency or lack of homogeneity.
Packaging material: any object, irrespective of the type of material, used to pack and protect goods,
to enable their handling and transport from producers to consumers.
Fertiliser: any substance or material containing one or more plant nutrients recognised and used as
such to boost plant growth and production. Mineral fertilisers, organic fertilisers, organo-mineral
fertilisers, biostimulants, soil amendments and all fertilisers are considered as fertilisers and referred
to as such.
Customised fertiliser: any fertiliser prepared based on the customer's formula.
Hazardous fertilisers: any fertiliser that could cause an explosion or other damages if mishandled
during transport, storage or use.

Mineral fertilisers: mineral fertilisers produced by the chemical industry or derived from phosphate
and potash natural deposits.
Organic fertilisers: animal or plant fertilisers.
Organic-mineral fertiliser: fertiliser obtained from the mixture of mineral and organic fertilisers.
Registration: registration of a natural person or legal entity carrying out an activity in the fertiliser sub-
sector in Cameroon with the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Label: Indication of any content in written, printed or graphic form on the immediate packaging.
Fertilising agent: a product used to ensure plant nutrition or to improve the physical, chemical and
biological properties of soils.
Formulation: mixture of several nutrients making up a fertiliser.
Production: manufacture of fertilisers.
Promoter: any natural person or legal entity who has received an Authorisation to market a fertilising
agent.
Fertiliser sub-sector: A branch of Agriculture, which deals with research, supervision and regulation
in terms of soil fertility and fertilisation, as well as the import, export, packaging, distribution and use of
fertilisers. It also includes stakeholders, who contribute to improve soil productivity within the
framework of sustainable management.
Article 3: Fertilisers marketed in Cameroon shall be subject to the legislative and regulatory provisions
governing commercial activity, environmental protection, public health, mines and industries.




Page | 117
        CHAPTER II: EVALUATION COMMITTEE AND AUTHORISATION PROTOCOL FOR THE
        MARKETING OF FERTILISERS
SECTION I: FERTILISER EVALUATION COMMITTEE
Article 4: A Fertiliser Evaluation Committee, hereinafter referred to as the Committee,1 is hereby
established within the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Article 5: The Committee shall:
    -   rule on application files for marketing authorisations and those pertaining to their renewal,
        suspension and withdrawal;
    -   give advice on the restriction or prohibition on the use of a fertiliser;
    -   validate experimentation protocols;
    -   give technical advice on any fertiliser file that is entrusted to it.
Article 6: (1) Chaired by the Minister in charge of Agriculture or a representative, the Committee shall
include:
    -   two (02) representatives of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture;
    -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Trade;
    -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Environment;
    -   one (01) representative of the Ministry of public health;
    -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Finance;
    -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Water;
    -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Livestock;
    -   one (01) representative of Cameroon Standards and Quality Agency;
    -   one (01) representative of the Institute of Agricultural Research for Development;
    -   one (01) representative of a fertiliser analysis laboratory authorised by the Ministry in charge of
        Agriculture.
          (2) Members shall be appointed by their respective Administrations or structures.
          (3) The composition of the Committee shall be established by decision of the Minister.
          (4) The Department in charge of Regulations and Quality Control of Fertilisers shall be
responsible for the Committee’s secretariat.
          (5) The Committee’s operating expenditure shall be covered by the budget of the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture.
Article 7: The Committee’s secretariat shall:
    -   ensure the logistical and technical organisation of meetings;
    -   prepare the Committee’s working documents;
    -   report on items on the agenda during sessions;
    -   monitor the implementation of activities;
    -   identify barriers to the successful implementation of selected activities;
        propose technical and financial facilitation measures necessary for the implementation of
        activities;
    -   make any useful proposal to the Committee;
    -   ensure the implementation of the Committee's decisions and recommendations;
    -   carry out any other mission entrusted to it by the Committee during its sessions.
Article 8: (1) The Committee shall meet twice (2) a year, in an ordinary session at the request of its
Chairperson.
However, the Committee may meet in an extraordinary session at the request of a promoter, who shall
cover the costs of the session.
If necessary, the Chairperson may convene an extraordinary session of the Committee.
                                                                                                     Page | 118
Article 9: (1) Written notifications shall be sent to members, fifteen (15) days before the meeting,
indicating the agenda, date and venue of the meeting.
           (2) In case of emergency, this period may be reduced to seven (7) days.
Article 10: The Chairperson of the Committee may invite any person with expertise in an item on the
agenda to take part in the Committee's deliberations in an advisory capacity.
Article 11: (1) The Committee may validly sit only in the presence of 2/3 of its members.
             (2) The Committee shall take decisions by a simple majority of members present. In the event of
equal votes, the Chairperson shall have the casting vote.
             (3) The Committee’s decisions shall be recorded in the minutes, which shall be approved in advance
by Committee members and co-signed by the Chairperson and the Secretary of the meeting. The minutes shall
mention, in addition to the names of members present, persons invited in an advisory capacity.
Article 12: The Committee shall examine the files referred to in Article 6 above and take the following measures:
    -   A Marketing Authorisation of the Product for specific uses covering a renewable period of (5) years;
    -   Provisional Marketing Authorisations for a period of two (2) years renewable once, as the case might
        be;
    -   Modification or extended use of the product;
    -   Renewal of a marketing authorisation or provisional marketing authorisation;
    -   Transfer of a Marketing Authorisation;
    -   In-depth study of the fertiliser for a period of time as the case might be
    -   Restriction on the use;
    -   Refusal of a Marketing Authorisation;
    -   Suspension of a marketing authorisation or provisional marketing authorisation;
    -   Withdrawal of a marketing authorisation or provisional marketing authorisation.
Article 13: 1) The promoter has the right to appeal the Committee's decisions and may therefore request that
the case be re-examined. In this case, the promoter shall send a request for this purpose to the Minister in charge
of Agriculture within sixty (60) days of the notification of results of the session, to avoid foreclosure penalty.
              (2) During the next ordinary session or an extraordinary session, the case shall be re-examined
based on the provisions of Article 9.
SECTION II: PROCEDURES AND CRITERIA FOR THE EVALUATION OF FERTILISERS
Article 14: (1) Fertilisers shall be subject to Marketing Authorisation prior to their import, production
and distribution in Cameroon.
              (2) The procedure to evaluate fertilisers involves operations to ensure that any fertiliser
for use in Cameroon shall:
     - increase the yield of target crops;
     - improve physico-chemical and/or microbiological properties;
     - provide economic added value to users;
     - preserve animal, human and environmental health.
Article 15: (1) The procedure to evaluate fertilisers shall include the following steps;
    - physico-chemical and/or microbiological analyses by the National Analysis Laboratory of the
        Ministry in charge of Agriculture or a laboratory approved by the Ministry;
    - agronomic efficiency tests conducted by the Department in charge of Regulations and Quality
        Control of Fertilisers, with the support of an officially recognised research institute;
    - the examination of the complete scientific data by the Fertiliser Evaluation Committee,
        showing that the product is effective for its intended use and does not present any risk on
        human, animal, or environmental health, under the recommended conditions of use.
            (2) However, some fertilisers may be exempted from agronomic efficiency tests.          A
decision by the Minister in charge of Agriculture shall determine the list of such fertilisers.
            (3) the promoter shall bear the costs incurred for operations referred to in paragraph 1
above.



Page | 119
Article 16: (1) The examination of the scientific data of a fertiliser referred to in Article 16 paragraph
1, shall be subject to the production of a file in three (3) copies by the promoter comprising:
    - a stamped application for fertiliser examination, addressed to the Minister in charge of
         Agriculture;
    - a technical file (composition of the product, forms and solubilities of nutrients, instructions for
         use, results of analysis, safety data form);
    - a toxicological/eco-toxicological file;
    - an original label design or its model;
    - a description of the packaging;
    - an agronomic efficiency test report;
    - a certificate of conformity issued by the Director in charge of fertiliser regulations and control;
    - a certificate of origin issued by the chamber of commerce of the country of origin of the
         product or by any other competent body;
    - a tax clearance certificate and the taxpayer's card;
    - the authorisation to carry out a commercial activity in Cameroon issued by the Minister in
         charge of Trade, where appropriate;
    - a test report stating “Slow-release fertiliser" or "Stabilised fertiliser", where appropriate;
    - proof of payment of relevant fees in accordance with the regulations.
The Secretariat may request additional documents or information depending on the specific nature of
the fertiliser.
Article 17: Fertilisers are evaluated based on the following criteria:
    - its efficiency on crops;
    - the Fertiliser does not have a significant direct or indirect negative impact on human, animal,
        or environmental health under the conditions of use;
    - the toxicological and environmental elements can be determined;
    - the nature and quantity of fertilising products, impurities and other toxicologically and eco-
        toxicologically relevant components can be determined using proven methods, where
        appropriate;
    - contaminants resulting from the authorised uses and the toxicological and environmental
        characteristics can be determined using proven methods;
    - the physico-chemical and/or biological properties are considered suitable;
    - the maximum residue levels of contaminants are known, as determined by the Ministry in
        charge of Agriculture or any other relevant international organisation.
SECTION III: ISSUANCE OF FERTILISER MARKETING AUTHORISATIONS
Article 18: (1) In case approval by the Committee, the Minister in charge of Agriculture shall issue a
decision for Marketing Authorisation, valid for five (5) years renewable, or a Provisional Marketing
Authorisation, valid for (2) two years, renewable once.
            (2) The Marketing Authorisation shall grant import or re-export exclusivity to the promoter
of the product or to a local representative.
            (3) However, a compliance analysis shall be carried out systematically for each imported
or locally manufactured batch at the promoter's expense.
SECTION IV: RENEWAL OF MARKETING AUTHORISATIONS
Article 19: (1) Any promoter with a Marketing Authorisation may apply for renewal.
              (2) The application for renewal shall be sent to the Committee at least six (6) months
before the expiry of the Marketing Authorisation and shall contain the following documents and relevant
information:
     - data on human toxicity;
     - available environmental data;
     - data on maximum levels of contaminants;
     - the follow-up report on the use and after-effects of the fertiliser, four (4) years after its
        introduction on the market.
                                                                                             Page | 120
SECTION V: AMENDMENT OR EXTENSION OF MARKETING AUTHORISATIONS
Article 20: (1) The Marketing Authorisation of a fertiliser shall be amended in case of change in the
method of use, the formulation or the target crop.
            (2) In this case, the fertiliser shall be subject to the tests provided for in Article 16 above.

SECTION VI: WITHDRAWAL OF MARKETING AUTHORISATIONS
Article 21: (1) A decision to withdraw a fertiliser Marketing Authorisation or Provisional Marketing
Authorisation shall be based on scientific evidence or the precautionary principle when the product:
    - is no longer effective for the intended use;
    - has adverse effects on plants or plant products under the recommended conditions;
    - has adverse effects on human, animal, or environmental health under normal conditions of
        use;
    - has incomplete or misleading information provided during testing;
    - is withdrawn at the request of the promoter holding the Marketing Authorisation.
            (2) The withdrawal of the Marketing Authorisation or the Provisional Marketing
Authorisation shall be established by decision of the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
SECTION VII: SUSPENSION OF MARKETING AUTHORISATIONS
Article 22: (1) A fertiliser Marketing Authorisation, or Provisional Marketing Authorisation may be
suspended in the event of violation of the legislative and regulatory provisions in force by the promoter
of the said fertiliser.
            (2) The suspension of the Marketing Authorisation or the Provisional Marketing
Authorisation shall be established by decision of the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
SECTION VIII: TRANSFER OF MARKETING AUTHORISATIONS
Article 23: (1) At the request of the promoter, the Marketing Authorisation may be transferred to
another promoter.
For this purpose, he/she must provide the following:
    - an application stamped at the current rate;
    - a copy of the Marketing Authorisation decision;
    - a marketing authorisation transfer agreement, jointly signed by the two parties;
    - a tax clearance certificate;
    - a notarial deed on the transfer of the Marketing Authorisation;
    - proof of payment of application fees.
(2) The transfer of the Marketing Authorisation shall be documented by the issuance of a certificate of
transfer of the Marketing Authorisation signed by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
SECTION IX: CHANGE OR CONTRACT/MANDATE EXTENSION OF LOCAL REPRESENTATIVES
Article 24: (1) The promoter may request the change of his/her local representative. As such, he/she
shall provide the following:
    - an application stamped at the current rate;
    - a tax clearance certificate;
    - a copy of the decision to register the new local representative as a wholesale distributor;
    - a notarial deed on the change or extension of the contract/mandate of the local
        representative.
            (2) The change or mandate/contract extension of the local representative shall be
documented by the issuance of a certificate of change or mandate/contract extension of the local
representative signed by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.




Page | 121
        CHAPTER III: SPECIFICATIONS FOR FERTILIZER SUB-SECTOR STAKEHOLDERS
Article 25: Any natural person or legal entity wishing to market fertilisers shall first register with the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
SECTION I: WHOLESALE DISTRIBUTOR SPECIFICATIONS
Article 26: (1) Natural persons or legal entities wishing to market fertilisers as wholesalers shall meet
the following requirements:
    - employ at least one professional engineer or equivalent in agricultural sciences, unless the
         promoter fulfils this requirement;
    - be registered in the Trade and Personal Property Credit Register at the Registry of the
         competent court, under the conditions provided for by the Uniform Act on General
         Commercial Law;
    - have a storage room that meets current standards;
    - have a sales outlet equipped according to the standards in force for the fertiliser trade;
    - market authorised fertilisers;
    - keep the transaction register (purchase and sale receipts, stock management, etc.;
    - have appropriate materials and equipment (pallets or shelves, adequate lighting, etc.);
    - have, where appropriate, an authorisation to carry out commercial activity issued by the
         Minister in charge of Trade;
    - have a declaration of the start of activity from the Ministry in charge of Trade in accordance
         with the law governing commercial activities in Cameroon;
    - have measuring instruments approved and controlled by the service in charge of legal
         metrology.
               (2) At his/her request, the wholesaler may be authorised to repackage the fertiliser. In
addition to the conditions mentioned in paragraph 1, he/she shall have:
    -   an authorisation from the promoter of the fertiliser;
    -   measuring instruments approved and controlled by the service in charge of legal metrology;
    -   packing machines and packaging that comply with the regulations in force.

SECTION II: RETAIL DISTRIBUTORS SPECIFICATIONS
Article 27: Natural persons or legal entities wishing to market fertilisers as retailer distributors shall
meet the following requirements:
    -   have an employee with minimum level of Agricultural Technician, unless the promoter fulfils
        this requirement;
    -   be registered in the Trade and Personal Property Credit Register at the Registry of the
        competent court, under the conditions provided for by the Uniform Act on General
        Commercial Law;
    -   have, where appropriate, an authorisation to carry out commercial activities issued by the
        Minister in charge of Trade;
    -   have a storage room that meets current standards;
    -   have a sales outlet equipped according to the standards in force for the marketing of
        fertilisers;
    -   market authorised fertilisers;
    -   keep the transaction register (purchase and sale receipts, stock management, etc. ....);
    -   have appropriate materials and equipment (pallets or shelves, adequate lighting, etc.);
    -   have measuring instruments approved and controlled by the service in charge of legal
        metrology.




                                                                                             Page | 122
    CHAPTER IV: PACKAGING AND STORAGE OF MARKETED FERTILISERS
Article 28: (1) Imported, exported or manufactured fertilisers shall be packed in original bags, cans,
drums or bottles in accordance with the regulations in force.
            (2) The packaging shall ensure the safety of the Fertiliser during storage, handling and
transport.
Article 29: The characteristics of the components of the fertiliser offered for sale shall be clearly and
appropriately marked on the label.
Article 30: All fertiliser labels shall have the following information in English and French:
    - the type of fertiliser;
    - the trade name;
    - the type of formulation;
    - fertilising agents, ingredients, or microbial strains and their contents;
    - the net weight or volume;
    - the Marketing Authorisation;
    - the batch number;
    - the date of manufacture;
    - the expiry date, if any;
    - name and address of the manufacturer;
    - the indication "packaged by: name of the packaging company", if applicable;
    - specific instructions for use, safety and incompatibility;
    - caution symbols related to special risks associated with the use of these products;
    - solubility at 25°C.
Article 31: (1) Where plant nutrients appear on a label in any form, they shall be considered declared.
            (2) Primary nutrients shall include total nitrogen (N), available phosphate (P20s) and
soluble potash (K20).
            (3) Secondary nutrients and micro-nutrients shall include the elemental or oxide forms.
Article 32: (1) Fertilisers shall be stored under good conditions to maintain adequate humidity,
temperature, lighting and health conditions. Fertiliser stocks shall be isolated from any product that
could alter the quality of the fertiliser or cause accidents.
             (2) The storage of risk fertilisers shall comply with the safety standards laid down in a
decision of the Minister in charge of Agriculture.


       CHAPTER V: INFRINGEMENTS AND PENALTIES
Article 33: (1) No one shall distribute or sell any fertiliser with misleading marks.
              (2) A fertiliser shall be deemed to have a misleading mark if
    - its label is falsified, inaccurate, illegible, counterfeit or false;
    - it is distributed or sold under the name of another brand of fertiliser;
    - its label does not comply with legal and regulatory requirements.
Article 34: (1) It shall be strictly forbidden to sell fertilisers in packaging other than the original
packaging, except under the conditions defined in Article 27 paragraph 2 above.
           (2) It shall be strictly prohibited to sell fertilisers without registration.
           (3) It shall be strictly prohibited to sell fertilisers lacking declared elements.
           (4) In the three cases referred to above, the fertilisers shall be seized by the relevant
authority.




Page | 123
Article 35: In addition to the provisions of Articles 34 and 35 above, infringements to this Joint Order
shall include the following:
    - the distribution of fertilisers non-compliant with the regulatory requirements;
    - the distribution of fertilisers containing harmful substances or properties, even when used in
         prescribed doses, which may harm plant development, human and animal health and the
         environment;
    - Refusal to comply with registration formalities and submit fertilisers or related documents for
         quality control;
    - refusal to comply with fertiliser use control procedures.
Article 36: Infringements provided for in Articles 34, 35 and 36 above shall be recorded in a report co-
signed by two (02) sworn agents of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture, pursuant to the regulatory
provisions in force.
Article 37: Infringements of Articles 34, 35 and 36 above shall be sanctioned in accordance with the
provisions of Articles 18, 19, 20 and 22 of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the fertiliser
sub-sector in Cameroon.
       CHAPTER VI: FINAL AND TRANSITIONAL PROVISIONS
Article 38: Any holder of a Marketing Authorisation shall communicate to the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture, within three (03) months, any new information on the potential dangers of a fertilising
product or its residues on human, animal, or environmental health.
Article 39: Natural persons or legal entities operating in the fertiliser sub-sector shall comply with this
joint order within a period of six (06) months, from the date of its signature.
Article 40: This joint order shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency and
inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French.
                                       Yaoundé, 29 August 2019

                                                         The Minister of Environment, Protection of
                                                             Nature and Sustainable Development,
 The Minister of Public Health

 MANAOUDA Malachie
