                                                                                              Page | 124
   3.2.2.     Joint Order No. 00116/MINADER/MINCOMMERCE/MINEPDED/MINSANTE of 29
       August 2019 to lay down conditions for the production, import, export, packaging,
       storage and distribution of fertilisers or any other related activities

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT, THE MINISTER OF TRADE,
THE MINISTER OF ENVIRONMENT, PROTECTION OF NATURE AND SUSTAINABLE
DEVELOPMENT AND THE MINISTER OF PUBLIC HEALTH
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 64-LF-23 of 13 November 1964 on the protection of public health;
Mindful of Law No. 96/12 of 05 August 1996 on the framework law on environmental management;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No. 2004/002 of 21 April 2004 governing legal metrology in Cameroon;
Mindful of the framework law No. 2011/012 of 6 May 2011 on consumer protection in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No. 2015/018 of 21 December 2015 governing Commercial activities;
Mindful of Law No. 2016/004 of 18 April 2016 governing foreign trade in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No.2018/020 of 11 December 2018 on the Framework Law on Food Safety;
Mindful of Decree No. 92/089 of 4 May 1992 to specify the duties of the Prime Minister, as amended
and supplemented by Decree No. 95/145 of 4 August 1995;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/421 of 01 October 2012 to organise the Ministry of Environment, Protection
of Nature and Sustainable Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2012/513 of 12 November 2012 to organise the Ministry of Trade;
Mindful of Decree No. 2013/093 of 3 April 2013 to organise the Ministry of Public Health;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/02 of 4 January 2019 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/2584/PM of 23 August 2011 to lay down conditions for the protection of
soils and the sub-soil;
                                 HEREBY ORDER AS FOLLOWS:
       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: This Joint Order lays down conditions for the production, import, export, packaging, storage
and distribution of fertilisers or any other related activities.
          (2) This order is signed pursuant to Article 5 of Law No. 2003/007, regulating the fertiliser
sub-sector in Cameroon.
Article 2: Activities referred to in Article 1 above shall be subject to the legislative and regulatory
provisions in force governing commercial activities, environmental protection, public health, mines and
industries.




Page | 125
Article 3: For the purpose of this joint order, the following definitions shall apply:
    - Related activity: any service that contributes to the functioning of the fertiliser sub-sector.
        This includes: transport, consultancy and research in the fertiliser sub-sector.
    - Soil amendment: any substance used to enhance the physico-chemical and/or biological
        properties of the soil in order to improve its performance.
    - Biostimulants: any substance or microorganism applied to plants to enhance nutrition
        efficiency, abiotic stress tolerance, crop quality traits, and/or the availability of nutrients in the
        soil or rhizosphere, regardless of its nutrients content.
    - Retail distributor: any natural person or legal entity, according to professional standards,
        who purchases fertilisers from wholesale distributors and resells to farmers.
    - Wholesaler distributor: any natural person or legal entity, according to professional
        standards, who purchases fertilisers from producers or imports them, in order to sell in bulk to
        retailers.
    - Packaging: mixing and packing of fertilisers.
    - Distribution: the entire process involving purchase for wholesale or retail, transport, storage
        and preservation of fertilisers.
    - Packaging material: any object, irrespective of the type of material, used to pack and protect
        goods, to enable their handling and transport from producers to consumers.
    - Fertiliser: any substance or material containing one or more plant nutrients recognised and
        used as such to boost plant growth and production. Mineral fertilisers, organic fertilisers,
        organo-mineral fertilisers, biostimulants, soil amendments and all fertilisers are considered as
        fertilisers and referred to as such.
    - Customised fertilisers: any fertiliser prepared based on the customer's formula.
    - Mineral fertilisers: fertilisers of mineral origin, produced by chemical industries or derived
        from phosphate and potash.
    - Organic fertilisers: animal and/or plant fertilisers origin.
    - Registration: registration of a natural person or legal entity operating in the fertiliser sub-
        sector in Cameroon with the competent administration.
    - Export: The act of taking fertilisers out of the national territory into a foreign country.
    - Fertilising agent: any product used to enhance plant nutrition or to improve the physical,
        chemical and biological properties of soils.
    - Formulation: a mixture of several nutrients that make up the fertiliser.
    - Import: the act of introducing fertilisers into the national territory.
    - Production: manufacture of fertilisers.
    - Promoter: any natural person or legal entity who has received an Authorisation to market a
        fertilising agent.
    - Fertiliser sub-sector: A branch of Agriculture, which deals with research, supervision and
        regulation in terms of soil fertility and fertilisation, as well as the import, export, packaging,
        distribution and use of fertilisers. It also includes stakeholders, who contribute to improve soil
        productivity within the framework of sustainable management.
    - Storage the act of stockpiling fertilisers in a restricted common area, for preservation,
        redistribution and/ or use.
    - Intensive use of fertilisers: an agricultural production system essentially based on the use
        of fertilisers and aimed at optimising production.
Article 4: (1) Only fertilisers with Marketing Authorisations or a Provisional Marketing Authorisations shall be
imported, produced, packaged, stored, distributed and used in Cameroon.
(2) However, any farmer can buy customised fertilisers. This requires a special authorisation from the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture.
(3) The special authorisation referred to above shall be subject to the following conditions:
    -   carry out preliminary analyses in order to determine the needs of cultivated soils in deficient fertilising
        agents;


                                                                                                       Page | 126
    -   determine the appropriate formulation;
    -   have the formula validated by the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Article 5: (1) Without prejudice to the legislative and regulatory provisions in force, the Minister in charge of
Agriculture may issue a special authorisation for the importation of fertilisers, not authorised for marketing in
Cameroon, for the following purposes:
    -   research by research institutes and companies operating in the fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon;
    -   agricultural extension;
    -   training support in educational institutions.
The above-mentioned special import authorisation shall stand as a technical opinion in accordance with Article
8(1) of Law No. 2016/004 of 18 April 2016 governing foreign trade in Cameroon.
        CHAPTER II: CONDITIONS FOR THE PRODUCTION, FORMULATION, IMPORT, EXPORT,
        PACKAGING, STORAGE AND DISTRIBUTION OF FERTILISERS
SECTION I: PRODUCTION AND FORMULATION OF FERTILISERS
Article 6: (1) Any natural person or legal entity involved in the production and/or formulation of
fertilisers shall register with the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
(2) For that purpose, the following conditions shall apply:
    -   employ at least one engineer or equivalent in agricultural sciences, unless the promoter fulfils
        this requirement;
    -   employ technician(s) with expertise in production and/or formulation processes;
    -   have one or more storage rooms that meet the required standards;
    -   have a contract or contracts with suppliers of raw materials;
    -   have fertilisers tested;
    -   have production and/or formulation protocols;
    -   have production and/or formulation standards that comply with the laws in force;
    -   have the appropriate work equipment (measuring instruments approved and controlled by the
        service in charge of legal metrology, testing equipment, storage, mixing, packing, transport,
        etc.);
    -   have an internal quality control unit (production control, analysis of manufactured fertilisers)
        where appropriate;
    -   have appropriate materials and equipment (pallets or shelves, adequate lighting, etc.);
    -   have infrastructure that meet required standards;
    -   keep the transaction register (purchase and sale receipts, stock management, etc. ....);
    -   have a certificate of compliance and an environmental licence or environmental note as
        appropriate;
    -   have a safety protocol and related equipment;
    -   have insurance covering fire and other risks;
    -   have a health insurance policy for staff;
    -   submit an inspection report on the premises issued by the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
(3) The internal fertiliser quality control units aim at ensuring that the fertiliser produced or formulated
complies with the declared parameters.
(4) The registration referred to in paragraph 1 above shall be valid for a period of 5 years renewable.




Page | 127
SECTION II: IMPORT OF FERTILISERS
Article 7: Without prejudice to the provisions of Articles 5 and 6 above, any natural person or legal entity wishing
to import fertilisers shall first register as a fertiliser Wholesale distributor, in accordance with the regulations in
force.
Article 8: (1) The import of fertilisers shall be subject to an Import Declaration submitted to the Ministry in charge
of Agriculture.
(2) This is exclusively reserved for of the Marketing Authorisation / Provisional Marketing Authorisation holders
or their local representatives.
(3) The Import Declaration, provided for in paragraph 1 above, is subject to the presentation of the following
documents:
       a) an application stamped at the current rate with the following information:
     - the address of the importer or supplier, as well as the distributor;
     - the expected date of arrival of the fertiliser and the point of entry into Cameroon;
     - the trade name, formulation, packaging, quantities and type of fertiliser to be imported and the country
         of origin;
     - the mode of transportation;
       b) a copy of the Marketing Authorisation or Provisional Marketing Authorisation decision;
       c) a pro forma invoice;
       d) a tax clearance certificate.
       e) proof of payment of declaration fees at the applicable rate.
       f) a copy of the certificate of registration in the importers' register issued by the service in charge of
            foreign trade.
(4) The file shall be submitted to the Department in charge of regulations and quality control of fertilisers at least
one month before the expected date of shipment.
(5) In case the file is submitted electronically via the Technical Administration Information System, the
composition of the file shall remain as referred to in paragraph 2 above.
              (6) The Import Declaration Receipt shall be issued to the applicant upon fulfilment of the above
conditions.
Article 9: (1) Wholesaler-Distributors shall provide the Ministry in charge of Agriculture every six months with
data on the type, quantities and value of fertilisers effectively imported for the purpose of compiling statistics.
            (2) Failure to comply with the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the Wholesaler distributor shall be
exposed to the penalties provided for by the regulations in force.
SECTION III: EXPORT OF FERTILISERS
Article 10: (1) Export of fertilisers shall be subject to a declaration submitted to the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture.
              (2) The Export Declaration, provided for in Paragraph 1 above, shall be subject to the presentation
of the following documents:
          a) an application stamped at the current rate with the following information:
           • the address of the exporter or supplier, as well as the consignee;
           • the expected date of export of the fertiliser and the exit point in Cameroon;
           • the trade name, formulation, packaging, quantities and type of fertiliser to be exported and
               the country of destination;
           • the mode of transportation.
          b) a copy of the Marketing Authorisation or Provisional Marketing Authorisation decision.
          c) a tax clearance certificate.
          d) proof of payment of declaration fees at the applicable rate.
          e) a copy of the certificate of registration in the database of exporters issued by the service in charge
              of foreign trade.
(3) The file shall be submitted to the Department in charge of regulations and quality control of fertilisers at least
one month before the expected date of shipment.
(4) In case the file is submitted electronically via the Technical Administration Information System, the
composition of the file shall remain as referred to in paragraph 2 above.
(5) The Export Declaration Receipt shall be issued to the applicant upon fulfilment of the above conditions.


                                                                                                         Page | 128
Article 11: Fertilisers in transit within the national territory shall be transported in sealed containers.
SECTION IV: PACKAGING AND STORAGE OF FERTILISERS
Article 12: (1) Any natural person or legal entity involved in the packaging and/or storage of fertilisers
shall register with the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
(2) For that purpose, the following conditions shall apply:
    - employ at least one engineer or equivalent in agricultural sciences, unless the promoter fulfils
         this requirement;
    - employ technician(s) with expertise in packaging and/or storage of fertilisers;
    - have one or more storage rooms that meet the required standards;
    - have a contract or contracts with suppliers of raw materials;
    - have fertilisers tested;
    - have packaging and/or storage protocols;
    - have packaging and/or storage standards that comply with the laws in force;
    - have the appropriate work equipment (measuring instruments approved and controlled by the
         service in charge of legal metrology, testing equipment, storage, mixing, packing, transport,
         etc.);
    - have appropriate materials and equipment (pallets or shelves, adequate lighting, etc.);
    - have infrastructure that meet required standards;
    - keep the transaction register (purchase and sale receipts, stock management, etc.);
    - have a certificate of compliance and an environmental licence or environmental note as
         appropriate;
    - have a safety protocol and related equipment;
    - have insurance covering fire and other risks;
    - have a health insurance policy for staff;
    - submit an inspection report on the premises issued by the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
(3) The registration referred to in Paragraph 1 above shall be valid for a period of 5 years renewable.
Article 13: The packaging and storage of fertilisers shall comply with the standards and regulations
in force.
SECTION V: DISTRIBUTION OF FERTILISERS
Article 14: Any natural person or legal entity wishing to distribute fertilisers shall first register as a
Wholesaler or Retailer, in accordance with the specifications set out in the regulations in force.

SECTION VI: TRANSFER OF PRODUCTION, FORMULATION, PACKAGING AND DISTRIBUTION UNITS
Article 15: (1) The transfer of a production, formulation, packaging or distribution unit from one place
to another or any change in the conditions of installation shall be authorised by the Ministry in charge
of Agriculture. Failure do so shall result in the withdrawal of the registration.
            (2) The authorisation for site transfer shall be subject to prior validation of the report on the
restoration of the site previously occupied by the Ministry in charge of environment.
SECTION VII: INTENSIVE USE OF FERTILISERS
Article 16: (1) Any natural person or legal entity using fertilisers intensively on an area of more than
10 hectares shall declare their activities to the competent Regional Delegate of agriculture.
(2) For that purpose, they shall provide:
    - a plan of the farm;
    - fertiliser use protocols.
(3) A Receipt for the declaration of activity shall be issued to the applicant upon fulfilment of the above
conditions by the Regional Delegate and sent to the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.




Page | 129
        CHAPTER III: REQUIREMENTS FOR CARRYING OUT RELATED ACTIVITIES
SECTION I: TRANSPORT OF FERTILISERS
Article 1 7: Fertilisers shall be transported in accordance with the procedures and standards provided
for by the regulations in force.
SECTION II: CONSULTING AND RESEARCH
Article 18 (1) Any natural person or legal entity wishing to be approved for agronomic consulting on
the use of fertilisers and research in the fertiliser sub-sector shall meet the following requirements:
    - employ at least one engineer or equivalent in agricultural sciences, unless the promoter fulfils
        this requirement;
    - have a suitable building;
    - have, where appropriate, an authorisation to carry out commercial activity issued by the
        Minister in charge of Trade.
    CHAPTER IV: INFRINGEMENTS AND PENALTIES
Article 19: Infringements of this Joint Order shall include the following:
    -   the production, distribution and/or use of fertilisers non compliant with the regulatory requirements;
    -   the production, distribution and/or use of fertilisers containing harmful substances or properties, even
        when used in prescribed doses, which may harm plant development, human and animal health and the
        environment;
    -   Refusal to comply with registration formalities and submit fertilisers or related documents for quality
        control;
    -   refusal to comply with fertiliser use control procedures.
Article 20: Infringements provided for in Article 20 above shall be recorded in a report co-signed by
two (02) sworn agents of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture, pursuant to the regulatory provisions in
force.
Article 21: Infringements of Article 20 above shall be sanctioned in accordance with the provisions of
Articles 18, 19, 20 and 22 of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the fertiliser sub-sector in
Cameroon.
        CHAPTER V: FINAL AND TRANSITIONAL PROVISIONS
Article 22: Without prejudice to the provisions of the law governing foreign trade in Cameroon, the
import of fertilisers for purposes other than agriculture shall be subject to a special authorisation issued
by the Minister in charge of foreign trade after consultation with the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Article 23: Natural persons or legal entities operating in the fertiliser sub-sector shall comply within a
period of six (06) months, from the date of signature of this joint order.
Article 24: This joint order shall be registered, published according to the procedure of urgency and
inserted in the Official Gazette in English and French. / -

                                                                                                 Page | 132
   3.3.2.      Decision No. 00263/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRP/SRE/IE2 to lay down procedures
       for quality control of fertilisers for sale and the registration of operators in the fertiliser
       sub-sector in Cameroon

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 96/12 of 5 August 1996 on the framework law on environmental management;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon;
Mindful of Law No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to reorganise the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2015/434 of 2 October 2015 to reshuffle the Government;
Considering service needs,
                                   HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: This decision lays down the procedures for quality control of fertilisers for sale and the
registration of operators in the fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon.
Article 2: The fertiliser sub-sector is part of the agricultural sector involving fertiliser activities such as
manufacturing, import, export, packaging, distribution and use, as well as research, supervision and
regulation on soil fertility and fertilisation.
Article 3: Any natural person or legal entity carrying out an activity in the fertiliser sub-sector in
Cameroon shall register with the competent services of the Department in charge of the regulation of
fertilisers in the Register of operators of the fertiliser sub-sector.
CHAPTER II: QUALITY CONTROL PROCEDURES FOR FERTILISERS FOR SALE
Article 4: Within the framework of this decision, the following shall be considered as fertilisers:
    -   Soil conditioners;
    -   Growth stimulators
    -   Mineral fertilisers;
    -   Organic fertilisers;
    -   Organo-mineral fertilisers.
Article 5: Quality control of a fertiliser for use in Cameroon shall include the following two phases,
depending on whether it is locally manufactured or imported:
    - Assessment prior to the issuance of a distribution licence;
    - Control during manufacture, control at the various exit and entry points of the country, control
        of compliance with packaging, distribution and use standards.


Article 6: (1) The fertiliser evaluation procedure shall include a set of operations aimed at ensuring
that any fertiliser for use in Cameroon contributes to:
    - Increase the yield of the target crop;
    - Improve the physico-chemical and/or microbiological properties of the soil;
    - Provide economic added value to the user.



Page | 133
Article 7: (2) This shall include the following operations:
    - The physico-chemical and microbiological testing of a sample of the fertiliser by a laboratory
        approved by the Minister in charge of Agriculture;
    - Agronomic efficiency tests by the service in charge of fertiliser regulation;
    - The evaluation of the agro-economic performance of the said fertiliser by the service in
        charge of fertiliser regulation.
Article 8: The fertiliser promoter shall submit to the competent authority a file including:
    -   an application stamped at the current rate;
    -   A technical file;
    -   Samples;
    -   Receipts for payment of assessment fees.
Article 9: (1) The tests and analyses in Article 6 paragraph 1 shall result in the issuance of a certificate
of conformity, a certificate of quality, a test report answering the main questions of the experiment, and
an agro-economic evaluation form duly signed by the competent services of the Minister in charge of
Agriculture.
CHAPITRE III: FERTILISER REGISTRATION PROCEDURES
Article 10: The Register of operators of the fertiliser sub-sector shall comprise several categories of
professionals, namely:
    -   The register of fertiliser importers;
    -   The register of fertiliser exporters;
    -   The register of fertiliser distributors;
    -   The register of fertiliser manufacturers;
    -   The register of fertiliser formulators;
    -   The register of fertiliser packagers.
Article 11: Any natural person or legal entity wishing to work as a professional in one of the categories
listed in Article 9 shall declare his or her activity to the Department in charge of Regulations, Quality
Control and Certification of Agricultural Inputs and Products.
Article 12: (1) The declaration shall be subject to the production of a complete file (see Appendices 1
and 2) for the desired professional category.
           (2) In addition to the complete file, the applicant shall:
    - have facilities, equipment and appliances that meet standards;
    - have appropriate personal protective equipment for personnel;
    - have qualified staff with technical and practical skills in handling fertilisers
    - undertake to use only fertilisers authorised to be marketed in Cameroon;
    - undertake to comply with the regulations in force.
Article 13: (1) The registration of a natural person or legal entity shall be effective following the
issuance of the decision to register the operators of the fertiliser sub-sector the Minister in charge of
Agriculture.
             (2) The decision on registration in the register of operators of the fertiliser sub-sector
referred to in subsection (1) shall contain the registration number of the person registered.
Article 14: (1) The import of fertilisers shall be authorised by the fertiliser regulatory authority.



                                                                                               Page | 134
CHAPTER IV: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 15: (1) The import of fertilisers shall be authorised by the fertiliser regulatory authority.
             (2) The marketing of a batch of fertiliser shall be effective after conformity and quality
control.
             (3) This shall be reserved exclusively for companies with a marketing authorisation.
However, in special cases, the Ministry in charge of Agriculture may import organic fertilisers into
Cameroon.
Article 16: (1) The costs of the fertiliser assessment procedure shall be covered by the promoters of
the products.
             (2) the costs of the said tests shall be determined by decision of the Minister in charge of
Agriculture.
Article 17: This decision, which enters into force from the date of signature, shall be registered and
published wherever necessary.


                                                                              Yaounde, 21 March 2017
                                                The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                  Henri EYEBE AYISSI




Page | 135
   3.3.3.      Decision No. 00600/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRP/SRE of 16 November 2020
       establishing the list of fertilisers exempted from agronomic efficiency tests

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/007 of 10 July 2003 regulating the fertiliser sub-sector in Cameroon;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and supplemented by Decree
No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018,
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/02 of 4 January 2019 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Joint Order No.0015 MINADER/MINCOMMERCE /MINEPDED /MINSANTE of 31 August 2022 laying down
conditions for the marketing of fertilisers in Cameroon;
Mindful of Joint Order No.00116 MINADER/MINCOMMERCE/MINEPDED/MINSANTE of 29 August 2019 laying down
conditions for the production, import, export, packaging, storage and distribution of fertilisers or any other related activities.
                                       HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: This Decision establishes the list of fertilisers exempted from agronomic efficiency tests.
Article 2: The fertilisers exempted from agronomic efficiency tests are listed in the table below:
 No.      Fertiliser
 1.       Borax [NaB4O7.H2O,]
 2.       Crushed limestone [CaCO3 +MgCO3]
 3.       Calcium lime [CaCO3 + MgCO3]
 4.       Magnesian lime [CACO3 + MgCO3]
 5.       Quicklime (CaCO3]
 6.       Calcium chloride (CaCl2]
 7.       Potassium chloride [KCL]
 8.       Di-ammonium phosphate (DAP) [(NH4)2HPO4]
 9.       Dolomite [CaCO3 + MgCO3]
 10.      Kieserite [MgSO4.H2O]
 11.      Mono-ammonium phosphate (MAP) [(NH4)H2PO4]
 12.      Ammonium nitrate [NH4NO3]
 13.      Calcium nitrate [Ca (NO3)2]
 14.      Potassium nitrate [KNO3]
 15.      NPK 20-10-10 complex
 16.      Magnesium oxide [MgO]
 17.      Tricalcium phosphate [Ca3(PO4)2]
 18.      Rock phosphate
 19.      Ammonium sulphate [(NH4)2SO4]
 20.      Magnesium sulfate [MgSO4]
 21.      Manganese sulphate [MnSO4]
 22.      Potassium sulphate [K2SO4]
 23.      Zinc sulphate [ZnSO4]
 24.      Enriched superphosphate (ESP)
 25.      Single superphosphate (SSP)
 26.      Triple superphosphate (TSP)
 27.      Composts
 28.      Any fertiliser consisting of one or two nutrient(s) (major, micro, trace, or heavy metals within the required limits)
 29.      Any single-use amendment product
 30.      Urea [CO(NH2)2]


Article 3: This decision shall be registered and communicated wherever necessary. /-

                                                            The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                                        Gabriel MBAIROBE

                                                                                                                   Page | 136
   3.4. CIRCULAR




Page | 137
   3.4.1.     Circular Letter No. 00001/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRP/SRE of 24 May 2021 on the
       presentation of application files for marketing authorisation for fertilisers.

                 THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
                                                    TO
                ALL ECONOMIC OPERATORS IN THE FERTILISER SUB-SECTOR
Ladies, Gentlemen,
It has been brought to my attention that most files submitted to the Ministry for marketing authorisations
do not comply with relevant regulatory instruments.
In order to harmonise the composition of files so as to t facilitate processing during sessions, I have
the honour to remind you that, in addition to the use of spirals and separators, the documents of the
file shall be classified as follows:
   1) An application for examination of the fertiliser stamped at the current rate, addressed to the
      Ministry in charge of Agriculture;
   2) Content;
   3) Proof of payment of study fees in accordance with the regulations;
   4) Material Safety Data (a form indicating physical, physico-chemical and microbiological
      properties, information form on safety measures, indications and contra-indications,
      intoxication, antidote (if applicable), a full report on the hazard profile for mammals, a full report
      on the risks of the fertiliser to the environment);
   5) Certificate of compliance issued by the Director of Regulations and Quality Control of Fertilisers;
   6) Original labelling template or its label;
   7) Description of the packaging;
   8) Agronomic efficiency test report (if applicable);
   9) Instructions for use (final).
In addition to the documents listed above, you are requested to provide the results of solubility and
mobility analyses.
I attach great importance to the strict implementation of the provisions of this circular.
                                                                                  Yaounde, 24 May 2021

                                                 The minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                     Gabriel MBAIROBE
