Page | 141
4.2. LAW




           Page | 142
   4.2.1.       Law N°2003/O03 OF 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection

The National Assembly has deliberated and adopted;
The President of the Republic hereby enacts the law set out below:
        CHAPTER I- GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: The present law institutes the main rules governing phytosanitary inspections in Cameroon.
Article 2. - Phytosanitary inspection is done through:
    -   the elaboration, the adoption and the adaptation of standards in the domain;
    -   the prevention and fight against enemies of plants and plant products;
    -   the appropriate use of phytosanitary products without any danger on humans, animals and the
        environment ;
    -   the dissemination and extension of appropriate techniques of phytosanitary protection;
    -   the control on the importation and exportation of phytosanitary products, plants, plant products and other
        regulated articles that can cause the spread of pests and diseases;
    -   the control of phytosanitary products, plants and plant products that can serve as diseases and pests
        vectors in the national territory.

Article 3 - Within this law and its implementation texts, the following definitions are upheld:
« phytosanitary activity»: all operations related to the production, the distribution and the use of plant
products and phytosanitary products in the farm, in store and for the spraying of stores and
transportation means.
« regulated items »: all plants, plant product, storage places, bags, transportation means, containers,
soils, all other organisms, objects or material that can carry or spread diseases and pests within
phytosanitary measures related to international transport.
« auxiliary » pests and disease organisms that live depending on another and that can limit the
population of its hosts. These include parasitoids, parasites, predators and pathogenic agents.
« Phytosanitary certificate »: an official document with an international convention format used for
plant protection and testifying the sanitary state of a parcel that must pass through phytosanitary
control.
«Certificate of conformity to norms »: an official document issued by the national organ on
normalisation and testifying that the evaluated product is in conformity with norms.
« registration »: it is a procedure through which the competent authority accepts the importation
distribution and use of a phytosanitary product after examining the scientific data to indicate that the
product is efficient for its purpose and does not present any risk for the environment, human beings
and animals.
« inspection »: official visual examination of plants, plant products and other regulated items in order
to determine the presence or absence of pest/disease organisms and/ or to ensure that phytosanitary
regulation is respected.
« quarantine organism »: a pest/disease organism which has a high economic potential in a risk area and which
is not yet present but not yet widely disseminated in the area and which can be fought against.
« pest/disease organism »: any specie, part or biotype of a plant or animal as well as pathogenic agent, that
can disturb plants or plant products.
«pesticide»: any substance or combination of substances used to discard, destroy or fight against insects,
disease vectors, and undesired plant varieties or animals that can cause damage or show signs of destruction
either during production, processing, storage, transportation or marketing of food products agricultural products,
wood and non-timber forest products.

« Phytosanitary product »: pesticides or other substances to be used as plant growth regulators, a

Page | 143
defoliator, desiccant agents, fruit lighteners or to prevent premature fruit fall, as well as substances
applied on plants either before or after harvest to prevent damage during storage and transportation.
« plant product»: any product of plant origin, including pollens and manufactured products which,
because of their nature or their processing, can constitute a risk of introducing or spreading pests.
« plant quarantine » a series of activities that aim at preventing the introduction and/or the spread of
pests or at fighting against them officially.
« quarantine zone » a demarcated area within which a quarantine organism is found and needs to be
officially fought against.
Article 4 -The phytosanitary activity is carried out freely in the entire nation by any physical person or
corporate body in the respect of the present law and regulations.
Article 5 -The competent authority in the domain of phytosanitary protection is the Minister in charge
of Agriculture. He can however delegate powers to any person or corporate body as provided for in the
regulations.
Article 6 - The Minister in charge of Agriculture can ask for assistance from other competent
departments for a better implementation of the phytosanitary law.
       CHAPTER II - ON PHYTOSANITARY PROTECTION OF THE NATIONAL
                                               TERRITORY
                                 I- Applicable phytosanitary measures
Article 7- Applicable phytosanitary measures in Cameroon were elaborated and put into effect
following the national regulation and international standards on the domain.
                                           II - Plant quarantine
Article 8 - (1) It is either prohibited or introduced, to keep or to transport any quarantine organism in
the national territory, no matter their state of development.
 (2) The list of quarantine organisms is regularly updated and published by order from the Minister in
charge of Agriculture.
(3) However, some dispensations may be granted for research, experiments or training purposes.
Article 9 - (1) It is prohibited to import or export plants or plant parts, soils and culture mediums infested
by pests. During importation or exportation, plants, plants products, soil and culture mediums must be
accompanied by a phytosanitary certificate.
(2) On the other hand, the importation of regulated plants, plant products or biological control agents
depend on the acquisition of an importation permit which is issued under special conditions provided
for by law.
Article 10 - (1) The importation or exportation of plants and plant products must be done only at official
entry and exit points.
(2) The importation of plants and plant products that are subject to import license and especially the
importation of plant material to be used for multiplication or planting must be done only at the entry
point mentioned on the import license. These products and materials can previously be put in a
quarantine station of an intermediary country or in the country of origin.
Article 11 - The phytosanitary certificate and the importation permit mentioned in article 9 above are
issued at the expense of' the applicant by the services of the phytosanitary police post.
Article 12 - The Minister of Agriculture issues as the need arises the list of plants, plant parts and plant
products whose importation is prohibited or restricted in Cameroon due to their origin.

                                                                                                 Page | 144
Article 13 - In case a non-classified pest is introduced, propagated or discovered; the competent
authority must take complementary phytosanitary measures on the matter.
Article 14 - (1) In case of the presence or assumed presence of a quarantine organism in one part of
the country, the area will be declared a quarantine zone as per regulation until there is total control or
eradication of the organism.
(2) During this period, all movement of plants, plant parts or soil out of this area is prohibited.
                                    III - Phytosanitary supervision
Article 15 - Any physical person or corporate body who has or is exploiting an area, or has plant
products or items in store and observes or suspects the presence of a quarantine organism or any
other pest should immediately inform the competent phytosanitary authority of his area.
Article 16 - Any physical person or corporate body exploiting either in a rural or urban area must make
sure of a good sanitary state of crops he plants, stores, transports or sells.
Article 17 - (1) The Minister of Agriculture puts in place a supervisory team on the appearance of
pests, the evolution of the population and warning on attacks.
(2) He updates and publishes the list of pests.
Article 18 - Some parts of the country can be classified as risk zones or prohibited zones by law.
                                     IV - Phytosanitary application
Article 19 - (1) Chemical treatments must be carried out in accordance with appropriate agricultural
practices as prescribed by the competent authority in order to preserve human and animal health as
well as protect the environment from the dangers of the accumulation of the residues of phytosanitary
products.
(2) The method of the treatment of stored food stuff must guarantee the absence or presence of the
tolerated level of phytosanitary products and preserve the organoleptic quality of the treated products.
Article 20 - (1) Any physical person or corporate body interested in doing phytosanitary treatment as
a professional must first of all seek an authorization from the competent authority.
(2) The conditions for issuing the authorization are laid down by law.
   CHAPTER III: THE USE, INSPECTION AND CONTROL OF PHYTOSANITARY PRODUCTS
                            I-THE USE OF PHYTOSANITARY PRODUCTS
Article 21 - (1) Only registered products or those that have been temporarily authorised to be sold
must be imported, distributed, packaged or used in Cameroon.
(2) The conditions for the application of sub-section (1) above are laid down by law.
Article 22 - (1) Phytosanitary products must be sold and used in their original bags and under their
original labels.
 (2) The norms for bagging and labelling as well as the conditions for formulation and packaging of
phytosanitary products are laid down by law.
Article 23 - (1) The use of phytosanitary products under conditions different from those specified at
the time of their registration is prohibited.
(2) Special measures may be taken by law for the protection of auxiliaries and pollinating.
Article 24 - The sale of obsolete phytosanitary products must be declared to the authority in charge of
phytosanitary issues on the first day of their expiry date.


Page | 145
Article 25 - (1) Any individual or corporate body interested in the execution of professional
phytosanitary activities pertaining to manufacturing, importation, exportation, formulation, packaging
and distribution must first be authorized.
(2) The conditions of agreement here mentioned in (1) above are laid down by law.
Article 26 - Any advertisement on a non-authorised phytosanitary product is prohibited.
Article 27 - (1) Phytosanitary treatment equipment, fabricated, authorised and distributed in Cameroon
must be certified for their conformity to the standards in force.
(2) The conditions for the certification of phytosanitary treatment equipment are laid down by law.
                       II - Inspection and control of phytosanitary products
Article 28- All plants, plants products, soils and culture medium, biological control. organisms as well
as phytosanitary products are subject:
   -   to phytosanitary inspection, no matter their place of production, multiplication, method of
       storage and transport;
   -   to control during fabrication, importation, exportation. packaging, distribution and usage.
Article 29- Phytosanitary inspection and the control of phytosanitary products are carried out with
respect to national norms, the inspection procedural code adopted by the national phytosanitary
authority.
Article 30- Any individual or corporate body possessing plants, plant products or phytosanitary
products must declare them to agents of phytosanitary inspection and control of phytosanitary products
at the points of entry and exit of the national territory.


       CHAPTER IV - SANCTIONNING DEFAULTERS
                                      I - Procedure for sanctions
Article 31- Without any prejudice to recognise prerogatives of the Legal Department and to the general
competence of the Judicial Police, the sworn agents on phytosanitary inspection are charged with
inspecting, reporting and the follow-up of the prosecution of defaulters in the phytosanitary domain.
To this effect, they report the facts, seize plants, plant products or biological control agents introduced
without the certificate or importation permit, phytosanitary products sold in bulk on stages, as well as
those introduced or used contrarily to the provisions of the present law through the establishment of a
report.
The written and signed report by the sworn officials is in respect of all observations taken up till all
wrong registrations.
Article 32- The sworn agents charged with phytosanitary inspection and the control of phytosanitary
products, in the execution of the functions and following the procedures provided for in the present law,
may:
   -   need the assistance of the public in the search and measure of plant and phytosanitary products
       introduced, sold or circulating fraudulently or for the abstention of the identity of the defaulter;
   -   visit places, trains, ships, vehicles, or air craft keeping, transporting the said products;
   -   order the treatment, put into quarantine, destroy or seize plants or plants products;
   -   reject all seals.



                                                                                              Page | 146
                                     II - Offences and sanctions
Article 33- Any person involved with the following offences will be fined with the sum of 50000 Frs:
   -   failure to present a phytosanitary certificate;
   -   failure to declare plants, plant products or phytosanitary products;
   -   failure to declare obsolete phytosanitary product.
Article 34- Whoever introduces regulated plants, plant products or agents of biological control without
the importation permit will be punished with a prison term of one (1) to three (3) months and a fine of
one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) francs CFA.
Article 35 - Whoever violates the provisions of articles 20 and 26 of the present law shall be punished
with a prison term of either one (1) or three (3) months or a fixed amount of one hundred (100,000) to
one million (1,000,000) francs CFA.
Article 36 - (1) Anyone who, by dishonesty, negligence or non-respect of the regulations, causes
pollution before, during and after a phytosanitary treatment will be punished according to the previsions
laid down in article 261 of the penal code.
(2) Any person who causes intoxication on any humans leading to incapacity will be sanctioned
according to previsions of article 289 (1) of the penal code.
       CHAPTER V: GENERAL AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 37 - (1) There will be a National Phytosanitary Council, a consultative organ responsible for the
policy of plant protection in Cameroon.
 (2) The composition, the missions and functioning modalities of the National Phytosanitary Council
are laid down by law.
Article 38 - The previsions of law N°90/ 013 of 10th August 1990 on phytosanitary protection have
been taken care of.
Article 39 - This law shall be registered, published according to the emergency procedure and
inserted in the official Gazette in English and French.


                                                                              Yaounde, the 21 April 2003
                                                                       The President of the Republic
                                                                                             Paul BIYA




Page | 147
4.3. DECREES




               Page | 148
   4.3.1.    Decree no. 2005/0769 /PM of 06 April 2005 on the organization of the national
       phytosanitary council

                      THE PRIME MINSTER AND THE HEAD OF GOVERNMENT
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No.92/089 of 4 May 1992 to specify the duties of the Prime Minister modified and
completed by decree No 95/145 of 5 August 1995;
Mindful of decree No 2004/320 of 8 December 2004 bearing the appointment of a Prime Minister.
                                            DECREES:
       CHAPTER I - GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1 - This present decree bears the organization of the National Phytosanitary Council,
hereinafter referred to as the "Council".
Article 2- (1) Placed under the Minister charged with Agriculture, the Council shall be a Consultative
organ on plant protection policy in Cameroon.
(2) In this respect, it shall:
   -   advice the government on all matters relating to a sustainable development of the phytosanitary
       activity:
   -   issue an opinion on all the legislation or regulatory drafts, on all technical;
   -   directives as well as on general measures envisaged by the Administration concerning crop
       protection;
   -   make suggestions to the Minister in charge of Agriculture on the organization and regulation of
       the sector;
   -   give an opinion on the implementation of phytosanitary policy:
   -   Make decisions on all issues pertaining to crop protection.


   CHAPTER II - ORGANISATION AND FUNCTIONING OF THE COUNCIL
Article 3 - (1) Presided by the Minister in charge of Agriculture or his representative, the Council shall
comprise among others the following members:
   -   three (03) representatives of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture;
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Public Health;
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Finance
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Livestock;
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Commerce
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Nature Protection;
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge Justice;
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge Transport;
   -   one (01) representative of the Ministry in charge of Scientific Research and Innovation:
   -   Two (02) representatives of the Phytosanitary Industry sector;
   -   one (01) representative of the Agro-Industry Sector:
   -   one representative of the producers organization
   -   one (01) representative of phytosanitary treatment and sanitation companies
   -   Two (02) representatives of professional agricultural products exporters;
   -   Two (02) representatives of Non-Governmental Organizations respectively involved in
       consumer and environmental protection.


Page | 149
 (2) The President of the Council may invite any individual or corporate body according to his
competence in some matters on the agenda of the session to a meeting, in a consultative capacity.
(3) Members of the Council shall be appointed by an Order of the Minister in charge of Agriculture upon
the recommendations of the Administration and the Organizations to which they belong.
(4) The secretariat of the Council shall be ensured by the Department for Regulation and quality control
of agricultural products in the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Article 4 - (1) The Council shall meet at least twice (02) a year on the convocation of its President.
(2) The convocation notice shall reach members fifteen (15) days before the start of the session. It
shall indicate the time, date, and venue of the meeting and shall be accompanied by the working
documents for the session.
(3) Decisions of the Council! shall be taken by a simple majority of the members present. The vote of
the President shall be decisive in the event of a tie.
ARTICLE 5 - At the end of every session, the Council shall submit a written report to the Minister in
charge of Agriculture.


       CHAPTER III: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 6 - (1) The duties of the President and the Council members shall be gratuitous.
(2) However, Council Members as well as invited persons to a session shall benefit from working
facilities necessary for the accomplishment of their missions.
Article 7 - The functioning expenditure of the Council shall be borne by the budget of the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture.
ARTICLE 8 - This decree shall be registered and published following the urgency procedure then,
inserted in the Official Gazette in French and English.
                                                                                 Yaounde, 6 April 2005
                                              .            The Prime Minister, Head of Government
                                                                                        INONI Ephraim
                                                                                                         .




                                                                                            Page | 150
   4.3.2.       Decree no. 2005/0770/ PM of 06 April 2005 on the conditions for crop protection

                          THE PRIME MINISTER, HEAD OF GOVERNMENT.
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on crop protection;
Mindful of Decree No 92/089 of 4 May 1992 to precise the duties of the Prime Minister modified and
completed by Decree No 95/145 of August 1995;
Mindful of Decree No 2004/320 of 8th December 2004 organizing the Government;
Mindful of Decree No 2004/320 of 8th December 2004 appointing a Prime Minister;
                                               DECREES:
       CHAPTER I - GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1 - The present decree fixes condition of crop protection.
Article 2 - Crop protection is done through the fight against major pests or against other
organisms harmful to plants.
Article 3 – Within the context of this decree, the following shall mean:
   -   Biological fighting agent: an auxiliary antagonist, competitor or any other biological entity
       able to self- reproduce, used in the fight against harmful organisms.
   -   Good phytosanitary practices: expression regrouping methods of fighting against harmful
       organisms to plants with the aim of preserving a good healthy state of plant and plant product
       human and animal health as well as the environment from hazards resulting from the use of
        phytosanitary products and measures.
   -   Integrated protection: An approach aimed at increasing agricultural production based on
       ecological principles and which reinforces farmers' capacity of promoting plant health in a
       balanced agro-ecological system variably using economically available technologies especially
       in plant selection, biological fight, cultivation methods and regulatory measures.

   CHAPTER II - PROTECTION AGAINST HARMFUL ORGANISMS
                                          I - Biological means
Article 4 - (1) Biological PROTECTION shall be realized by research institutes or other Agricultural
institutes offering plant protection services, agricultural extension services, farmers and famer’s
organization as well as other concerned Administration.
Article 5 - Any institution desiring to promote the use of foreign biological fighting agents must obtain
an authorization from the Minister in charge of Agriculture. To this effect, the application file for the
authorization shall comprise the following documents:
A stamped application;
An introduction programme which spells out the following:
   -    preliminary studies on the identity of the harmful organisms, pertinent information on it's origin
        and the importance of the damage caused as well as experiences acquired elsewhere with
        harmful organisms;
    - results of inquiries and surveys in the areas of origin of the biological fighting agent;
    - description of the quarantine unit;
    - the production and release methods of the biological fighting agent.
Article 6 - Before undertaking any release, the promoter shall make sure that the damage caused by
the harmful organism justifies the effort to fight and that the introduction of a biological fighting agent
constitutes a judicious approach.


Page | 151
Article 7 - (1) The exotic biological fighting agent shall first of all be confirmed prior to their production and release
on a large scale.
(2) Releases shall be done in precise zones on the authorization of the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
(3) The release conditions shall be fixed and defined by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Article 8 - After every release, the promoters shall evaluate the impact of the biological fighting agents both on
the population of the harmful organism as well as on the non-target organisms.
                                         II - Integrated protection of crops
Article 9 - The fight against harmful organisms shall be done following the integrated protection principles with
the aim of:
    -    reducing dependence vis-à-vis the phytosanitary products;
    -    exercising a better control on the use of phytosanitary products;
    -    Reducing the risks related to abusive and inappropriate use of phytosanitary products.
Article 10 - The implementation of the integrated fight and the establishment in priority of this approach implies
the participation of farmers through the following:
    -   the supply of necessary assistance and resources to promote initiatives aimed at the adoption and use
        of the integrated fight;
   -    the support of research activities involving farmers;
support to the organization of training courses bringing together farmers, researchers and extension workers;
   -    the sensitization of consumers on the quality of agricultural products;
   -    the training of producers on the integrated protection practices.


    CHAPTER III - INTERVENTIONS AGAINST MAJOR PESTS AND OTHER HARMFUL ORGANISMS
                                         I - Interventions on major plagues
Article 11- (1) The State shall provide direct support to phytosanitary interventions relating to major pests.
 (2) Phytosanitary treatments within the framework of interventions shall be done either through specialized
services of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture or by any public or private institution appointed to this effect.
(3) Once decided upon, the said interventions shall be systematic and mandatory for all farming unit.
Article 12 - The Minister in charge of Agriculture shall prescribe the phytosanitary measures on major pests. He
shall fix by an Order the list of major pests and update it when need arises.


                             II - Alert and the fight against other harmful organisms
Article 13 - (1) The Authority in charge of phytosanitary protection shall disseminate throughout the national
territory information on the harmful organisms and on the necessary preventive and curative treatments for the
good sanitary state of the plants and their products.
(2) To this effect, it shall design or put in place alert and intervention systems, the objective of which shall be to
monitor the alerts and interventions.
Article 14 - (1) The fight against harmful organisms shall be implemented and led by farmers
or farmer organizations. This shall be carried out at the time the circumstances permit following the integrated
approach provided for in article 10 above.
(2) The use of phytosanitary products shall be done with due respect for good phytosanitary
practices.
Article 15 -The competent services of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture, in collaboration with other concerned
administrations and institutions shall support farmer organizations in the following domains:
    -   the organization, structuring and putting in place of intervention units;
    -   the development and extension of technical itineraries;
    -   the training on good phytosanitary practices;
    -   the elaboration and presentation of strategies for the fight against harmful organisms;
    -   the evaluation of risks related to the use of pesticides.
                                                                                                           Page | 152
Article 16 (1) - The Minister in charge of Agriculture may prescribe by Order the treatments and
necessary measures for the prevention of the propagation of harmful organisms on the farm.
 (2) He may equally forbid risky practices which may cause the development and dissemination of
harmful organisms following the same modalities.
 (3) If a proprietor refuses to affect the treatments and measures mentioned in paragraph (1) above
officials for plant inspection and control shall take necessary measures for their execution. They shall
forward a copy to the competent administrative authority.
 (4) The operations of the fight shall be effected by a certified structure and under the control of the
plant protection services.
(5) The expenses of the operation shall be charged to the proprietor.
(6) In case of non-payment by the concerned within three (03) months the or at all times that the
treatment is ensured by the plant protection services, the recovery shall be carried out as in direct
contributions on roll drawn up by the sworn officials in charge of plant control.
Article 17 - (1) Farmers shall use the following only:
    - released or temporarily authorized pesticides complying with international standards
    - pertaining to distribution and use of pesticides;
    - phytosanitary products for use indicated on the label;
services of personnel trained in the field by the services in charge of plant protection or agricultural
extension or any other specialized structure.
(2) Furthermore, they shall:
   -   apply phytosanitary products on surfaces and structures that were used for the production,
       transport, transformation and the storage of agricultural or forestry products;
   - apply them with care in order to avoid endangering human and animal health and in
preserving the environment.
(3) It is forbidden to employ children under fifteen years of age and pregnant women in crop
protection work.
Article 18 - Phytosanitary products shall be stored in places presenting no risk for human and animal
health or the environment.
                                         III - Final provisions
Article 19 - Texts from the Minister in charge of Agriculture shall precise, when need be, the
implementation modalities of this decree.
Article 20 - All previous provisions contrary to the present decree are hereby repealed, especially
those of Decree No. 92-233-PM of 25 May 1992 to fix the implementation modalities of Law No. 90–
013 of 10 August 1990 on phytosanitary protection.
Article 21 - This decree shall be registered and published following the emergency procedure, then
inserted in the Official Gazette in French and English.




                                                                                 Yaoundé, 6 April 2005
                                                           The Prime Minister Head of Government
                                                                                       INONI Ephraim




Page | 153
   4.3.3.     DECREE N° 2005/0771 /PM of 06 APRIL 2005 to fix conditions of plant quarantine
       operations

                         THE PRIME MINISTER, HEAD OF GOVERNMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 92/O89 of 4 May 1992 to define the duties of the Prime Minister modified and
completed by Decree N°95/145 of 5th August 1995;
Mindful of Decree No. 2004/320 of 8th December 2004 to organize the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2004/321 of 8th December 2004 to appoint a Prime Minister,
                                               DECREES
       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1 - This decree shall fix conditions for the execution of plant quarantine operations.
Article 2 - Within the context of this decree, the following shall mean:
Phytosanitary risk analysis: consistent process of evaluating biological proofs or other scientific or
economic data to determine if a harmful organism should be checked as well as the severity of eventual
phytosanitary measures to be taken against it;
Phytosanitary certificate: an official document established following the pattern of the
International Convention for Crop Protection of the FAO, issued by the Phytosanitary Authority and
which certifies the phytosanitary state of all released products;
Destination inspection: inspection carried out outside the entry and exit points, especially in the final
destination places of imported parcels or consignments;
Inspection on site: inspection carried out between the entry and exit points;
Integrated protection: approach of fighting harmful organisms based on the ecological principles and
which reinforces farmers' capacity to promote crop health in a balanced agro-ecological system,
making use of economically available and sustainable technologies, especially variety selection,
biological fight, cropping methods and regulatory measures;
Official campaign: active implementation of the regulations and phytosanitary procedures in a
mandatory manner with the objectives of eradicating or checking quarantine organisms or the fight
against pests, whether regulated or not;
Quarantine organism: harmful organism which has a potential importance for the economy of the
threatened zone, which is not yet present in the zone or that is present but not largely disseminated
and is officially fought against;
Entry and exit points: all ports, airports, post offices, land border points designated for the importation
of parcels and/or for the entry of passengers.
       CHAPTER II: PHYTOSANITARY PROTECTION AND SURVEILLANCE OF THE
       TERRITORY
                                           I - Import licenses
Article 3 - (1) Without prejudice to commercial laws and regulations, import licenses shall be issued,
depending on the origin and only for products presenting high risks of spreading quarantine organisms,
by the Department in charge of regulation and quality control of inputs and agricultural products.
(2) The list of plants and plant products, soils and areas of cultivation of products presenting high risks
of spreading quarantine organisms, shall be published by Order of the Minister in charge of agriculture
and regularly updated according to the evolution of phytosanitary risk analysis.

                                                                                              Page | 154
Article 4 - (1) In a situation considered as presenting a very high risk of introduction of quarantine
organisms, the Ministry in charge of Agriculture may demand the inspection of plants, plant products,
soils or cultivation areas considered as suspect in the exporting country before the issuance of the
import license.
(2) The charges for obtaining the import license shall be borne by the importer.
Article 5 - (1) The granting of an import license is subject to constituting and forwarding a file
to the Authority in charge of Phytosanitary Protection latest one month before the date of the first
loading.
(2) The file mentioned in paragraph (1) above shall comprise:
    - stamped application with exact address of the producer or the quarantine station having
realized the plant, plant products, the expel date of arrival of the products in Cameroon, as
well as the entry point in Cameroon and the quantity and variety of the products to be imported, the
cultivation zone of the product concerned;
    -   a photocopy of the pro-forma invoice specifying the means of transportation, the importer's
        address the port of shipment as well as the address of the supplier.

                                     II - Phytosanitary certification


Article 6 - (1) Phytosanitary certification shall consist of the:
    - phytosanitary inspection operations;
    - eventual phytosanitary treatments;
    - issuing of reports and attestations of phytosanitary inspections;
    - issuing of phytosanitary certificates.
(2) The inspection charges and other necessary activities for the establishment of phytosanitary
certificates shall be borne by the user respectively.
(3) The charges shall be jointly fixed by the Ministers in charge of Finance and Agriculture.
Article7- Phytosanitary certificates attached to export parcels shall be issued at the exit points by the
authorities of phytosanitary police posts after a concluding phytosanitary inspection.
Article 8 - At importation, articles regulated as high-risk introduction products of quarantine organisms
shall be accompanied by a phytosanitary certificate or origin respecting the conditions and
requirements of the import license related to them. After verification, a phytosanitary certificate for re-
exportation shall be issued to this effect.
Article 9 - Plants and plant products in transit must be accompanied by a phytosanitary certificate of
origin. After verification of the packets, a phytosanitary certificate of re-exportation shall be delivered
to this effect.
                      III - Verification for phytosanitary conformity of parcels
Article 10 - Parcels with high risk introduction products of quarantine organisms shall be subject to
phytosanitary conformity verification before their use or distribution on the national territory at the
expense of the importer.
Article 11 - The verification for phytosanitary conformity products and parcels presenting high risks of
spreading quarantine organisms shall be carried out by the laboratories of the Authority in charge of
plant quarantine or any other laboratory recognized by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Article 12 - (1) The sampling shall be carried out by sworn officials in charge of phytosanitary
inspection in accordance with scientific norms in the domain and, as much as possible, in the presence
or the proprietor.
(2) In the case of the non-contestation of the analysis results, the test sample kept by the phytosanitary
police post shall be returned to the user within two (02) months upon request.
Page | 155
                    IV - Phytosanitary treatment at importation and exportation
Article 13 - (1) The objective of phytosanitary treatment shall be the elimination of harmful organisms
detected during the phytosanitary inspections of regulated articles or the prevention of their
dissemination.
 (2) They shall be prescribed and supervised by the services in charge of plant quarantine and carried
out by registered bodies under the Ministry in charge of Agriculture in following modalities defined in a
specifications record.
(3) The expenses involved shall be borne by the user.
(4) The list of these companies shall be regularly updated and published annually.
Article14 - (1) The treatment mentioned above shall give rise to the issuance of a phytosanitary
treatment attestation by the institution or company having realized the said treatment.
(2) The attestation cited in paragraph (1) above shall be requested for during the establishment of the
phytosanitary certificate.
                   V- Publication of the lists of regulated Quarantine operations
Article 15 - (1) The Ministry in charge of Agriculture shall fix annually, by regulation, the list of all
regulated pests. He shall take all phytosanitary measures deemed useful against them with a view to
reducing their incidence on plants and plant products. To this effect, he shall fix:
   -    the list of plants, plant products, soils and cultivation areas of which the production, importation
        and exportation are prohibited in Cameroon;
    - the list of plants, plant products, soils and cultivation areas submitted to a production
        declaration, the import and export as well as the special conditions which related to them and
        for which an official fight could be engaged.
Article 16 - (1) The Minister in charge of agriculture may establish, by Order, a quarantine area within
the national territory in case of a declared epidemic or the appearance of a harmful quarantine
organism.
(2) In the case of the delimitation of quarantine areas within the national territory, the movement of
plants, plant products, soils and cultivation areas towards or outside these areas, shall be submitted
to the acquisition of a special phytosanitary certificate.
(3) The Authority in charge of Phytosanitary Protection shall decide on the conditions under
which plants, plant products, soils and cultivation areas as well as packages and other forms of
packaging likely to serve as supports to harmful organisms, may circulate in the national territory.
       CHAPTER III - MODALITIES FOR THE INTRODUCTION OF BIOLOGICAL FIGHTING
       AGENTS
Article 17 - (1) The import of exotic biological fighting agents shall be subject to the acquisition of an
import permit.
(2) To this effect, the importer must produce a file comprising the following:
   -   the specificity of the biological fighting agent;
   -   the entry point;
   -   the persons or organizations susceptible to receive the parcel;
   -   an introduction programme;
   -   the preliminary studies on the identification of the harmful organism;
   -   all pertinent information on its origin and importance of damage occasioned as well as the experiments
       carried elsewhere with the harmful organism;
   -   the results of inquiries and exploration in the area of origin of the biological fight agent;
   -   the description of the quarantine unit of the importing organization;
   -   the conditions for production and release of biological fighting agents;
   -   the envisaged fighting methods in case of release or accidental dispersion.

                                                                                                Page | 156
Article 18 - In the case of the successive import of the agent destined for use in biological fighting, in
the integrated protection or even as bio-pesticide, only the agents with a quality similar to that of the
first approved import may be authorized and released.
Article 19 - Prior to all import, the importer must ensure:
    -   their conformity to international norms in the domain of labelling, conditioning and
        advertisement;
    -   that the biological fighting agents had been submitted to an assessment of their effects on
        human and animal health and on the environment.
Article 20 - All parcels must keep documents containing information on:
    - the identity and the mode of identification mode of the biological fighting agent;
    - the handling and transportation of the parcel in all safety;
    - rearing or cultivation conditions;
    - the methods for handling the agent;
    - the contaminants, their identification and eradication.
Failure to comply with the above requirements will expose the products to repatriation or to
destruction.
        CHAPTER IV - PHYTOSANITARY INSPECTION AND THE MISSIONS OF SWORN
        OFFICIALS
ARTICLE 21 - (1) The objective of phytosanitary inspection shall be to prevent:
    -   the introduction or the propagation of regulated organisms;
    -   the contamination by harmful organisms or the quarantine of plants and plant products in the
        process of processing and packaging.
(2) Phytosanitary inspection shall be a requirement for plant quarantine to be fulfilled by physical and
moral persons ensuring responsibility for:
    -   import or export of plants, plant products, soils or cultivation areas;
    -   production fields for plants destined for international trade or of seed production fields:
    -   warehouse stocking of plants and plant products destined for international trade:
    -   production and plants and plant products transformation units as well as the means of
        transportation and parcel post offices;
    - Commercialization of plants, plant products designed for local markets as well as for export.
(3) Inspection shall be carried out at the initiative of the officials in charge of plant quarantine or at the
request of physical or moral persons involved in the activities mentioned in paragraph (2) above.
Article 22 -Following the phytosanitary state realized after inspection of the product to be imported or
exported or to be distributed, sworn agents in charge of phytosanitary inspection shall, as may be the
case:
    - authorize the use or distribution;
    - undertake an emergency action;
    - refuse the issuance of a phytosanitary certificate;
    - place the products in quarantine for observation or their repatriation:
    - treat them, cause them to be treated or proceed to their destruction in case of infestation.
(2) The expenses of the treatment, repatriation and destruction shall be borne by the user.
Article 23 - (1) The inspection and the other necessary activities for the establishment of phytosanitary
certificates shall only be entrusted to the Administration in charge of plant production.




Page | 157
        I - Phytosanitary inspection of plants, plant products, soils and cultivation areas
Article 24 - (1) The phytosanitary inspection of plants, plant products, soils or cultivation areas shall
be realized on the entire freight or on a representative sample.
(2) It shall take place on the entire national territory or on other areas where plants and plant products
are cultivated as well as on areas used for the cultivation of internationally destined plants.
 (3) It shall particularly be carried out at the entry and exit points of the national territory, eventually at
the station of the plant quarantine or at the sites of production, processing and the packaging of
products destined for export as well as ship holds, plane luggage compartments and parcels post
offices destined for Cameroon.
 (4) However, phytosanitary inspection posts for plants, plant products, soils and cultivation areas
outside the services of phytosanitary police situated at the entry and exit points of the national territory,
may be realized at the request of the user after the authorization of the Authority in charge of the
phytosanitary protection.
(5) When the phytosanitary inspection is taking place outside the local place of service, the team in
charge should be composed of at least two (02) sworn officials from the services in charge of the
phytosanitary police.
 (6) In the same way, when the phytosanitary inspection involves the movement of the phytosanitary
authority from his place of work situated at the entry and exit of the national territory, the cost of the
movement shall be borne by the user.
                          II - Inspection of seed and plant production farms
Article 25 - Any physical or moral person producing plants, suckers, grafting, graft carriers or linear
deep-rooted plants aimed at multiplication as well as for seed, shall register himself at the competent
services under the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
Article 26 - (1) In the event of the presumption of the presence of a harmful organism in the
establishments or in places of multiplication, a treatment or placing in quarantine shall be ordered until
complete disinfection.
 (2) The proprietor or the exploiter shall then carry out phytosanitary measures within the prescribed
deadlines and bear the charges.
Article 27- (1) Within the framework of seed certification, phytosanitary inspection on the seed
production farms shall be realized systematically on the entire exploitation by the services in charge of
phytosanitary protection or by any competent certified organism.
(2) In the fields of the production of other plants, the inspection shall be done on the entire
exploitation at the request of the exporter when required by the importing country.
(3) The frequency of inspection shall vary with the plant species cultivated in accordance with the
biology of the harmful organisms concerned. In each case, a minimum of two (02) inspections spread
out in time shall be carried out in a planting season.
 (4) In each of the cases defined in the paragraphs above, phytosanitary inspection shall be followed
by the deliverance of an inspection attestation in the course of the planting period.
Article 28 - The inspections and phytosanitary analyses in carried out by laboratories specialized in
seeds and plants imported or exported may only be done by the services in charge of phytosanitary
protection.
                       III - Phytosanitary inspection of other regulated articles
Article 29 - (1) The inspection of other regulated items destined for international commerce shall also
be carried out throughout the national territory.




                                                                                                 Page | 158
(2) That shall be done essentially at the entry and exit points of the national territory, eventually at for
exportation as well as in the holds of ship, plane luggage compartments and parcels postal offices
leaving or destined for Cameroon.
Article 30 - The phytosanitary inspection of post office parcels shall be done within the services in
charge of postal parcels, eventually in the areas of the officers in charge of international
correspondences following conditions defined by regulations.
Article 31 - (1) With regard to stores or warehouses for plants or plant products destined for
international exchanges, the phytosanitary inspection shall be done on the entire installations.
(2) In the units designed for processing and packaging plants and plant products destined for
international exchanges and for the local markets, the inspection shall be done on the sites for
production, processing and packaging chain as well as in the internal warehouses of the units.
(3) These be shall done at least two (02) times a year, followed by a written inspection report.
Article 32 - (1) The inspection of the means of transport for plants and plant products destined
for international and local exchanges shall exclusively be done at the entry and exit points of the
national territory. This shall be effected in:
    - the luggage compartments and plane cabins of planes carrying out international flights;
    - holds and cabins of ships involved in international transport;
    - all trains, vehicles and any other transport means for plants and plant products.
(2) This gives right to the issuance of a phytosanitary inspection written report.
       CHAPTER V - MISSIONS OF SWORN OFFICIALS AND PROSECUTION OF OFFENDERS
                                     I - Missions of sworn officials
Article 33 - (1) To facilitate the execution of their duties, the phytosanitary inspectors and controllers
shall have access to different bills of lading and international forwarding documents.
(2) In the discharge of their duties, the sworn officials may oppose the distribution of plants and plant
products, postal parcels which are not in line with national phytosanitary regulations.
(3) They shall sample out all sent international parcels containing plants or plant products in the
presence of the proprietor according to the scientific rules in the domains.
Article 34 - (1) In conformity with the law, the sworn officials shall fulfil their task as judicial police
officers with special competence in matters of import, export and the management of plants, plant
products and other regulated articles.
(2) They shall establish an official report of facts, the seizure of plants, plant products, soils and
introduced or used cultivation areas introduced or used in violation of the provisions of the law and the
statement of offence.
(3) The statement written and signed by the sworn official in the presence of the proprietor shall be
held as authentic and accurate until proven false.
Article 35 - The officers in charge of phytosanitary inspection of plants, plant products and other
regulated articles, within the exercise of their functions and in conformity with law procedures in force,
may:
   -   request the assistance of the forces of law and order for the search and seizure of plants and
       plant products introduced, sold, circulating fraudulently or to obtain the identification of the
       offenders;
   -   check localities, trains, ships, vehicles or aircrafts, transporting or likely to transport or even
       house the said products;
   -   order phytosanitary treatment, the destruction or the repatriation of plants, plant products and
       other regulated articles at the expense of the promoter;
   -   affix seals.


Page | 159
Article 36 - (1) The sworn official charge of phytosanitary protection shall take oath before any
territorially competent court of first instance under the conditions of common law, at the behest of the
phytosanitary authority.

(2) - They shall be obliged to wear uniforms and to present their professional cards and mission order
at request.
(3) - The phytosanitary inspector or controller loses his function as such once the sworn official no
longer works in a phytosanitary regulated structure.

                                    II - The prosecution of offences
Article 37 - (1) Any violation to the provisions of this decree, especially the import of plant and products,
soils and cultivation areas without permit or phytosanitary certificate, shall lead to the seizure, the
destruction or repatriation of the stock at the expense of the offender, without prejudice to, legal action
provided for by the law.
2- The measures taken shall be recorded in a written report co-signed by the sworn official and the
offender. The report shall be held as a true record of the facts stated therein until proven false.
3- The report shall be forwarded to the territorially competent procurator of the Republic.

       CHAPTER VI: FINAL DISPOSITIONS
Article 38- The provisions of this decree also apply to plants ant plant products, soil and cultivation
areas in transit whose conditioning presents a risk of introduction or propagation of regulated harmful
organisms.
Article 39- All previous contrary, especially those of Decree N° 92/233/PM of 25 May 1992 fixing the
modalities of the implementation of law N°90/013 of 10 August1990 on phytosanitary protection are
hereby abrogated.
Article 40-This decree shall be registered and published following the urgent procedure and then
inserted in the Official Gazette in French and English./-

                                                                                       The Prime Minister,
                                                                                     Head of Government
                                                                                         INONI EPHRAIM
