    4.3.4.       Decree N° 2005/0772 /PM OF 06 April 2005 to fix conditions of plant quarantine
                                             operations

                          THE PRIME MINISTER, HEAD OF GOVERNMENT
Mindful of the Constitution:
Mindful of Law NO 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 bearing Phytosanitary Protection;
Mindful of Decree No, 92/089 of 4 May 1992 to precise the attributions of the Prime Minister, modified
and completed by Decree No, 95/145 of 5 August 1995;
Mindful of Decree No 2004 /320 of 8th December 2004 on the organization of the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2004/321 of 8th December 2004 on the appointment of a Prime Minister


                                                 DECREES
       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1 - This decree shall fix the conditions of release and the control of phytosanitary products.
Article 2 - Within the context of this decree, the following shall mean:
Antagonist: organism which does not cause serious destruction but whose presence protects the
host from the damage of other harmful organisms;
Bio-pesticide: a generic term generally applied to a biological fighting agent most, often a pathogen,
formulated and applied in an analogous manner to a chemical pesticide and normally used to rapidly
reduce the population of a harmful organism in a short term fight;
Release: process at the end of which the competence authority approves the import, distribution and
use of a phytosanitary product, after a complete scientific data showing that the product is efficient for
the intended use and does not present any risks to human, animal health and the environment, in the
recommended conditions of use;
Entry or exit points: airports, river or maritime ports, centres for parcels postal offices or officially
designated land frontier points;
Phytosanitary products: pesticides, bio-pesticides and other substances destined for use as a
regulator for the growth of plants, as defoliants, desiccating agents, fruit lighteners or products Used
to stop the premature falling off of fruits as well as substances applied on crops, before and after
harvest to protect the produce from deterioration during storage in warehouses or during
transportation.
Residues: specific substances left by a phytosanitary product in agriculture products or in food.
       CHAPTER II: RELEASE PROCEDURE
                                                I - Release
Article 3 - Any phytosanitary product shall first and foremost be subjected to the release procedure
before its import, distribution and use.
Article 4 - (1) The release shall comprise the following operations:
 a) chemical analysis of a sample taken from the product done by nationally recognized laboratory or
one recognized abroad,
b) the biological efficiency tests for use indicated during one (01) to two (02) agricultural cycles which
are realized by a research institute for:
new phytosanitary products;
    - the extension of the release of a phytosanitary product in view of the control of enemies other
        than those for which it was initially released.
(c) Pre-extension tests for at least one (1) agricultural cycle carried out by the service of plant protection
of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture in the event of a concluding biological efficiency test.

Page | 161
(d) joint bio-efficiency and pre-extension tests for at least one (1) agricultural cycle for:
    -  phytosanitary products whose active substance is well-known and released, but presented
       under another formula,
    - new phytosanitary products whose biological efficiency trails have been concluded during the
       first year of experimentation;
    - The modification of a known commercial specialty or the modification of active substances in a
       given commercial specialty,
(2) The expenses of the operation in paragraph (1) shall be borne by the promoter.
Article 5 - (1) The implementation of the tests cited in article 4 (above) shall be ordered by the
Minister in charge of Agriculture.
(2) The latter may either prohibit or authorize them subject to all the conditions deemed necessary for
preventing hazards and risks for human, animal health and the environment.
Article 6 - (1) Prior to applying the tests mentioned above, preliminary dose screening tests may be
done in order to determine the dose in an active substance or of the products formulated for use and
to enlighten the producer on the efficiency of the phytosanitary products vis-à-vis the harmful organism
targeted.
(2) The preliminary tests shall be jointly executed by phytosanitary firms and certified research
Institutions or specialized structures certified by the Minister in charge of Agriculture, operating in this
domain.
(3) Reports of the said tests shall be presented to the competent authority upon request for them to
complete any information it possesses.
Article 7 - (1) The release of a phytosanitary product shall require the promoter or distributor submit a
file consisting of the following documents;
    -  a stamped application for the release of the specialty;
    -  a description form;
    -  technical documents;
    -  an analytical document;
    -  a toxicological document;
    -  eco-environmental documents;
    -  an original labelling pattern or its sketch;
    -  a sample for reference of the active matter(s) contained in the specialty and a sample of the
       specialty;
    - release attestations or certificates issued in other countries;
    - a report of biological efficiency and pre-extension tests;
    - an analysis bulletin issued by a laboratory certified by the Ministry in charge Agriculture;
    - a certificate of origin issued by the Chamber of Commerce of the country of origin of the
       phytosanitary product or by any other competent structure;
    - A report of the residual content tests of the phytosanitary product in the treated plants or plant
       products;
     -   the name and description of the local representative.
 (2) The file cited in paragraph (1) above shall be submitted in three (03) copies at the secretariat of
the Commission against a receipt at least four (4) weeks before the holding of the session.
Article 8 - For bio-pesticides release, the promoter shall submit a file comprising the following
documents and information:
    -   precise identification of the targeted harmful organism and its presumed origin;
    -   an evaluation of its importance;
    -   a precise identification form or a sufficient bio-pesticide characterization in order to identify it without
        ambiguity;
                                                                                                      Page | 162
    -  a list of its auxiliaries and their impact in the spread zone;
    -  an analytic form of the bio-pesticide specificity and all the potential risks which it may present to the non-
       target hosts;
    -  the proposed contamination agents and the laboratory elimination procedures of their colonies;
    -  procedures permitting the identification and elimination of crops, the host on which the
pathogenic agent has been cultivated;
   -    the qualification of persons susceptible to manipulate bio-pesticides under laboratory
conditions, of production and in the field;
    -      risks on the health of humans and animals which are likely to be exposed;
    -      a study of the impact on non-target organisms and on the environment;
    -      a description of emergency procedures if the bio-pesticides, after spraying, causes unforeseen
           undesirable effects;
    -      a detailed report of laboratory analyses or field observations on the scale of known and potential
           antagonists;
    -      the nomenclature of used additives in the formulation;
    -      a report of the biological efficiency tests;
    -      an analysis bulletin delivered by a laboratory appointed by the Minister in charge of Agriculture;
    -      the description of quarantine installations;
    -      The credentials of the staff.


                                            II - Renewal of release
Article 9 - (1l) the company, owner of the active substance mark or the commercial specialty,
may apply for the renewal of the release approval
(2) The application for the renewal of release approval shall be forwarded to the Commission at
least six (6) months before the expiry date of the release and shall include the following documents
    -      data on the toxicity on man;
    -      available environmental data
    -      data on the maximum limits of the residue:
    -      report of remote effects of the phytosanitary products on successive crops;
    -      a monitoring report of the phytosanitary product after eight (8) years of use.

                            III - The modification and treatment of the release
Article 10 - The release of phytosanitary products shall be modified if proven that the direction for use
is modified or that the applied doses have been modified.
Article 11 - (1) The withdrawal of the release of a phytosanitary product may be ordered in cases
where the said product:
    -     is no longer effective against the target enemy;
    -     presents undesirable effects on the plants and plant products;
        -    has an adverse effect on human and animal health within the normal conditions of its use.
(2) The withdrawal of the release may also occur;
    -      when incorrect or fallacious indications have been given in the data on the basis of which the
        release approval was granted;
    - at the request of the owner of the mark.
Article 12 - The holder of a release shall imperatively communicate to the phytosanitary authority any
new information relating to potential dangers of a phytosanitary product or its residue on human, animal
health or the environment.
                                      IV - Transfer of release approval
Article 13 - (1) Upon the request of the holder firm, a release may be transferred to another firm.

Page | 163
(2) The firm owning the release shall furnish a tile the transfer comprising these documents:
-       a stamped application;
-       a copy of the release Order
-     an agreement of the release transfer, jointly signed by the holder and the firm applying for the
acquisition of the release.
(3) The release transfer gives rise to the issuance of an attestation of the cession of the release signed
by the Phytosanitary Authority.
                            V- Research, teaching and emergency situations
Article 14 - Without prejudice to the provisions of article 10 above, the import of small quantity
phytosanitary products for experimentation or use, when not released or in case of not obtainment of
a provisional sale authorization, may receive a special authorization from the Authority in charge of
Phytosanitary Protection to:
    - research institutions;
    - agricultural extension services;
    - phytosanitary products analyses laboratories;
    - phytosanitary research unit firms installed all over the national territory:
    - teaching institutions.
Article 15 - In the event of calamities and in the absence of a released phytosanitary products this
effect, the Minister in charge of Agriculture may authorize the immediate use of a non- released
phytosanitary product:
    -     if no equivalent released product is available on the market and that the active matter is known
          by competent international organizations;
    -     if the said product benefits from authorized placement on the markets in the country of origin
          or when it is already used elsewhere for similar reason;
    -     if it is not subject to a withdrawal of its authorization for placing on the market in a third country
          or if it does not belong to the group of persistent organic polluters.

    CHAPTER III: CERTIFICATION OF TREATMENT APPARATUS
Article 16 - (1) Any phytosanitary treatment model and type of apparatus of treatment shall be
submitted to the procedure of certification prior to its import, distribution and use, in conformity with
international norms in that matter.
(2) The certification shall comprise the following operations:
    -  control tests for technical specifications realized in a specialized laboratory or any other
       institution specially endowed by the Minister in charge of Agriculture;
    - performance test in the field realized by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
(3) The expenses arising from these tests shall be borne by the promoters.
Article 17 - The certification mentioned in article 16 above mainly applies to the following types of apparatus:
     -   manual back sprayers;
     -   motorized back sprayers;
     -   back prior sprayers;
     -   centrifugal sprayers;
     -   thermic nebulisation apparatus;
     -   powder sprayers;
     -   Granule sprayers;
     -   Cooling nebulizors;
     -   Traction apparatus.
Article 18 - (1) For the purpose of certification of phytosanitary treatment apparatus, the promoter shall furnish
a file comprising the following:
                                                                                                     Page | 164
a) a stamped application carrying the marks of the apparatus and information on its technical specifications;
b) a user's manual containing the information here below:
    -   initial assembly:
    -   identification of all spare parts, accompanied by a broad and detailed view of the whole apparatus:
    -   regulating and calibre levers;
    -   cleaning and safe discharge of waste liquids;
    -   usual maintenance and storage;
    -   security and habitual use in the field;
    -   the safe release of pressure in the tank and spear;
    -   the durability of the constitutive materials;
    -   the flow of buzzards and spurts, the quality of the fountain or of the cloud of smoke and the characteristics
        of the droplets;
    -   the calibre of the buzzard and the pressure of service to be used;
    -   the precautions to take in reducing contamination of the users and pollution of the environment;
C) a report of the tests of technical specification control;
d) a report of performance tests issued by the Authority in charge of phytosanitary protection;
e) an engagement to ensure service after sale and placing on the markets spare parts;
f) a sample of the apparatus or a report of performance tests jointly realized by the importing firm and technical
services of the Ministry in charge Agriculture.
(2) The file mentioned in paragraph (1) above shall be submitted at the secretariat of the Commission in three
(3) copies against a receipt latest four (4) weeks before the holding of its Session.
Article 19 -Treatment apparatus sold or used shall in no case present any danger to the users.
They should easily and worthily be used in the normal using conditions. They shall respect the defined
characteristics prescribed by the Phytosanitary Authority, failing of which, international requirements must be
respected.
        CHAPTER IV: NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR PHYTOSANITARY PRODUCTS RELEASE
        AND CERTIFICATION FOR TREATMENT APPARATUS
Article 20 - A National Commission Phytosanitary Products Release and Certification for Treatment
Apparatus (hereinafter called the "Commission") is hereby created under the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture, for the purpose of:
    -   deciding on release files of phytosanitary products and files relating to renewal of release;
    -   Suggesting on the certification files of phytosanitary treatment apparatus;
    -   giving an opinion on the restriction or banning of the use of a phytosanitary product or on the
        withdrawal of the release;
    - Giving a technical opinion on any file confided to it;
    - Validating the experiments protocols.
Article 21 - (1) Presided by the Minister in charge of Agriculture or his representative, the Commission
shall be composed and besides as follows;
     - Two (2) representatives of the Minister in charge of Agriculture;
     - Two (2) representatives of the Minister in charge of scientific Research;
     - One (1) representative of the Minister in charge of Higher Education'
     - One (1) representative of the Minister in charge of Livestock:
     - One (1) representative of the Minister in charge of Public Health:
     - One (1) representative of the Minister in charge of Environment and Nature Protection;
     - One (1) representative of the Minster in charge of Commerce;
     - One (1) representative of the Minister in charge of Energy and Water.
(2) The members of the Commission shall be appointed by the Minister in charge of Agriculture
following proposal from the Administration of origin.
(3) The President of the Commission may invite any person, based on his competence on an issue on
the agenda of the day, to take part in the Commission's deliberation with a consultative voice.

Page | 165
(4) The secretariat of the Commission shall be performed by the Department of regulation and quality
control of agricultural inputs and products;
Article 22 - The Commission shall meet twice a year and as often as need be at the convocation of its Presidents
at the meeting.
Article 23 - The convocation to the session shall be in writing and forwarded to members fifteen (15) days before
the fixed date of the meeting. They shall specify the agenda and the venue of the meeting.
Article 24 - (1) The decisions of the Commission shall be taken by a simple majority of the members presents.
(2) They shall be recorded in a report and countersigned by the President of the Commission and the Secretary.
Article 25 - The Commission shall examine the files mentioned in article 20 above and shall deliberate on one
of the following measures.
    -   the maintenance of the phytosanitary product under study within period scheduled following the
        amplitude of the problem discovered;
    -   the provisional sale authorization for a period of two (2) years non-renewable, following the nature of the
        problem;
    -   the release of a phytosanitary product for the indicated uses for a period of five (5) years, renewable
        once by a tacit carry forward;
    -   the modification or extension of the users of a phytosanitary product;
    -   the cancellation of a provisional sale authorization;
    -   the withdrawal of the release;
    -   the renewal of a release;
    -   restriction.
Article 26 - The release shall be granted to commercial specialties and not to active substances. It is
also given on the basic of scientific and technical knowledge and during a usage that is in conformity
with the indications on the label and good phytosanitary practices. It is clear that:
   - the phytosanitary product is effective against the target enemy;
   - the phytosanitary product docs not have a direct or indirect adverse effect on the health of
human, animal health or on the environment in the normal conditions of use;
    -  from the toxicological and environment perspectives, major component may be determined.
    -  the nature and quantity of active matters impurities and other important elements, from the
       toxicological and eco-toxicological approaches may be determined with the help of
       experimented methods;
    - the residues resulting from the accepted uses and the characteristic from the toxicological and
       environment viewpoints may be determined with the help of experimented methods;
    - the physical and chemical properties are judged as convenient;
    - the maximum limits of the residues, determined by the Phytosanitary authority or any other
       competent international organization in the domain are known;
    - residual remains of the phytosanitary product in the treated products are short of the maximum
       limit of the residue.
Article 27 - The deliberations of the Commission shall be competed to execution by Order from the
Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Article 28 - (1) The promoter shall have the right to repeal and may, to this effect, request for re-
examination of a file when he feels that the decision taken against him appears to be unjustified.
He may:
    -  either request to be heard by the Commission. To this effect, he could be assisted by a certified
       expert of his choice; or
    - apply to the Minister in charge of Agriculture for a re-examination of the decision within thirty
       (30) days following the notice of the results of the session, at the pain of being time-barred.
(2) The re-examination or hearing shall take place during the next session.


                                                                                                     Page | 166
Article 29 - The functions of the President and Commission members shall be free of charge,
However, the members shall benefit from working facilities necessary for the accomplishment of their
missions.
Article 30 - The functioning financial needs of the Commission shall be borne by the budget of the
Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
         CHAPTER V: ACTIVITIES RELATED TO PHYTOSANITARY PRODUCTS
                               I - The importance of phytosanitary products
Article 31 - (1) Without prejudice to the laws and regulations on commerce, the import of phytosanitary
products shall be declared to the Authority in charge of phytosanitary protection by the importer.
(2) Importation shall be exclusively reserved for the local representatives of phytosanitary firms owning
release authorizations. However, in emergency cases of a fight against major pests, the Phytosanitary
Authority may import released phytosanitary products.
Article 32 - (1) The declaration mentioned in article 31 above shall contain the following:
    -   the exact address of the trademark or supplier;
    -   the probable arrival date of the arrival of the products in Cameroon as well as the entry point;
    -   the name and quantity of pesticides imported as well as the countries of origin and destinations;
    -   the means- of transportation and attestation of importation transfer signed by the holder of the
        release;
    - the importer' s address or that of the supplier as well as the distributors.
(2) The file shall be submitted to the Department of regulation and control of agriculture products at
least one (1) month before the forecasted date of shipment.
Article 33 - The containers of pesticides or bio-pesticides in transit through the national territory or
containing pesticides made, formulated or conditional in Cameroon for exportation shall be submitted
to control.
Article 34 - Any importer shall furnish yearly data on the type, quantity and value of the phytosanitary
products, imported for the purpose of statistics development.
               II - Manufacturing formulation and packaging of phytosanitary products
Article 35 - (1) The manufacturing, formulation and packaging activities released phytosanitary products shall
be submitted to registration from the Minister in charge of Agriculture prior to a renewal period of ten (10) years
(2) The registration mentioned in paragraph (1) above shall be granted upon submission of a file including the
following:
    a) stamped application indicating:

    -    the name and surname or the type of applicant;
    -    the exact address of the trademark holder or supplier;
    -    the pesticides, bio-pesticides or the biological fighting agent for application is forwarded.

    b) a certified true copy of a medical care agreement subscribed to under a medical doctor;
    c) the geographical localization of installations as well as the sources of danger of the accountable for
       the situation of the areas;
    d) a situation plan, a mass plan and a detailed plan of the production unit;
    e) an environmental impact assessment realized by the Permanent Secretariat for Environment in the
       Ministry in charge of Environment;
    f)   a visit report of the installation by the competent services of the Authority in charge of crop protection;
    g) a general description of the technical process;
    h) emergency plans, comprising security equipment, alert and intervention means provided for within and
       without the establishment;
    i)   the list of forecasted employment and their qualifications;

Page | 167
    j)   a commitment to subscribe an insurance covering fire and other risks;
    k) a legalized commitment of the promoter to use only the Phytosanitary products which he applied for;
    l)   the procedure for the elimination of the effluents, waste and packages.
                          III - Distribution of released phytosanitary products
Article 36 - Released phytosanitary products may be distributed or resold by physical or moral persons
certified by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Article 37 - (1) Any physical or moral person who buys phytosanitary products for commercial
purposes from phytosanitary firms based in Cameroon, or their representatives, shall be considered a
distributor of phytosanitary products.
(2) The distributor shall verify that the phytosanitary products bought are released or are provisionally
authorized for sale in Cameroon within the validity period.
Article 38 - The distributor of phytosanitary products shall possess a diploma from an Agriculture
training institution, or holder of an attestation of training of three (3) month at least issued by a certified
training institution
Article 39 - (1) The authorization for a qualified distributor of phytosanitary products and bio-pesticides
issued by the Minister in charge of Agriculture and valid for a renewable period of five (5) years, shall
be subject to the production of a file which comprise the following:
    -    stamped application;
    -    an information form;
    -    a copy of certificate or attestation;
    -    an attestation of presentation of original of the certificate or attestation;
    -    a curriculum vitae;
    -    a certified true copy of the past contract with the supplier of the product about to be distributed;
    -    a legalized commitment to respect the legislation and the phytosanitary regulations in force;
    -    a report on the existence of the local places signed by a sworn official of the competent;
    -    phytosanitary protection;
    -    a work contract binding the proprietor to the employees.
              IV - Professional phytosanitary treatment for the benefit of third parties
Article 40 - (1) The registration for professional phytosanitary treatment to the benefit of a third party
shall be issued by the Minster in charge of Agriculture for a renewal period of five (5)
(2) The applicant must be the holder of a certificate from an agricultural training institution or attestation
of three (3) months training in phytosanitary training issued by a renowned institution.
In this last regard, the training shall be authorized by the Minister in charge of Agriculture.
Article 41 - The registration shall cover all or part of the activities here below:
    -    crops phytosanitary protection;
    -    treatment of stored food commodities and agricultural products;
    -    Cleaning up of the premises and products warehouse units, plane luggage compartments and
         other means of transportation;
    -    The treatment of Woods;
    -    the release of chemicals.




                                                                                                  Page | 168
Article 42- (1) The applicant for phytosanitary treatment shall:
   -   possess headquarter, equipment and apparatus for spraying pesticides;
   -   procure appropriate and sufficient material for bodily protection of personnel against exposure
       to products during the different handlings;
    - possess security material which may help in detecting eventual toxic gas leakages;
    - possess a staff with technical and practical competences for the handling of agriculture
       pesticide;
    - commit himself to make use only of released phytosanitary products;
(2) Besides, he shall produce a file comprising:
   -    a stamp application;
   -    an information form;
   -    a copy of certificate or attestation of training of at least three (3) months in an institute
        specialized in agriculture;
   -    an attestation of presentation of the original certificate or attestation of training;
   -    a curriculum vitae;
   -    a commitment to respect the legislation and phytosanitary regulations in forces;
   -    justification documents of the quality of the staff;
   -    a report of evidence of the existence of the premises issued by a territorially competent sworn
        official;
   -    a commitment to subscribe an insurance covering risks of fire and others;
   -    a work contract binding the proprietor to the employees.
                             V- Renewal and treatment of authorisation
Article 43 - In the event of renewal of the registration mentioned in article 39, 40, and 42 above, the
promoter shall present a file comprising:
   -   A certificate true copy of a medical care agreement subscribed to under a medical doctor;
   -   An insurance covering fire accidents of the premises and infrastructure and risks of damage
       that the third party may suffer in the fulfilling or during the exercise of his activities;
    - A legalized plan of the premises;
    - Evidence of the existence of the premises issued by a sworn official territory competent.
Article 44 - Authorizations and accreditation may be subject to suspension or withdrawal by the
Minister in charge of Agriculture:
   -    if the beneficiary fails to comply with the legislation and regulations in force;
   -    where these installations may constitute a source of danger to the health of humans, animals
        and the environment.
       VI - Transfer of manufacturing, formulation, packaging units and the distribution of
                                     phytosanitary products
Article.45 - (1) The transfer of manufacturing, formulation, and packaging units or the distribution from
one place or locality to another or any modification of the installations conditions shall be declared to
the national competent services in charge of phytosanitary control within the national territory, failure
of which the authorization shall be withdrawn.
(2) The transfer or modification of installations in mentioned in paragraph (1) above shall only be carried
out after the authorization of the authority in charge of crop protection.




Page | 169
       CHAPTER VI:         CONTROL        OF    PHYTOSANITARY          PRODUCTS        AND     TREAMENT
       APPARATUS
                I - Control of phytosanitary products and the Treatment apparatus
Article 46 - (1) The control of phytosanitary products and the apparatus shall be an obligation to which
physical and moral persons shall submit themselves in ensuring the responsibility of:
    - import or export of phytosanitary products and phytosanitary treatment apparatus;
    - production, formulation and packaging units of phytosanitary products;
    - warehouses for the storage or distribution of phytosanitary products;
    - means of transportation and postal parcels.
(2) The control of phytosanitary products shall be carried out all round the national territory, especially
at the entry and exit points.
3) In the event of presumption of fraud during controls, the sworn officials may take some samples and
have them analysed at the expense of the proprietor.
Article 47 - (1) The objectives of the control of phytosanitary products and the treatment apparatus
shall aim at:
   -    the preventing the importation or distribution of non-released phytosanitary products;
   -    insuring the conformity and quality of phytosanitary products in use throughout the national
        territory;
    - insuring the use of phytosanitary products with minimal risks on human, animal health or on the
        environment.
(2) This gives rise to the issuance of an attestation of the quality of pesticides or the conformity of the
treatment apparatus.
Article 48 - (1) At the import, export or during distribution, the phytosanitary products and treatment
apparatus shall be subject to control or quality analyses.
(2) Phytosanitary products imported, produced, formulated or packaged locally and the treatment
apparatus shall be equally subject to analyses and verification for quality at the expense of the
promoter before their distribution and use.
(3) ln the case of the import of prohibited phytosanitary products or uncertified treatment apparatus,
sworn officials in charge of inspection shall proceed to repatriate them at the expense of the proprietors.
                                     II - Mission of sworn officials
Article 49 - (1) Post-release control of phytosanitary products, inspection of treatment apparatus in
stores, warehouses, or the distribution, means of transportation, production units and packaging shall
be carried out by sworn officials from the Ministry in charge of Agriculture.
(2) They shall take an oath before the Court of First Instance territorially under the conditions of ordinary
laws at the behest of the Authority in charge of phytosanitary products.
3) They shall be compelled to wear uniforms and to present their professional cards and mission orders
when and where required.
(4) The quality of inspector or phytosanitary controller shall be lost once the agent no longer works in
a structure in charge of phytosanitary regulations.
Article 50 - (1) To facilitate the execution of their mission, the phytosanitary inspectors and controllers
have access to bills lading and to different international forwarding documents. They shall have to
oppose the distribution of phytosanitary products and treatment materials that are none conforming.




                                                                                                Page | 170
Article 51 - (1) The sworn officials from the Ministry in charge of Agriculture shall participate. In the
pursuit and prosecution of offences committed at importation, manufacturing, packaging, the
distribution and use of phytosanitary products and treatment apparatus
2) In this respect, they shall proceed to establish the facts, seize the phytosanitary products sold in
bulk or retailed or non-release in circulation, as well as those treatment apparatus introduced of used
in violation of the provisions of the law.
(3) A report of their operations shall be issued by the sworn officials and countersigned by the offender.
The report shall be considered as evidence of the facts found therein until proven to be false. it shall
be transmitted to the territorially competent State prosecutor.
Article 52 - The sworn officials in charge of controlling phytosanitary products and treatment Apparatus
control may, in the execution of their functions and following the procedure provided by law and
regulations in force
   -      request the intervention of judicial police officers for the search and seizure of phytosanitary
       products sold or circulating fraudulently or to obtain the identity of the offender:
   -      visit premises, trains, ships, vehicles or suspected plane luggage compartments susceptible to
          transporting or keeping the said products;
   -      affix seals;
   -      order the repatriation of phytosanitary products on the offender.
                                          III - Control of residues
Article 53 - (1) The control of the residues of phytosanitary products in agriculture products is aimed
at safeguarding the consumers' health.
(2) The taking of products samples for the analysis of the residues shall be carried out after harvest in
the markets, super markets, processing units, stores and sale by skilful personnel from the Ministry in
charge of Agriculture.
 (3) The analyses results shall be communicated to the Minister in charge of Agriculture, who shall
publish them and instructs envisaged actions in the area of information and sensitization with reference
to the FAO/WHO norms in force.
Article 54 - The norms in matters of maximal limits of residues and the results of the analyses shall be
communicated to the Minister in charge of Agriculture who shall publish them and prescribe envisaged
actions in the area of information and sensitization.
         CHAPTER VII: TRANSITIONAL AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 55 - The release Orders and various registrations established prior to the date of the signature
of this decree shall remain in force until their expiry date.
Article 56 - Importers of treatment apparatus and persons intervening in the phytosanitary crop sector
have a time limit of twelve (12) months from the date of signature of this decree to comply with the
regulations and law in force.
Article 57 - All other contrary previous provisions to this decree, especially those of decree No 902-
233-P M of 25 may 1992 to fix the implementation modalities of Law No 90-o13 of 10 August 1990 on
crop protection, are hereby repealed.
Article 58 - This decree shall be registered and published following the urgent procedure then, inserted
in the Official Gazette in French and English./-
                                                                                   Yaounde, 06 April 2005
                                                              The Prime Minister, Head of Government
                                                                                           INONI Ephraim

4.4. ORDERS




              Page | 172
   4.4.1.     Joint Order No. 00022/MINADER/MINFI of 4 March 2019 to lay down the costs of
       inspection and certification of phytosanitary products as well as the management
       procedures of the income from these operations


THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT, THE MINISTER OF FINANCE,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of the Law No. 003/2003 of April 15, 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Law No. 2018/012 of 11 July 2018 relating to the fiscal regime of the State and other public
entities;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2013/066 of 28 February 2013 to organise the Ministry of Finance;
Mindful of Decree No. 2018/190 of March 02, 2018 to supplement certain provisions of Decree No.
2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2018/191 of 2 March 2018 to reshuffle the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/002 of 4 January 2019 to to reshuffle the Government;
Decree No. 2005/07771/PM of 6 April 2005 establishing the modalities for the execution of plant
quarantine operations;
In view of Decree No. 2005/05/0772/PM of 6 April 2005 establishing the conditions for the approval
and control of phytosanitary products;
Mindful of Order No. 029/CAB/PM of 04 April 2014 to set modalities for inspections and controls of
merchandises by State technical services at the Douala Sea port;
                                  HEREBY ORDER AS FOLLOWS:
       CHAPTER I: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: This joint order lays down the costs of inspection and certification of phytosanitary products
as well as the management procedures of the income from these operations.
This pursuant to Article 6 Paragraph 2 of the law n°2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary
protection.
       (2) Fees for phytosanitary inspection and certification operations shall be charged for
compliance with phytosanitary standards, plant quarantine, phytosanitary surveillance and pest control.
        (3) The costs referred to in paragraph 1 above shall be borne by the users involved in plant
protection activities and shall specifically relate to:
    - the cost of import and export inspection of shipments of plants, plant products
         and other regulated items and accompanied baggages;
    - the field certification fees;
    - the costs related to pest risk analysis;
    - the cost of appliance and equipment conformity testing;
    - the import permit fees;
    - the costs of licensing:
    - the costs of plant protection treatments supervision and other activities;
    - the costs for inspection of the means of transport and storage environment;
    - the costs of issuing prior authorisations for the phytosanitary sub-sector;
    - the costs for laboratory analysis of samples;
    - the costs for technical audits of structures carrying out phytosanitary activities;
    - the travel costs of the competent phytosanitary authority.


Page | 173
Article 2: The costs referred to in Article 1 paragraph 2 above are defined in Appendices I II, II, IV, V
and VI which is an integral part of this Order.
Article 3: The provisions of this decree shall apply to all operations listed in Article 1 Paragraph 2
above, carried out by the competent phytosanitary authority at the level of the Department in charge
of phytosanitary regulations, as well as at all phytosanitary control points within the national territory,
that is, maritime, airport and land borders.
        CHAPTER II: RECOVERING AND SECURING PHYTOSANITARY COSTS
Article 4: The costs of phytosanitary inspection, quality control and certification operations shall be
paid into a treasury deposit account at the Specialised Paymaster’s Office of the Ministry in charge of
Agriculture and Rural Development entitled "Phytosanitary Certification".
Article 5: (1) The account shall be managed by:
    -   an authorising officer who is the Minister in charge of Agriculture;
    -   an accounting officer appointed by the Minister in charge of Finance.
The authorising officer shall implement the budget of this account. Therefore, he/she shall commit,
liquidate and authorise expenditures in accordance with the provisions of this Order.
          (2) He/she may delegate the management of the account to the Director in charge of
phytosanitary regulations in accordance with the provisions in force.
Article 6: The authorising officer shall produce administrative accounting for each financial year
showing all revenue and expenditure operations to be transmitted to the Ministry in charge of Finance
and to any State body competent for the clearance of accounts.
Article 7: (1) The accounting officer, assisted, if necessary, by the appointed administrators, shall be
responsible for collecting revenue and paying for expenditure, in accordance with the regulations in
force.
For this purpose, he/she shall be solely responsible for the quality and accuracy of the entries in order
to ensure:
    -  the management and safekeeping of such funds;
    -  all withdrawals on the basis of documents duly signed by the authorising officer.
        (2) He/she shall be personally, financially and legally responsible for the financial and
accounting operations. He/she shall provide a guarantee in accordance with the texts in force.
        (3) He/she shall produce a management account for each financial year, showing all revenue
and expenditure transactions.
        CHAPTER III: MANAGEMENT OF INCOME GENERATED BY INSPECTION, CONTROL
        AND CERTIFICATION OPERATIONS
Article 8: (1) The bodies in charge of inspection, control and certification operations shall submit a
statement of requirements to the Authorising Officer, as well as a statement of expenditure covering
the last six months and copies of the relevant supporting documents, by the 15th of the month following
the end of the six-month period. An Imprest Fund shall be opened for this purpose at the Department
in charge of Phytosanitary Regulations to carry out the said inspection, control and certification
operations, in line with the regulations in force.
           (2) The Director in charge of Phytosanitary Regulations shall ensure the production and
consolidation of statements of expenditure for the running of the bodies in charge of inspection, control
and certification operations, to be submitted for clearance.
Article 9: (1) The phytosanitary inspectors, phytosanitary controllers and associated personnel,
particularly the staff of regional services in charge of phytosanitary regulations, agricultural posts,
phytosanitary bases and brigades involved in phytosanitary certification and surveillance, shall receive
an allowance referred to as “share”.


                                                                                              Page | 174
           (2) On the proposal of the Director in charge of phytosanitary regulations, the share shall
be paid quarterly to all the staff designated above in Paragraph 1 under the following conditions:
    -   Phytosanitary inspectors: 5 shares;
    -   Phytosanitary controllers: 3 shares;
    -   Associate staff: 2 shares.
The method of calculating the value of a share is as follows:
Total number of shares = (Number of inspectors x 5) + (Number of controllers x 3) + (Number of
associated staff x 2)

                             Total amount of allowances to be allocated
Value of a share = _______________________________________________
                                  Total number of shares
             (3) For disciplinary purposes, the share of an agent may be reduced or withdrawn by
decision of the Minister in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development on the proposal of the Director
in charge of Phytosanitary Regulations.
Article 10: The revenue of the account shall be distributed as follows
    -   share referred to in Article 9 above = 20%;
    -   support for the development of phytosanitary surveillance systems, phytosanitary warning
        systems and the promotion of integrated pest management and plant health = 5%;
    -   support for the development of the phytosanitary certification system (equipment, training,
        laboratories, logistics, forms, functioning of the National Phytosanitary Council, etc.) = 25%;
    -   support for the running and supervision of bodies in charge of inspection, control and
        phytosanitary certification operations = 25%;
    -   Public Treasury = 25%.
     CHAPTER IV: FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 11: The Director General of the Treasury and the Director in charge of Phytosanitary
Regulations shall be responsible, each in their capacities, for the implementation of this decree, which
shall be communicated wherever necessary.

                                                Yaoundé, 4 March 2019
                                                Signed by

 The Minister of Agriculture                                                 The Minister of Finance

   4.4.2.      Order No. 00085/19/A/MINADER/CAB of 1 July 2019 to set the period of validity
       for sprayers certification orders

THE MINISTRY OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT,
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018 to supplement certain provisions of Decree No.
2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2019/001 of 4 January 2019 to appoint a Prime Minister, Head of Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0772/PM of 6 April 2005, on the registration and protection of
phytosanitary products;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0770/PM of 6 April 2005 on modalities for phytosanitary control
operations,
                                  HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: (1) This Order sets the period of validity for sprayers certification orders.
(2) All certified sprayers in Cameroon shall be governed by the provisions referred to in paragraph
(1) above.
Article 2: The certification of sprayers, referred to in this article, shall be for five (5) years, renewable
by tacit agreement.
Article 3: The National Commission of Pesticide Registration and Certification of Sprayers
(CNHPCAT) and the Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of
Agricultural Inputs and Products (DRCQ) shall be responsible for the implementation of this decree,
which shall be registered and communicated wherever necessary./ -


                                                                                    Yaoundé, 1 July 2019
                                                The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                      Gabriel MBAIROBE




                                                                                               Page | 182
   4.4.3.     Order No. 0699/A/MINADER/SG/CNHPCAT of 23 July 2013 prohibiting the use
       of carbofuran-based phytosanitary products

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0772/PM of 6 April 2005, on the control and registration of phytosanitary
products;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018,
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/409 of 9 December 2011 to appoint a Prime Minister, Head of
Government;
Mindful of      Decree No. 2011/410 of 9 December 2011 to form the Government;
Mindful of Order No. 001/05/A/MINADER/SG/DRCQIPA/SDRPEAT of 23 January 2006 to establish
the composition of the National Commission of Pesticide Registration and Certification of Sprayers.
Considering the recommendations of the National Commission of Pesticide Registration and
Certification of Sprayers which met in its Session of 22 April 2013,

                                  HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: Carbofuran-based phytosanitary products shall be prohibited from import, export,
manufacture, packaging, storage, distribution and use in Cameroon, from the date of signature of
this Order.
Article 2: The phytosanitary products referred to in Article 1 above shall include the following:
BASTION 10 G, FURAPLANT 1- 0 G, SESAME 5 G, SESAME 10 G.
Article 3: (1) Any natural person or legal entity having stocks of the phytosanitary products referred
to in Article 2 above shall report to the Regional Delegations of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture
and Rural Development (Regional services in charge of quality control of agricultural inputs and
products, regional phytosanitary bases) within three (3) months.
            (2) The controlled use of the declared stocks shall take place within twelve (12) months
following the entry into force of this ban.
Article 4: The Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural
Inputs and Products (DRCQ) and the Department in charge of Agricultural Development (DDA) shall
be responsible for implementing this Order, which shall be registered and communicated wherever
necessary.

                                                                              Yaoundé, 23 July 2013
                                              The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                             Lazare ESSIMI MENYE




Page | 183
   4.4.4.     Order No. 00829/A/MINADER/SG/CNHPCAT of 30 July 2013 prohibiting the use
       of dimethoate-based phytosanitary products

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0772/PM of 6 April 2005, on the control and registration of phytosanitary
products;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/409 of 9 December 2011 to appoint a Prime Minister, Head of
Government;
Mindful of       Decree No. 2011/410 of 9 December 2011 to form the Government;
Mindful of Order No. 001/05/A/MINADER/SG/DRCQIPA/SDRPEAT of 23 January 2006 to establish
the composition of the National Commission of Pesticide Registration and Certification of Sprayers.
Considering the recommendations of the National Commission of Pesticide Registration and
Certification of Sprayers which met in its Session of 14 February 2011,
                                  HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: Dimethoate-based phytosanitary products shall be prohibited from import, export,
manufacture, packaging, storage, distribution and use in Cameroon, from the date of signature of
this Order.
Article 2: The phytosanitary products referred to in Article 1 above shall include the following:
     - CALLIDIM 200 EC,
     - CALLIDIM 400 EC,
     - CYPERDIM 220 EC,
     - CYPLANDIM 260 EC,
     - DIMEX 400 EC,
     - DIMEZYL 400 EC,
     - METE.OR 400 EC
     - PLANTHOATE 400 EC.
Article 3: (1) Any natural person or legal entity having stocks of the phytosanitary products referred
to in Article 2 above shall report to the Regional Delegations of the Ministry in charge of Agriculture
and Rural Development (Regional services of quality control of agricultural inputs and products,
regional phytosanitary bases) within three (3) months.
            (2) The controlled use of the declared stocks shall take place within twelve (12) months
following the entry into force of this ban.

Article 4: The Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural
Inputs and Products (DRCQ) and the Department in charge of Agricultural Development (DDA) shall
be responsible for implementing this Order, which shall be registered and communicated wherever
necessary.

                                                                              Yaoundé, 30 July 2013
                                              The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                             Lazare ESSIMI MENYE

                                                                                        Page | 186
   4.5.2.     Decision No. 01465/D/MINADER/CAB of 4 October 2017 on the organisation of
       phytosanitary controls at regional level

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0772/PM of 6 April 2005, on the control and registration of phytosanitary
products;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government, as amended and
supplemented by Decree No. 2018/190 of 2 March 2018;
Mindful of Decree No. 2015/434 of 2 October 2015 to reshuffle the Government;
Considering service needs,
                                      HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: This decision organises phytosanitary controls at the level of various regions throughout
the national territory.
Article 2: (1) Phytosanitary controls at the level of the Regions shall be initiated either by the
territorially competent phytosanitary administration or by the natural person or legal entity carrying
out any phytosanitary activity in the Region.
(2) They shall be organised and carried out by the territorially competent Phytosanitary Service,
under the supervision of the Regional Delegates of MINADER.
(3) Phytosanitary controls shall be carried out either at the entry and exit points from the national
territory, or where phytosanitary activities are carried out within the national territory.
(4) Phytosanitary controls shall be permanent activities of the phytosanitary administration, which,
in order to achieve the objectives of plant protection, may schedule, additional specific control
missions in the Region.
Article 3: The phytosanitary controls mentioned in article 2 paragraph 3 above shall be carried out,
at the borders, by Phytosanitary Posts and, within the national territory, by Regional Services of
quality control of agricultural inputs and products, with the assistance of the support services, at the
initiative of the territorially competent DRADER Service or of any other phytosanitary structure
created within the national territory.
Article 4: Within the national territory, the areas of competence of MINADER staff and all other
designated structures shall be defined in line with the territorial deployment of the Customs
administration, with a view to facilitating some joint controls required for foreign trade.
Article 5: Border phytosanitary controls
(1) Border controls shall be carried out mainly for phytosanitary activities having a direct impact on
foreign trade as a result of import or export.
(2) Controls at the entry and exit points of the territory shall be carried out for phytosanitary
surveillance and certification purposes, primarily at borders. They may also be carried out outside
the border at the request of users.
(3) The control requested outside the border shall be carried out by the Phytosanitary Post that is
responsible for the quality of the cargo in the customs clearance process. If the phytosanitary post
has difficulties in carrying out its mission effectively, they shall refer the matter to the Regional
Delegate territorially competent who shall set up, under his supervision, a relay mechanism.
(4) The relay work requested by the Phytosanitary Post shall aim at complying with the certification
requirements.
(5) Relay activities shall result in the production of intermediate deliverables as defined in the Phytosanitary
Inspection Manual. These deliverables shall be attached to the application for certification at the border.
Article 6: Phytosanitary controls outside the borders

Page | 187
Phytosanitary controls outside the borders shall be essentially done for surveillance in phytosanitary
activities within the national territory. These controls can also be carried out to support the
certification missions of phytosanitary posts at borders.
Phytosanitary controls outside the borders may be carried out permanently to support certification
or in specific missions organised by territorially competent MINADER staff.
Article 7: Permanent controls.
Permanent controls shall be carried out on phytosanitary activities at the request of border posts or
users to meet specific certification requirements. The staff responsible may be from the territorial
control points, the regional quality control services, the phytosanitary brigades and bases, or the
Sub-Divisional delegations.
Article 8: Control missions
They shall be initiated, organised and supervised by the MINADER Regional Delegates.
They shall be carried out by a team of MINADER staff as follows:
Regional mission team                       Qualifications/Functions               Observation
Regional Supervision                  Regional Delegate                      Not sworn
                                      2 sworn officials in charge of quality
Rapporteur                            control of agricultural inputs and Sworn
                                      products
                                      Competent DDADR
Members                               competent DAADR                        Management staff
                                      Competent CBP
Article 9: (1) At the end of the phytosanitary control missions, a mission report, to which are attached
reports on infringements, shall be prepared and transmitted to the phytosanitary protection authority,
through hierarchy, as soon as possible.
             (2) The latter shall decide on the need to refer the matter to the territorially competent
courts where the offence was committed.
Article 10: (1) Control activities shall aim at ensuring compliance with conventional, legislative and
regulatory provisions on plant health.
             (2) Infringements shall be recorded untill proven otherwise.
Article 11: All previous provisions contrary to the provisions of this Decision are hereby repealed.
Article 12: This Decision, which enters into force from its signature, shall be registered and published
wherever necessary.
                                                                              Yaoundé, 4 October 2017
                                              The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                Henri EYEBE AYISSI




                                                                                           Page | 188
   4.5.3.    Framework Agreement between the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
       Development (MINADER) and CROPLIFE Cameroon (CLC)

This agreement establishes a collaboration framework for the joint implementation of the conditions
for agricultural development compliant with Phytosanitary Ethics.

                              FRAMEWORK AGREEMENT BETWEEN

The Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, hereinafter referred to as: "MINADER",
represented by Mr. Gabriel MBAIROBE, in his capacity as Minister of Agriculture and Rural
Development, on the one hand,
                                                     And
CROPLIFE CAMEROUN, hereinafter referred to as "CLC", PO Box: 3224 Douala, TELEPHONE:
233 39 23 74 / 699 24 52 38, E-mail: infos@croplife- cmr.org / hfosso@fimex-international.com,
represented by Mr. HenrI FOSSO, in his capacity as President of CROPLIFE Cameroon, on the other
hand.
Collectively referred to as "the Parties" and individually as "the Party".

PREAMBLE
   - Considering that Cameroon ratified the FAO International Plant Protection Convention on 5
     April 2006;
   - Considering Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection, and
     subsequent implementing decrees;
   - Considering the important contribution of CROPLIFE Cameroon to the training of farmers
     on the rational use of pesticides over the last ten years;
   - Considering the common will to develop a phytosanitary sector that respects ethics and
     guarantees healthy and abundant food production;
   - Considering the consensus on the need for a formal framework of collaboration to pursue
     and capitalise on the informal relationship maintained for more than ten years between
     both parties as part of the joint training of stakeholders in the phytosanitary sector,
The parties agree on the following:

        CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Article 1: Objective of the Agreement
1.1 The purpose of this framework agreement is to define and specify the terms of collaboration
between MINADER and CLC as part of implementing the conditions for agricultural development
compliant with phytosanitary ethics.
1.2. The implementation of an activity selected by the parties shall be subject to specific negotiations.
Article 2: Definition of terms
For the Implementation of this agreement, the following definitions shall apply:
Pesticide: any substance or combination of substances intended to repel, destroy or control pests,
disease vectors and undesirable species of plants or animals causing damage or otherwise causing
harm during the production, processing, storage or marketing of food, agricultural products, wood
and non-woodforest products;
Phytosanitary product: pesticides and other substances intended for use as plant growth
regulators, defoliants, desiccants, fruit thinners, or to prevent premature fruit drop, as well as
substances applied to crops either before or after harvest to protect the produce from deterioration
during storage and transport.
Good phytosanitary practice: term regrouping control methods against organisms harmful to crops
with a view to preserving the good health conditions of plants and plant products, human and animal
health, and the environment against the risks arising from the use of plant protection products and
other phytosanitary measures.




Page | 189
Article 3: Scope of Application
3.1 The activities envisaged under this Agreement shall include:
    -   Updating the legislation regulating the manufacture, import, distribution, handling and use of
        pesticides and biotechnology;
    -   Combating counterfeiting and illicit trade in Phytosanitary products;
    -   Reorganising the phytosanitary sector;
    -   Finding appropriate and efficient solutions to the phytosanitary challenges faced in
        Cameroon;
    -   Training in and promoting good agricultural and phytosanitary practices throughout the
        country;
    -   Discussing issues of national interest for "Agro-industries".
    -   Issues on the health and phytosanitary quality of food produced in Cameroon and in the Sub-
        Region.
In order to allow a better control of the range of phytosanitary protection tools, as well as solutions
proposed to agricultural professionals, MINADER and the CLC shall:
    -   Improve the profile of molecules and formulations in agricultural production;
    -   Improve packaging, protective equipment and treatment application systems and develop
        new solutions in the use of phytosanitary products;
    -   Impart good phytosanitary practices in all agricultural sectors;
    -   Develop a surveillance system for the proper management of obsolete stocks;
    -   Contribute to scientific innovation and the development of systems capable of delaying soil
        erosion, limiting various types of pollution (soil, air, water, etc.) and strengthening
        biodiversity.

    CHAPTER 2: OBLIGATIONS OF THE PARTIES
Article 4: Joint commitment
            (1) MlNADER and CLC shall collaborate to:
    -   Seek funding for the implementation of the selected projects and/or activities;
    -   Share technical expertise or any other contribution deemed relevant to the selected
        projects and/or activities.
           (2) MlNADER and CLC declare that this framework agreement has been established in
               good faith and, will consequently, ensure the successful implementation thereof.

         CHAPTER 3: FINANCIAL PROVISIONS
Article 5: Sources of funding
The activities and projects carried out within this framework agreement shall be subject to a financial evaluation
submitted to the appraisal of the parties;
The technical, material and financial resources shall be provided by either the signatories of this agreement or
a third party/donor.
In any case, the expenditure process must comply with the standards of the donor.
        CHAPTER 4: ASSESSMENT, DURATION AND AMENDMENT OF THE AGREEMENT
Article 6: Steering and Monitoring/Evaluation Committee
6.1 As part of the implementation of this framework agreement, a Steering and Monitoring/Evaluation
Committee shall be set up, comprising DRCQ and DDA on behalf of MINADER and two (2) representatives of
CROPLIFE. The Committee shall be responsible for guiding, monitoring and evaluating the implementation of
the obligations of the Agreement.
To this end, the Committee shall:
    -   Ensure that the commitments made by the parties to this framework agreement are respected;
    -   Coordinate and take actions to resolve identified problems;

                                                                                                    Page | 190
    -   Prepare the global and annual action plans and the related budgets;
    -   Carry out regular monitoring missions in the field to ensure proper implementation of the activities
        provided for in the action plan;
    -    Draw up annual reports and submit them to the various hierarchies.
The Committee shall meet twice (2) a year in ordinary session. However, it may meet as needed at the request
of one of the parties.
The services of members are free of charge. However, members may receive session allowances and other
benefits in accordance with the regulations in force.
Article 7: Duration and Amendment of the Agreement
This agreement shall be established for a period of five (05) years, renewable after a conclusive evaluation.
It may be modified by amendment clause at the request of either party.

        CHAPTER 5: COMMUNICATION
Article 8: Communication between the parties
All communication relating to the implementation of this Framework Agreement shall be in writing,
with explicit reference to the Agreement, and sent to the following addresses:
For MINADER:
Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, Department in charge of Agricultural Development
(DDA), or Department in charge of Regulations, Quality Control and Certification of Agricultural
Inputs and Products (DRCQ) or the General Inspectorate of Agricultural Development (IGDA).
For Croplife Cameroon:
Croplife Cameroun, PO Box: 3224 Douala
Phone: 233 39 23 74 / 697896109 / 699245238
FAX: 233 39 23 75 / 233 39 23 74
E-mail: infos@croplife-cmr.org

        CHAPTER 6: MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS
Article 9: Settlement of disputes
Any dispute arising from the implementation of this Agreement shall be settled amicably within the
Committee.
Otherwise, the dispute shall be referred to the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural
Development for arbitration.
Article 10: Entry into force This Agreement shall enter into force from the date of signature by the
parties.
Article 11: This Agreement is established and signed by the parties in two (2) copies in French./.

                                                                                             For MINADER
 For CROPLIFE CAMEROUN
 Henri FOSSO                                                                          Gabriel MBAIROBE




Page | 191
   4.5.4.     Decision No. 01326/16/D/MINADER/SG/Q/SDRP/SRP of 25 November 2016
       prohibiting the import and registration of metalaxyl-based pesticide formulations in
       Cameroon

THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
Mindful of the Constitution;
Mindful of Law No. 2003/003 of 21 April 2003 on phytosanitary protection;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0772/PM of 6 April 2005, on the control and registration of phytosanitary
products;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0770/PM of 6 April 2005 on modalities for phytosanitary control;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/0771/PM of 6 April 2005, to lay down the conditions for plant quarantine
operations;
Mindful of Decree No. 2005/118 of 15 April 2005 to organise the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural
Development;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/408 of 9 December 2011 to organise the Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2011/409 of 9 December 2011 to appoint a Prime Minister, Head of
Government;
Mindful of Decree No. 2015/434 of 02 October 2015 to reshuffle the Government;
Considering the need to contribute to the quality of Cameroon cocoa,
                                 HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:
Article 1: The import and registration of METALAXYL-based pesticide formulations into Cameroon
shall be prohibited from the date of signature of this decision.
Article 2: All import and distribution facilities shall be required to declare within a period of three (3)
months from the date of signature of this Decision their stocks of pesticides containing this molecule
to the Ministry in charge of Agriculture and Rural Development.
Article 3: A transitional period of eighteen (18) months shall be granted for the marketing of stocks
of the pesticides declared in Article 2, starting from the date of signature of this decision.
Article 4: The National Commission of Pesticide Registration and Certification of Sprayers
(CNHPCAT) shall be responsible for the implementation of this decision, which shall be registered
and communicated wherever necessary.
                                                                           Yaoundé, 25 November 2016
                                               The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                   Henri EYEBE AYISSI




                                                                                              Page | 192
   4.6. CIRCULARS




Page | 193
   4.6.1.    Circular letter No. 0915/LC/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/LNAD of 12 May 2015
       addressed to Phytosanitary Posts in Cameroon



Subject: Sampling of pesticides for post-registration analysis.
It has been brought to my attention that the sampling of imported pesticides for compliance analysis
at the laboratory are either not regularly carried out or carried out by laboratories not approved by
MINADER; or sent very late at the National Laboratory for Diagnostic Analysis of MINADER. In a bit
to end this practice, I have the honour to inform you that henceforth, the collection of pesticide
samples will be systematically carried out at the time of import. In accordance with the relevant
regulations in force, only the MINADER laboratory and laboratories approved by the latter are
authorised to analyse these samples. Furthermore, the Department in charge of Regulations and
Quality Control has set up a mechanism to facilitate the transportation of such samples through
specialised services, to be communicated to you.
I attach great importance to the strict implementation of the provisions of this circular letter.
                                                                                 Yaoundé, 12 May 2015
                                               The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                Lazare ESSIMI MENYE




                                                                                             Page | 194
   4.6.2.     Circular No. 3096/MINADER/SG/ORCQ/SDRP of 13 June 2017 on the use of
       Methyl Bromide for the treatment of export goods


               THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
                                                TO
                            ALL HEADS OF PHYTOSANITARY POSTS.
Some trade union leaders in the timber sub-sector have applied for authorisation to use methyl
bromide to treat goods to be exported, particularly to China and India.
As such, I wish to remind you that in 1985 the State of Cameroon ratified the Vienna Convention for
the protection of the ozone layer and its Montreal Protocol on substances that deplete the ozone
layer.
Our country has since then been actively involved in the implementation of related activities. Thus,
based on an established timetable to eliminate the said substances, developing countries have
eliminated the use of Methyl Bromide in quarantine and pre-shipment operations since 2015.
Henceforth, Cameroon has made its position known as a non-consumer of Methyl Bromide, a
position adopted by all African countries and other developing countries including India and China.
Therefore, any use of this product is illegal and prohibited.


                                                                           Yaoundé, 13 June 2017

                                              The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development

                                                                             Henri EYEBE AYISSI




Page | 195
   4.6.3.     Circular letter No. 00005/LC/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRP/SRP of 5 July 2019 to
      amend the provisions of circular letter No. 0001/19/LC/ MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRP/SRP
      of 15 January 2016 on the validity period of a phytosanitary product in Cameroon

                THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
                                                  TO
            ALL ECONOMIC OPERATORS IN THE PHYTOSANITARY SUB-SECTOR


From the date of signature of this circular letter, the period of validity of all phytosanitary products
formulated in Cameroon since July 2017 shall be three (3) years.
Holders of stocks of such products formulated before the specified period are invited, on the one
hand, to declare their stocks to the services in charge of phytosanitary regulations within three (3)
months following the date of signature of this circular letter and, on the other hand, to take all
necessary measures to carry out a compliance test of the declared stock, after which appropriate
measures shall be prescribed.
I attach great importance to the strict implementation of the provisions of this Circular. /-
                                                                                  Yaoundé, 5 July 2019
                                               The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                  Gabriel MBAIROBE




                                                                                            Page | 196
   4.6.4.     Circular letter No. 0000720/LC/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SRP of 30 December 2020
       prohibiting the import and registration of pesticide formulations based on chlopyrifos,
       chlorpyrifos-ethyl and chlorpyrifos-methyl in Cameroon



                THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
                                                  TO
                   ALL OPERATORS IN THE PHYTOSANITARY SUB-SECTOR
The European Union (EU), Cameroon's strategic trading partner, has tightened the regulations on
maximum residue limits of chlorpyrifos in agricultural products, including Arabica and Robusta
coffee. These measures will therefore affect exports of these products to their markets.
In order to limit any possible prejudice of these measures to the Made in Cameroon label, I have the
honour inform you that from the date of signature of this circular letter, the import and registration of
pesticide formulations based on Chlorpyrifos, Chlorpyrifos ethyl and Chlorpyrifos methyl shall
prohibited in Cameroon.
I attach great importance to the strict implementation of the provisions of this Circular. /
                                                                         Yaoundé, 30 December 2020
                                               The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                  Gabriel MBAIROBE




Page | 197
   4.6.5.     Circular letter No. 04434/16/L/MINADER/SG/DRCQ/SDRP/SRP of 25 November
       2016 prohibiting the acquisition and distribution of Metalaxyl-based pesticide
       formulations within the framework of public contracts in Cameroon



                THE MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND RURAL DEVELOPMENT
                                                    TO
                                             -    DIRECTORS
    -   COORDINATORS OF PROJECTS AND PROGRAMMES IN THE COCOA SUB-SECTOR


SUBJECT: Prohibition of the acquisition and distribution of metalaxyl-based pesticide formulations
within the framework of public contracts in Cameroon.


          I have the honour to inform you that due to problems posed by the presence of Metalaxyl
residues, above the maximum tolerable limits, in pesticide formulations the acquisition and
distribution of pesticides based on this molecule shall be prohibited within the framework of public
procurements for the treatment of cocoa orchards in Cameroon.
I attach great importance to the strict implementation of the provisions of this Circular. /-


                                                                         Yaoundé, 25 November 2016

                                                 The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development
                                                                                   Henri Eyebe Ayissi




                                                                                            Page | 198
